[[sep-vplan-area-2]]
==== 2. Fabric and remap

[[sep_address_map_test]]
===== 2.1 `sep_address_map_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_address_map_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Prove the CPU-LSU decodes `sep_cpu_ctrl` and one safe CSR in every LSU-reachable CSR block (including Adams Bridge and the entropy pool), and that the unpopulated hole inside `sep_cpu_ctrl` reads OKAY with zero and is not a live alias.

*Test Coverage*: One safe CSR in every LSU-reachable CSR block, plus a `sep_cpu_ctrl` sweep and the interior hole.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode with the LSU splice owning the bus.
. Read every `sep_cpu_ctrl` reset-check register and require the exported reset value (CHK-CPU-CTRL-RESET).
. Response-check hardware-driven registers, and log the value of `SEP_TEST_CTRL` with its strap bits `fast_spi_en`, `fast_iccm_en`, `fast_dccm_en`, `fast_sram_en`, `fast_pka_en` and `sep_standalone` (bits 31 to 26; `OBS-SEP-TEST-CTRL value=0x<..> fast_spi_en=<b> fast_iccm_en=<b> fast_dccm_en=<b> fast_sram_en=<b> fast_pka_en=<b> sep_standalone=<b>`; no check reads it); read the low half of `REFERENCE_COUNTER` twice and require it to advance (the reference clock is driven, so the counter is live and no reset value applies).
. Write/readback/restore the SEP base/size CSRs and the pure-RW storage registers (masked), and read each register back after its restore.
. Write the implemented `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` bits, read them back, then restore reset after the fabric walk.
. Walk one readable CSR per LSU-reachable block (DMA, WDT, scratch, reset, OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, CSRNG, EDN, ESRC, ABR NAME, entropy-pool STATUS, lifecycle, KM mailbox, eFuse shadow, AXI mailbox, inbound filter CFG, alias remap, outbound remap, SPI) with the PR-OUT tap running and require OKAY (value-check where reset is known), and no AR handshake on the outbound port during the walk. Before the walk, outbound filter entries 0 and 1 are programmed as a pair over the 4 KiB page at the reset `SMU_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` (`allow_ns` 0 and 1, read and write allowed, `src_id` 0). After the walk, with the tap still running, read the word at offset `0x100` of that page and require OKAY and exactly one AR handshake on the outbound port, at that address: the route demux sends the SMU aperture to the outbound port (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, "Fabric Topology", output fabric), so the read shows that the tap records an outbound read in the window (`CTRL-FABRIC-WALK-LOCAL seen: rows=<n> pr_out_ar=0 [] live_addr=0x<a> live_ar=1 [ar[addr=0x<a> ...]]`; a walk AR handshake, or a liveness read with no AR handshake, more than one, or one at another address, logs `CTRL-MISSING FABRIC-WALK-LOCAL` and fails the leaf). Both entries return to their RDL reset values after the sequence.
. Read three words in the hole after `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` and before `SEP_SW_DEBUG` (including `0x10A3_0170`). Require OKAY with zero data, which `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` states as the contract for an offset inside a unit's extent that owns no register. Prove `SEP_SW_DEBUG` writable, then require a hole write to leave it unchanged.
. Read the word one past the eFuse-shim window with the PR-EXT tap running and require DECERR, with one AR handshake at that address and one DECERR R beat on the extension port (CTRL-EXT-DEMUX-PORT); then, with the tap still running, read the first word of the window and require no AR handshake on the extension port (`CTRL-EXT-DEMUX-SHIM seen: addr=0x<a> ext_ar=0 []`; any AR handshake logs `CTRL-MISSING EXT-DEMUX-SHIM` and fails the leaf) and a response other than DECERR (CHK-EXT-DEMUX-BOUND).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CPU-CTRL-RESET
|Every `sep_cpu_ctrl` register of the reset-check list reads its reset value from `hw/sys/sep/regs/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl/sep_cpu_ctrl.rdl` (the generated `reset32`). The test compares each readback with that value itself, and the shared scoreboard compares it too. A DUT with a wrong reset value fails, and so does a register with a non-zero reset value that does not decode. A register whose RDL reset is 0 proves its reset value only, not its decode: an offset between registers answers OKAY with read data 0 (`doc/trm/src/memory_map.adoc`), so a missing decode there reads like the reset value. The non-zero resets and the CHK-RW-READBACK legs prove that the block decodes. Evidence: `CHK-CPU-CTRL-RESET PASS: regs=<n> each equals its RDL reset value`; on a mismatch `CHK-CPU-CTRL-RESET FAIL` names each register with its readback and reset value.
|CHK-REFCNT-READ
|The reference counter is LIVE and readable: the low half is read twice and must advance. It counts on `clk_ref_i`, which the testbench drives, so no pinned value can be its expectation -- and the advance is a stronger statement than a reset compare, because a neighbouring register's storage, a stuck all-ones and a zero return all hold still between the two reads. The high half is read for the pair but is not value-checked.
|CHK-RW-READBACK
|Every pure read/write register returns exactly what was written, masked to its implemented bits. Each is then written back to its RDL reset value and read back; the readback must equal that reset value under the same mask.
|CHK-BASEADDR-RW
|The base and size registers accept a write, read back, and restore cleanly.
|CHK-FABRIC-WALK
|Every LSU-reachable CSR block in the walk returns OKAY on its chosen register; value-checked entries match the exported or seq-derived expectation. The walk has 22 rows, one per block listed in step 6 with cold and warm scratch as two rows, and the check requires each of them to be read, in table order, with an OKAY response; a shorter walk or a non-OKAY row fails (`CHK-FABRIC-WALK PASS: rows=22 of 22, each LSU-reachable block CSR read and answered OKAY`; otherwise `CHK-FABRIC-WALK FAIL: rows=<n> planned=<n> expected=22 not_okay=[...] missing=[...]`). The shared scoreboard also fails any non-OKAY row, including a row read without an expected value, through the `CHK-ADDRMAP FAIL` gate. `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` routes an address the local fabric does not decode to the SMN, where the bench outbound responder also answers OKAY with zero, so the walk runs with the PR-OUT observation tap and CTRL-FABRIC-WALK-LOCAL requires no AR handshake on the outbound port: each walked read is answered inside SEP. The liveness read of the SMU-aperture word in the same tap window must show its one AR handshake, so the absence cannot come from a tap that records nothing.
|CHK-EXT-DEMUX-BOUND
|The first word of the eFuse-shim window answers something other than DECERR, so the demux selected the shim port, and the word past the window answers DECERR. The PR-EXT observation tap proves that the word past the window leaves SEP on the extension port and that the DECERR is the port's reply (`CTRL-EXT-DEMUX-PORT seen: addr=0x<a> ar=[ar[addr=0x<a> id=0x<i> ...]] r=[r[resp=0x3 id=0x<i> last=0x1 ...]]`; anything other than exactly one AR handshake at that address and exactly one R beat with DECERR, the same ID and RLAST set logs `CTRL-MISSING EXT-DEMUX-PORT` and fails the leaf). The same tap proves that the read of the first word does not leave on the extension port: `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` ("External Window Reference Model") routes the window to the eFuse controller rather than out the external port (`CTRL-EXT-DEMUX-SHIM seen: addr=0x<a> ext_ar=0 []`; any AR handshake logs `CTRL-MISSING EXT-DEMUX-SHIM` and fails the leaf). The CHK grades the response class at the initiator. Both addresses come from the generated header by symbol. The value behind the shim is adopter-owned and is not graded — only which port the decode selected.
|CHK-CPU-CTRL-HOLE
|Each `sep_cpu_ctrl` word in the hole after `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` and before `SEP_SW_DEBUG` reads OKAY with zero data, and a write to `0x10A3_0170` does not change `SEP_SW_DEBUG` -- whose writability a control write proves first, since `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` is `sw = r` and no AXI write can move it. `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` states both halves: a unit accepts such an offset with zero/OKAY, and non-aliasing follows from exact-match register decode rather than from refusal. The check grades the response and the data; it does not prove the hole accesses stay on the local fabric, since the bench outbound responder also answers OKAY with zero.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Evidence*: the test logs `CHK-ADDRMAP FAIL` and stops before the CHK-CPU-CTRL-RESET, CHK-REFCNT-READ, CHK-BASEADDR-RW, CHK-RW-READBACK and CHK-FABRIC-WALK records if the shared scoreboard holds any response or value error. CHK-CPU-CTRL-HOLE and CHK-EXT-DEMUX-BOUND are logged after further accesses; a scoreboard error from those accesses fails the run in `check_phase`, after their PASS lines.

'''''

[[sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test]]
===== 2.2 `sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW, `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (console + verdict mailbox; OKAY / zero read data for any address). PR-CPU-IFU (`pr_cpu_ifu_*`, the raw IFU read request) and PR-IFU (`pr_ifu_*`, the IFU request after its local alias remap, with `pr_ifu_arlen_o`) are observation taps on the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list.

*Objective*: Proves the CPU-side address alias window remaps both data and instruction accesses to physical SRAM, that every IFU read carries the AxPROT that `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` states (table `sep-cpu-axprot-table`), and that every IFU read is a single beat (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc:38-39`: the CPU masters and the DMA issue single-beat transactions only).

*Test Coverage*: Alias window remaps LSU and IFU accesses to physical SRAM.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Probe the alias base CSR with a value distinct from its reset value, restore the operating base, and read back. The region size is a fixed localparam and is not programmed.
. Write through the alias; read the physical address.
. Write the physical address; read through the alias.
. Write a small function into SRAM, fence, and call it through its alias address.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CSR
|The alias base register is writable and reads back. It is probed with a value distinct from its reset value first — writing only the operating base would be unfalsifiable, since that is also the field's reset value and a read-only register would pass. `CHK-BASE-LIVE` below then proves the programmed base is actually consumed. The operating base is `0xd0000000` over a fixed 768 MiB window onto physical `0x10000000`; the probe value is `0xe0000000`.
|CHK-BASE-LIVE
|A write through the alias window with a NON-reset base programmed lands at the physical address that base selects. A remapper hardwired at the operating base (`0xD000_0000`) would map `0xE000_0318` to `0x2000_0318`, not `0x1000_0318`, and fails here. This is what makes CHK-CSR's probe more than a register readback.
|CHK-LSU-WR
|A data write through the alias lands at the physical address.
|CHK-LSU-RD
|A physical write is readable through the alias.
|CHK-IFU
|A call through the alias is fetched, remapped, executed, and returns its value.
|CHK-IFU-AXPROT
|Every IFU AR handshake on PR-CPU-IFU over the run carries ARPROT `0b101` (privileged, secure, instruction; `cpu.adoc`, `sep-cpu-axprot-table`), and the run has at least one. An X or Z on the tap fails. A core that drives the upstream VeeR `0b100`, or any other value, fails. Evidence: `CHK-IFU-AXPROT PASS: master=ifu ch=ar prot={0b101} count=<n> expect=0b101`.
|CHK-IFU-SINGLE-BEAT
|Every IFU AR handshake on PR-IFU from the CPU release to the end of the run has AxLEN 0, one beat (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc:38-39`: the CPU masters issue single-beat transactions only; AXI AxLEN is the beat count minus 1), and the run has at least one; the firmware fetches through the alias, so the IFU reads reach PR-IFU. An X or Z AxLEN fails. Catches an IFU that issues a line-fill burst. Anchor: PR-IFU. Evidence: `CHK-IFU-SINGLE-BEAT PASS: master=ifu tap=PR-IFU ch=ar count=<n> len={0} mismatches=0 expect=0`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Functional coverage*: owner window 764 opens when the CPU is released, after the cold reset, the TCM load and the fuse sense, and closes at the end of the run. `cp_master_ch[ifu_ar_101]` and `cp_cpu_single_beat[ifu_ar]` are held at each IFU handshake in the window and sample when the leaf writes `ifu_remap_pass` after CHK-IFU-AXPROT and CHK-IFU-SINGLE-BEAT passed (written 0 when the window opens); a run whose check fails leaves both at 0, and a reset drops them. An IFU AxPROT other than 0b101 samples an illegal bin of `cp_master_ch` at once.

*Anti-vacuity*: the instruction-fetch checker cannot pass vacuously — the fetch
demultiplexer runs on the post-remap address, so an alias address that was not
remapped routes to the error slave and never executes.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test]]
===== 2.3 `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), *real PROD fuse sense* — no `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RAND-REP — entries 0 and 7 × two windows × `{rw, r, w}` at match-all, plus entry 0 / window 0 source-ID match and mismatch, run every seed. Seed varies the window data, the entry-15 granule window, and the bound-probe registers. The ARID of the denied burst is drawn from 1 to 62 (63 is the pre-sense read) on a separate seeded stream, so the other draws keep their values.

*Objective*: Brings the lifecycle controller up in PROD so the inbound filter is genuinely active (`sep_debug=0`, value-checked against the lifecycle golden), then walks entries 0 and 7, two address windows, and `{rw, read-only, write-only}` at `src_id=0` (match-all), plus entry 0 / window 0 x `{rw, r, w}` at `src_id=0x5` with a matching AXI `user[3:0]` and one src-mismatch cell — 16 cells in one invocation — then the same-page `allow_burst=1` granule, page-bound, and `FILTER_CONFIG.locked` on entry 15.

*Test Coverage*: Sixteen inbound-filter cells plus burst granule, page-bound, and lock. Functional coverage: `sep_fabric_inbound_aperture_cg.cp_lite_split` and `sep_filter_range_granule_cg.cp_locked_write`, owner window 757. A window holds one graded burst or one refused `FILTER_CONFIG` write with its read-back; programming, staging, read-backs and the single-beat probes run with the window closed, and a FAIL closes the window first. `cp_lite_split` samples on the `lite_split_pass` label, which the leaf writes after CHK-BURST-TO-SINGLE and the response check of that burst passed.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Release reset and wait for fuse sensing to end. This brings the lifecycle controller up in PROD so the inbound filter is active.
. Walk entries 0 and 7, two windows, `{rw, r, w}`, and the source-ID match / mismatch cells — 16 cells.
. Walk `allow_burst` deny then allow on AR and AW of the scratch window (2-beat INCR, AxLEN=1). The allow window spans two 4 KB pages. The allowed burst becomes two Lite singles; the denied burst reaches none.
. Program an 8-byte `allow_burst=1` window inside one 4 KB page on entry 15. Prove the page grant on the bus, both page edges, then freeze `allow_burst` with `FILTER_CONFIG.locked`. The lock is sticky, so this step is the last filter step.
. Pulse `rst_ni`, send one external read of SRAM word 0 with ARID 63 while fuse sensing runs, wait for sensing to end and watch that read for 256 cycles after the SEP reset releases (OBS-SI-PRESENSE-READ). No other read uses ARID 63, so a read that gets no response leaves no later read to pair with. This step runs after every graded step: the coverage sampler pairs each System Interface read completion with the one read outstanding, so a read that gets no response must not overlap the graded reads.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ALLOW-RULE
|An allowed external read returns OKAY *and* the exact staged value. A denied read of the same address returns DECERR *and* read data that is not the staged value, so the blocked access did not reach the target. `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` (Blocked Transactions) names DECERR and no read data, so no exact deny payload is graded.
|CHK-BLOCK-DEFAULT
|A non-allowed external read in the *same CSR page* returns DECERR — so region reachability is proven by the allow case and cannot explain the deny.
|CHK-READ-ALLOWED
|The read-allowed bit gates the matched read in both directions.
|CHK-WRITE-ALLOWED
|The write-allowed bit gates the matched write in both directions, and the allowed write lands (confirmed by a CPU-side readback).
|CHK-OWNERSHIP
|The CPU-LSU reads inbound CFG, outbound CFG[0], alias/AP/STEE remap bases, `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR`, and `SEP_REGION_SIZE`. The external master completes DECERR on read and write of each address, and the denied write does not land. Those CSRs stay outside every allow window (firmware must not allow-list them). `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` and `SEP_REGION_SIZE` share the window-0 (`SEP_SW_DEBUG`) 4 KB page, so they also prove the live window is START/END, not the page.
|CTRL-NONVAC
|Allow and block were both observed under one lifecycle state. A summary control; it grades no DUT value of its own.
|CHK-SRC-ID
|A non-zero `FILTER_CONFIG.src_id` matches only the external master's `user[3:0]`. The match cells keep r/w-allow gating; the mismatch cell DECERRs with both allow bits set. Control: before the mismatch read, `CTRL-SRC-ID-CFG` reads `FILTER_CONFIG` of the entry back equal to the programmed word and reads the window with `user[3:0]` equal to `src_id`, which returns the staged value, so a disabled or mis-programmed entry fails. Evidence: `CTRL-SRC-ID-CFG PASS: entry=0 cfg_rw=0x<hex> expect=0x<hex> src_id=0x5 addr=0x<hex> user=0x5 resp=<code> rdata=0x<v> expect_rdata=0x<v>`.
|CHK-CELL
|One line per cell of the 16-cell walk. With every other entry disabled and only the cell's entry programmed: an allowed read returns OKAY and the staged value; a read with `read_allowed=0` returns DECERR and read data that is not the staged value; an allowed write returns OKAY and lands on a CPU-LSU readback; a write with `write_allowed=0` returns DECERR; on the source-ID mismatch cell the read and the write both return DECERR. A read of the blocked address in the same page returns DECERR in every cell.
|CTRL-RAND-REP
|Entries 0 and 7, two address windows, `{rw, r, w}` at match-all, plus entry 0 / window 0 source-ID match and mismatch — 16 cells in one invocation. Entry 15 carries the granule, bound, and lock cells.
|CHK-BURST-DENY
|With `allow_burst=0`, a single-beat external read of the scratch window is OKAY (anti-vacuous) and a 2-beat INCR (AxLEN=1) to the same address is DECERR. `traffic_filter.sv` `pass_burst` is not vacuously true. The R beats of the denied burst on the `m_axi` pins are graded too: two beats, each with RRESP DECERR and RID equal to the ARID, RLAST on the second beat only (AMBA AXI IHI 0022; `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` "Blocked Transactions" for DECERR). The ARID is drawn from 1 to 62 (63 is the pre-sense read), so an RID that drops to 0 fails.
|CHK-BURST-ALLOW
|With `allow_burst=1` and a programmed window that already spans two 4 KB pages, the same 2-beat INCR is OKAY and returns the staged scratch value. START/END read back as programmed (the same-page 4 KB granule widen does not fire). The granule widen itself is `CHK-PAGE-WIDEN`.
|CHK-BURST-WRITE-DENY
|The write-side `traffic_filter` (`aw.len`) with `allow_burst=0`: a single-beat external write is OKAY and lands; a 2-beat INCR write is DECERR and does not land.
|CHK-BURST-WRITE-ALLOW
|With `allow_burst=1` and the same two-page window, a 2-beat INCR write is OKAY and the poke lands on a CPU-LSU readback. START/END stay the programmed range.
|CTRL-BURST-WALK
|Summary of the `allow_burst` walk: `CHK-BURST-DENY`, `CHK-BURST-ALLOW`, `CHK-BURST-WRITE-DENY`, `CHK-BURST-WRITE-ALLOW` and the `CHK-BURST-TO-SINGLE` read and write legs all passed, so the deny and the allow side were walked on AR and on AW of the scratch window. It grades nothing of its own.
|CHK-BURST-TO-SINGLE
|A filter-allowed 2-beat INCR (AxLEN=1) becomes two AXI-Lite singles on the system-CSR bridge (`u_system_csr_a2l_1`): AR/AW at start and start+4. A denied AxLEN=1 produces zero Lite handshakes (filter before the converter). `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`: convert burst to single. Each leg is logged as `CHK-BURST-TO-SINGLE PASS: leg=<deny|allow>_<AR|AW> ...`. Control CTRL-BURST-LITE-LIVE: before each denied burst, the single-beat access of the deny cell reaches the Lite port as exactly one single at its address, so the zero-handshake compare of the denied burst runs on a live port.
|CHK-WIDEN-GUARD
|Stimulus guard in `program_rule` (`seq_lib/sep_inbound_filter_rule_seq.py`). A rule programmed with `allow_burst=1` and START and END inside one 4 KB page must be requested as a page widen, or the leaf fails before the entry is written. This keeps a cell that wants a narrow window from getting the whole-page grant by accident. The DUT cannot fail this checker, and it has no PASS line.
|CHK-PAGE-WIDEN
|A same-page `allow_burst=1` window grants the whole 4 KB page (`hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc`: `START_ADDR` rounds down, `END_ADDR` rounds up, `allow_burst` selects 4 KB). Measured on the bus rather than in a register mirror: an external access to an address inside the granted page but outside the programmed `START..END` answers OKAY for read and write and carries the staged value. The hardware-adjusted `START_ADDR`/`END_ADDR` readback is the setup step showing the rewrite took effect; a CSR mirror is not evidence of what the filter passes.
|CHK-PAGE-BOUND
|The 4 KB grant stops at both page edges. Under a scratch-page grant an access one page below is refused; with that page granted instead it answers OKAY while the scratch address turns DECERR. Both probes are proven reachable first, so neither refusal can be a decode hole rather than a filter decision. This is the security contract: 4 KB is the block pitch for the DMA CSR, watchdog and scratch-bank blocks, so a page-crossing grant would reach a neighbouring block.
|CHK-CONFIG-LOCK
|`FILTER_CONFIG.locked` freezes `allow_burst`. The lock bit is write-once, and every later write to the entry's `FILTER_CONFIG` completes DECERR (`hw/ip/axi_filter/regs/filter_ctrl.rdl` `locked`; `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` "Locking a Filter Entry"). With `allow_burst` set, a write of 1 to `locked` (the high word of `FILTER_CONFIG`) reads back set. Then a low-word write that clears `allow_burst` must complete DECERR, and the low word reads back unchanged. A high-word write of 0 must complete DECERR, and `locked` reads back set. Last, an external 2-beat INCR read (AxLEN=1) inside the widened page but outside the programmed `START..END` answers OKAY, returns the staged value, and reaches the system-CSR bridge as two AXI-Lite singles. A single beat would not show this: `START`/`END` already hold the widened page, and a single beat inside that range passes with `allow_burst` 0 or 1. Only the burst depends on the bit, so the frozen bit still drives the filter and not only the CSR readback. The two write response codes are logged when they return and graded after the 2-beat read, so the read is checked whatever code the writes answered. Each refused write is graded against a read of the same register made just before it: the low word reads back unchanged after a write that clears `allow_burst`, and the high word keeps `locked` 1 after a write of 0 (`hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` "Locking a Filter Entry": reads still return the locked configuration). The response of both writes is graded DECERR.
|OBS-CONFIG-LOCK-HI-RSVD
|Logged only, no pass or fail. `FILTER_CONFIG` high-word bits [62:32] before and after the refused write of the high word. `filter_ctrl.rdl` names no field there.
|OBS-SI-PRESENSE-READ
|Logged only, no pass or fail. One external read of SRAM word 0 with ARID 63, sent after the release of the `rst_ni` pulse that ends the leaf and before fuse sensing ends: whether its AR handshake happened (`accepted`), `sep_reset_n` at that handshake, whether an R beat with ARID 63 came within 256 cycles after the SEP reset released (`response`, `rresp`). No specification line names the reset of the inbound port or what an inbound request gets before `sep_reset_n` releases.
|OBS-FILT-DENY-RDATA
|Logged only, no pass or fail. The address, the AxUSER, the response and the read data of every denied external read (`addr`, `user`, `resp`, `rdata`). The specification names no read data for a denial and no way to tell a denial from a reserved-address DECERR.
|CTRL-FILT-RSVD-ALLOW
|Premise of CHK-FILT-RSVD-READ. Entry 0 alone is enabled, read-allowed, with a window from the last SRAM granule (`SEP_SRAM_MEM_BASE_ADDR` + `SEP_SRAM_MEM_SIZE` - 8) to the Reserved base 0x10050000. The LSU stages a seeded word in that SRAM granule, and the external read of it returns the staged word, so the entry admits reads in its window (the response code is logged on the line). Evidence: `CTRL-FILT-RSVD-ALLOW PASS: entry=0 window=0x1003fff8..0x10050000 addr=0x1003fff8 resp=<code> rdata=0x<staged> expect=0x<staged>`.
|CHK-FILT-RSVD-READ
|Through the same entry, the external read of the Reserved row base 0x10050000 answers DECERR with 0xBADCAB1E (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc:41`, Reserved row 0x10050000-0x107FFFFF, "DECERR, 0xBADCAB1E / DECERR"; the column is the answer at the inbound AXI port, `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc:66-69`; addresses below 0x4000_0000 go on to the local crossbar, `:102-104`). A DUT that answers any other code or data fails. A filter denial gives the same code and data, so CTRL-FILT-RSVD-ALLOW shows the entry admits reads in its window, and `CTRL-FILT-RSVD-PASSED` requires this read to show exactly one AR in the 0x10050000 granule at the local crossbar's `ext` initiator (`xbar_ext_in_*`, past the inbound filter); a read the filter refuses shows none and fails (`CTRL-FILT-RSVD-PASSED PASS: addr=0x10050000 ar_past_filter=1 ars=[0x10050000] expect=1`). Evidence: `CHK-FILT-RSVD-READ PASS: row=reserved addr=0x10050000 resp=3 rdata=0xbadcab1e expect=3/0xbadcab1e`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: the walk is inbound-filter entries 0 and 7, two address
windows (`SEP_SW_DEBUG` and scratch-cold[0]), `{rw, read-only, write-only}` at
`src_id=0`, plus the source-ID product on entry 0 / window 0, then the
`allow_burst` deny/allow pair on AR and AW of scratch and the burst-to-single
Lite split, then the same-page 4 KB granule, page-bound, and `FILTER_CONFIG.locked`
on entry 15. `CHK-OWNERSHIP` keeps inbound CFG, outbound CFG, the remap banks, and `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` / `SEP_REGION_SIZE` outside every allow window. WRAP, FIXED and
`AxLEN>1` are not walked: the Lite bridges convert those bursts to singles, so the
filter only ever sees `tx_len==0`.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test]]
===== 2.4 `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: R/W, 64-bit, RO, valid, and write-once-set across the alias/AP/STEE remap and inbound/outbound filter CSR banks, and that every entry of the alias remap bank and of the inbound and outbound filter banks holds its own value. The AP and STEE output-remap banks are checked on one seeded region each per seed. Each filter entry and remap region is an independent rule with its own storage, so a decode that aliased two entries, or a bank that implemented fewer entries than the map declares, would be invisible to a sweep that walks one entry per group.

*Test Coverage*: Remap and filter CSR banks: R/W, 64-bit, RO, valid, write-once-set, and per-entry storage independence across all 16 alias regions, 16 inbound and 32 outbound filter entries.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Write each bank and require the readback to differ from the observed pre-write. Each written word is read first; when the seeded pattern equals that value under the word's R/W field mask, every R/W bit of the pattern is inverted, so a pattern equal to the reset value cannot hide a register that ignores the write.
. Prove RO `data_bus_width` ignores writes on both filters.
. Write a distinct pattern into every entry of each bank, then read all of them back, and require each to hold its own pattern.
. Set and try to clear the alias `valid` bit; it clears.
. Lock one inbound and one outbound filter entry, try to clear `locked`, then write the `FILTER_CONFIG` lo word, `START_ADDR` and `END_ADDR` of the locked entry; require DECERR on each write and no change to each word.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|A written register's readback differs from its *observed* pre-write value, and the neighbouring field is undisturbed. The pre-write sample is required: asserting only that the written pattern is non-zero is a property of the stimulus constructor, not of storage.
|CHK-ALIAS-RW
|Region start, end and attribute registers are read/write, including the 64-bit upper word: `REGION_START` hi, `REGION_END` lo and `REGION_ATTRS` lo each read back the written value, and that value differs from the word's *observed* pre-write value.
|CHK-AP-STEE-RW
|The output-remap CSRs are read/write across both banks: `REGION_ATTRS` lo and hi of the seeded AP and STEE region each read back the written value, and that value differs from the word's *observed* pre-write value.
|CHK-INFILT-CFG / CHK-OUTFILT-CFG
|Filter-configuration read/write fields (`rd`, `wr`, `ns`, `burst`, `src_id`, `group_id`) read back exactly on both the inbound and the outbound bank, and differ from the word's *observed* pre-write value.
|CHK-BANK-INDEP
|Every R/W word of every bank entry holds its own distinct pattern: 224 words across 16 alias regions (`REGION_START` lo+hi, `REGION_END`, `REGION_ATTRS` lo+hi) and 48 filter entries (`FILTER_CONFIG`, `START_ADDR`, `END_ADDR`) are each written a value carrying their bank number, entry index and word number, then all are read back and required to match through the same mask the write used. The bank term matters because the two filter banks are instances of one RDL array type and share stride and word list: without it, inbound entry *k* and outbound entry *k* would carry the same pattern and a decode that aliased one onto the other would still read back what it wrote. A bank that decodes two entries to one storage, or that implements fewer entries than the address map declares, fails here and the message names the entry and word; the walked word count is asserted against 224 so a short walk cannot pass on whatever it reached, and every entry count comes from the register export rather than a sequence literal. The per-group field sweep above picks one random entry per seed, so this is the only leg that shows the other entries exist and are distinct. Deterministic: the same words are covered on every seed, so the three `reseed` runs repeat this leg rather than sampling different slices. `FILTER_CONFIG`'s hi word is not walked -- its only field above bit 31 is `locked[63]`, which `CHK-WOSET` owns; the read-only `data_bus_width` is masked out here and graded by `CHK-RO`.
|CHK-RO
|The data-bus-width field reads its fixed value and ignores writes of the inverse pattern, on both filters. On each filter the field reads 3 (`filter_ctrl.adoc`, `data_bus_width` R 0x3) before and after a write of the inverted field (4) with the other fields unchanged. The write is made twice: first with the coverage window closed, then, after that instance passed, inside owner window 762 (`sep_filter_range_granule_cg.cp_dbw_ro`).
|CHK-VALID-RW
|The region valid bit is read/write in both directions.
|CHK-WOSET
|The write-once-set `FILTER_CONFIG.locked` bit holds, and while it is set every write to that entry's `FILTER_CONFIG`, `START_ADDR` and `END_ADDR` completes DECERR, on the inbound and the outbound bank. The clear write to the `FILTER_CONFIG` hi word must complete DECERR with `locked` still 1. A write that flips every R/W field of the `FILTER_CONFIG` lo word (`read_allowed`, `write_allowed`, `entry_enabled`, `allow_ns`, `src_id`, `group_id`, `allow_burst`), and a write that flips 4 KB-aligned bits of `START_ADDR` and `END_ADDR`, must each leave the word unchanged. Each probe logs the written value and the word before and after. Source: `hw/ip/axi_filter/regs/filter_ctrl.rdl` (`locked`) and `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` ("Locking a Filter Entry"). Every code is logged before it is graded.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: the field sweep (`CHK-NONVAC`, `CHK-ALIAS-RW`, `CHK-AP-STEE-RW`,
`CHK-INFILT-CFG`, `CHK-OUTFILT-CFG`, `CHK-RO`, `CHK-VALID-RW`, `CHK-WOSET`)
covers one seeded region or entry per bank per seed. `CHK-BANK-INDEP` covers
every alias region and every inbound and outbound filter entry, but not the AP
and STEE output-remap banks.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_slot_csr_strobe_rand_test]]
===== 2.5 `sep_fabric_slot_csr_strobe_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_slot_csr_strobe_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip; register model from the generated IP-XACT (`sep_reg_meta.register_fields`)

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: slot and register order, write order within each register's plan, access offsets and sizes of the reads, write data, the fill on inactive byte lanes, the BREADY/RREADY hold lengths and the W-ahead-of-AW gap. `reseed = 3` samples different data, fill and orderings; the plan shape (which accesses each register gets) is the same on every seed.

*Objective*: The byte-strobe, access-size, back-pressure and write-once-set rules hold at every slot of the five System-block fabric banks, not at one. Each of the 16 alias-remap regions, 16 AP and 16 STEE output-remap regions, 32 outbound and 16 inbound filter entries is its own register block behind its own AXI-Lite port, so a rule that one slot obeys says nothing about the others.

*Test Coverage*: All 224 registers of the 96 slots: reset, every byte lane written alone and in 16/32/64-bit groups, inactive lanes driven with data, read-only and reserved bits, the filter `START_ADDR`/`END_ADDR` granule write-back, `FILTER_CONFIG.locked` on all 48 filter entries, and a write and a read of every slot outstanding together on `s_axi`. Functional coverage: `sep_filter_match_cg.cp_locked_range_write` (`start_decerr_kept`, `end_decerr_kept`), both filled on every seed by one forced refused `START_ADDR` and one `END_ADDR` write per entry. Graded window and pass label: the window of the owner is open for one refused `START_ADDR` or `END_ADDR` write at a time, from one `clk_i` edge before the write to the release after its checks, and only while no mismatch is recorded; the lock write, the entry reads and the other writes run with it closed. The cell is held at the read-back and samples only on the rise of `slot_lock_pass`, which the leaf writes after the DECERR response, the read-back of the written lanes and the whole read of every register of the entry passed; the window closes at the first mismatch, which drops the held cell.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read every register as one 64-bit access and as one 32-bit word before any write.
. Per register, in seeded order: one 64-bit write, one write per byte lane, two 16- or 32-bit writes, each with seeded data on the active lanes and seeded fill on the inactive ones, and a seeded-size read after each write; then a 64-bit read.
. Per slot, twice: start a 32-bit write of one register's low word and a 32-bit read of its high word together on `s_axi`, once with BREADY and RREADY held and once with W presented ahead of AW and RREADY held; sample the `s_axi` pins during the pair; then read the register.
. Per filter entry, in seeded order: lock it with a write that strobes `FILTER_CONFIG.locked`; then four seeded writes to its registers, then one write to `START_ADDR` and one to `END_ADDR` (drawn on a salted `SepSeededRng` stream, so the seeded stream does not move), each chosen so that the entry would change if the write were taken, and a write of 0 to the lock bit; read every register of the entry. Each refused `START_ADDR` or `END_ADDR` write is preceded by a 64-bit read of the register (graded against the model) and followed by a read of exactly its written lanes and a whole read of every register of the entry (`FILTER_CONFIG`, `START_ADDR`, `END_ADDR`).
. Read every register of every slot.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SLOT-RESET
|Every register reads the reset value its RDL fields declare (`filter_ctrl.rdl`, `alias_remap.rdl`, `output_remap.rdl`, through the generated IP-XACT), as a 64-bit access and as a 32-bit word. Reserved bits read 0; `data_bus_width` reads its declared value 3.
|CHK-SLOT-STROBE
|Every write answers OKAY and changes exactly the writable field bits in its strobed byte lanes (AMBA AXI write-strobe rule); read-only and reserved bits keep their RDL value. The master drives seeded data on every inactive lane, and always a 1 on `FILTER_CONFIG.locked` and alias `REGION_ATTRS.valid` when their lane is inactive, so a block that ignored WSTRB would take the fill, lock the entry or arm the remap, and read back changed. A filter `START_ADDR`/`END_ADDR` pair that lands in one granule (4 KB with `allow_burst`, 2^`data_bus_width` bytes without) reads back widened to it, as `filter_ctrl.rdl` describes. The write count is asserted against the plan (11 per register). The W beats that carry data on an inactive lane are counted on the TB `s_axi` pins and at the AXI-Lite W port of each slot's register block (`fabric_slot_w_*` probes in `tb/tb_top.sv`, observation-only): the count must be non-zero, the slot-port count of beats and of fill beats must equal the `s_axi` counts, and every one of the 96 slot blocks must see fill, so the deny leg is exercised at the block that applies WSTRB. The log names how many writes moved the register and how many carried fill that differed from it.
|CHK-SLOT-PIPE
|On the `s_axi` pins, each pair has at least one cycle in which both the write (AW accepted) and the read (AR accepted) are unanswered; in the held pair, B and R each wait with VALID high and READY low (the hold lasts 40 to 71 cycles from the request); in the W-first pair, WVALID rises before AWVALID. Both answer OKAY, the read returns the half of the register the write does not strobe (its value does not depend on the order the DUT serves them in), and the write lands. Asserted for 2 pairs per slot. Overlap at the slot's own AXI-Lite port is not claimed: the AXI-Lite demux in front of each slot holds one transaction per direction and forwards W only after its AW, so the slot block does not see the two in flight together or W ahead of AW.
|CHK-SLOT-LOCK
|After a write that strobes `FILTER_CONFIG.locked` with a 1, every write to that entry's `FILTER_CONFIG`, `START_ADDR` and `END_ADDR` leaves the entry unchanged, and a write of 0 does not clear the lock (`filter_ctrl.rdl` `locked`, `onwrite = woset`). Each refused write is checked on the model to be one that would change the entry if it were taken. Every refused write, the write of 0 included, must complete DECERR (`filter_ctrl.rdl` `locked`: the write is steered to the AXI error subordinate); the response-code tally is logged. For every refused `START_ADDR` or `END_ADDR` write, the 64-bit read before it and the read of its written lanes after it equal the model, and so does the whole read of every register of the entry after it (`hw/ip/axi_filter/regs/filter_ctrl.rdl:80-84`, "every subsequent write ... is steered to the AXI error subordinate ... reads still return the locked configuration"; `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc:151-156`). Positive probe: the DECERR response of the same write, which is chosen on the model to move the entry. Live minimum (asserted): every entry has one passing refused `START_ADDR` and one `END_ADDR` write. All 48 filter entries are asserted locked.
|CHK-SLOT-FINAL
|At the end, every register of every slot holds its own model value, so no write to one slot or register moved another.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: CSR layer only. Whether a filter entry or remap region acts on live traffic with the programmed values is graded by the datapath tests in this bucket. Alias-remap `REGION_ATTRS.valid` is never set through an active lane, because a valid region rewrites the CPU-LSU traffic this test runs on.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test]]
===== 2.6 `sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master); `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (outbound responder: OKAY and zero read data for any address)

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the seed selects one bank, one remap region and one filter entry; the checkers and the addresses they compare follow from that choice. Coverage is one region of the bank per run, so the entry runs at a single seed and the entry claims one cell, not a sweep.

*Objective*: Proves that a programmed output-remap window translates a live beat to its remapped target, and that the outbound filter answers DECERR for a beat outside the remapped allow set and for the same beat with its allow entry disabled.

*Test Coverage*: Live outbound / AP remap datapath, not CSR storage alone.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program one region to `0x8000_0000` and allow-list the remapped beat. CPU-LSU read of the window is OKAY.
. Disable the outbound allow entry and repeat the read of the programmed window. It returns DECERR. Enable the entry again.
. A second region, left invalid so its beat passes through untranslated, returns DECERR.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-REMAP-TRANSLATE
|The programmed window completes OKAY at the remapped target. Identity would miss the allow window. The OKAY comes from `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv`, which answers any address, so the check shows that the beat passed the outbound filter, not which address it reached.
|CHK-FILTER-DROP
|A beat outside the remapped allow set is DECERR. One probe is the other region, left invalid, whose beat passes through untranslated. A second probe is the next beat of the allowed region, which translates to a non-zero address outside the one-beat allow entry.
|CHK-FILTER-DISABLE
|With the outbound allow entry disabled, the same remapped read that completed OKAY under CHK-REMAP-TRANSLATE returns DECERR. The target and the translation do not change, so the OKAY comes from the allow entry and not from the target.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_outbound_remap_filter_walk_test]]
===== 2.7 `sep_fabric_outbound_remap_filter_walk_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_outbound_remap_filter_walk_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master); `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (outbound responder: OKAY at any address); `seq_lib/sep_fabric_entry_walk_seq.py` (START/END readback model under the same-granule write-back rule of `filter_ctrl.rdl`)

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the seed selects the in-region beat, the offsets and the windows of every pair. Every region and every entry runs at each seed; one seed per regression, because the bit coverage does not depend on the seed.

*Objective*: Every AP/STEE output-remap region and every outbound-filter entry translate and compare all 56 address bits on live traffic.

*Test Coverage*: All 2 x 16 remap regions (`output_remap.rdl` offset[55:0], valid[63]) and all 32 outbound-filter entries (`filter_ctrl.rdl` start_addr[55:0], end_addr[55:0]). AP region r pairs with entry r and STEE region r with entry 16 + r. Each pair runs offset O (bit 55 clear) and then ~O, and each bound takes a value and its inverse, so every offset, START and END bit takes a 0->1 and a 1->0 step. Functional coverage: `sep_remap_output_offset_cg.cp_valid0_passthru`, owner window 760, one window per pair around the CHK-WALK-PASSTHRU read at the beat's own address; programming, read-backs and the restore run with the window closed, and a wrong response closes the window before the leaf fails.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program region r with offset O1, then enable only entry e with a random window that holds the remapped beat X1. A CPU-LSU read of the region answers OKAY.
. Narrow the window to the one granule at X1: OKAY at X1, DECERR at the next granule of the same region.
. Set START to the inverse of the first window's START (above X1): DECERR.
. Program offset O2 = ~O1, so X2 is at or above 2^55. A window holding X2 answers OKAY; a window ending at the inverse of that END (below X2) answers DECERR; the granule at X2 answers OKAY.
. For every address bit k above the granule, program a window where START bit k alone decides the response for a phase-2 beat of the region, then one where END bit k alone decides it.
. Clear valid: the granule at X2 answers DECERR and the granule at the beat's own local address answers OKAY.
. Restore the entry and the region to reset.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WALK-READBACK
|Every START/END (56 bits), FILTER_CONFIG and remap ATTRS write, bit-leg windows included, reads back as the readback model says (`filter_ctrl.rdl` start_addr: rounded write-back only when START and END share a granule).
|CHK-WALK-ALLOW
|A window that holds the remapped beat answers OKAY. The filter blocks by default and only this entry is enabled, so the OKAY places the 56-bit remapped address inside the window.
|CHK-WALK-EXACT
|The one granule at the predicted address `{offset[55:IDX_START], adjusted[IDX_START-1:0]}` (`output_remap.rdl` offset, region granularity; `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`: the output remap replaces the address with the region offset) answers OKAY and the next granule answers DECERR, so the remap produced exactly the predicted address.
|CHK-WALK-ABOVE
|A window whose START is above the beat answers DECERR.
|CHK-WALK-BELOW
|A window whose END is below the beat answers DECERR.
|CHK-WALK-BIT
|For every address bit k above the granule, and for START and for END, one window whose other bound is 0 or all-ones, so that bound's bit k alone puts a phase-2 beat of the region inside (OKAY) or outside (DECERR). The beat is chosen per bit among beats of the region whose offsets differ in one bit; the selector raises when a (bit, bound) has no such beat. A compare that ignores bit k of either bound flips the answer. Every window is read back.
|CHK-WALK-PASSTHRU
|With valid clear the beat passes through with its address unchanged (`output_remap.rdl` valid: "if clear, they pass through with their address unchanged"): the one granule at the beat's own local address answers OKAY, and the granule at the translated address answers DECERR.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_inbound_filter_window_walk_test]]
===== 2.8 `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_window_walk_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real PROD fuse sense (`sep_debug=0`, value-checked against the lifecycle golden)

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_window_walk_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master and the external SMN master); `seq_lib/sep_fabric_entry_walk_seq.py` (START/END readback model)

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the seed selects the staged value and every window. Every entry runs at each seed; one seed per regression, because the bit coverage does not depend on the seed.

*Objective*: Every inbound-filter entry compares all 56 address bits on live external traffic.

*Test Coverage*: All 16 inbound-filter entries (`filter_ctrl.rdl` start_addr[55:0], end_addr[55:0]). The target is a pure-RW CSR (`SEP_SW_DEBUG`) whose address has zero upper bits, so the upper bound bits [55:32] decide each response.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up PROD with the inbound filter active. Disable every entry and stage a value in the target CSR from the CPU-LSU master.
. For each entry, enable only that entry and program: a window [S, E] holding the target, then [S, ~E] (both END values at or above the target); START S_B and ~S_B (both above the target); a window ending below the target; the one granule at the target.
. Stage the cold scratch words as extra probes, and stage `SEP_NMI_VEC` (`nmi_vec`, the granule after the target) with a seeded value. For every address bit k above the granule, program a window where START bit k alone decides the response for one probe, then one where END bit k alone decides it (for k >= 32 the deciding bound is the one-hot `1 << k`). Then, on the same entry, program the one granule that holds each probe used by a deny leg.
. After each, the external master reads the target (and, for the granule, the next granule, then the next granule through its own one-granule window).
. After the walk, on one drawn entry from the reset bounds: write a range whose START and END lie in one granule and read START and END back, then a range whose START and END lie in two granules and read them back. Each pair runs first with the coverage window closed, with an external read of the target through the widened window, and is restored; then it runs again inside owner window 761 (CHK-WALK-WRITEBACK).
. On the same entry, program START at the granule after the target and END at the end of the target's granule (START above END), read the window back, enable the entry and read the target from the external master (OBS-FILTER-INVERTED).
. Restore the entry to reset.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WALK-READBACK
|Every START/END (56 bits) and FILTER_CONFIG write to the entry under test, bit-leg windows included, reads back as the readback model says. The setup disable of every entry before the walk is not graded.
|CHK-WALK-ALLOW
|A window holding the target answers OKAY with the staged value (the read reached the CSR).
|CHK-WALK-EXACT
|The granule at the target answers OKAY with the staged value; the next granule (`SEP_NMI_VEC`) answers DECERR and does not return its staged value. Control: on the same entry, the one granule that holds the next granule answers OKAY with its staged value, so the DECERR comes from the window and not from an unreachable address.
|CHK-WALK-ABOVE
|A window starting above the target answers DECERR and does not return the staged value (`hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc`, Blocked Transactions).
|CHK-WALK-BELOW
|A window ending below the target answers DECERR and does not return the staged value.
|CHK-WALK-BIT
|For every address bit k above the granule, and for START and for END, one window whose other bound is 0 or all-ones, so that bound's bit k alone puts a staged probe word inside (OKAY with its value) or outside (DECERR without it). The probe is the target or one of the cold scratch words, chosen per bit; the selector raises when a (bit, bound) has no probe. A compare that ignores bit k of either bound flips the answer. Every window is read back. Control: on each entry, every probe that a deny leg uses answers OKAY with its staged value through the one granule that holds it, so each deny leg has an allow cell on the same probe and entry.
|CHK-WALK-WRITEBACK
|A range write whose START and END lie in one 8-byte granule reads back as the widened granule (START rounded down, END rounded up), which differs from the written values; a range write whose START and END lie in two granules reads back as written (`hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc`, Address Range Granule; granule size from the RDL reset of `data_bus_width`, `filter_ctrl.adoc`). In the closed instance an external read of the target answers OKAY with the staged value, so matching widens too. Catches no write-back, a write-back of one bound only, wrong rounding, and a write-back across granules. Functional coverage: `sep_filter_range_granule_cg.cp_writeback`, held at each read-back of the graded instance and sampled only on the pass label `writeback_pass`, which the leaf raises inside the window after the graded check passed over all four 32-bit halves.
|OBS-FILTER-INVERTED
|Logged only, no pass or fail. The START and END read back after a window with START above END is programmed, and the response and read data of the external read of the target under that window (`entry`, `start`, `end`, `addr`, `resp`, `rdata`). The specification names no verdict for an inverted range.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test]]
===== 2.9 `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — one seed per regression (no `reseed`), because every run bashes every register. The seed varies the block order, the complement-vs-ones order and every touch value, never which registers are swept: the cell set is identical on every seed, so coverage is not seed-dependent. A new seed in each regression exposes a register that accepts one mask-legal value and refuses another. The reset walk is the inventory after reasoned skips. Write-lands is the full-mask registers; the inbound START/END pairs use the wrap model.

*Objective*: Proves that every register in the generated SystemRDL export reads its RDL reset value, that a write moves exactly its software-usable bits, that read-only and reserved bits hold, and that every side-effect-free register stores a seeded value. `env/sep_reg_meta.py` exports `addr`, `reset`, `mask` (software-usable field bits) and `mask_all` (storage, reserved included) from the generated SystemRDL.

*Test Coverage*: Seeded CSR bit-bash across the LSU-reachable register map.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build the seeded configuration from `SepRegBitBashCfg`. Set `clk_wdt` to eight core periods and bring up in `no_cpu`.
. Read every register in the reset walk and compare it to its exported reset, with the bits of fields that have no RDL reset masked out (CHK-RESET).
. For every register in the write walk, write the complement of the current value and all ones, in the seeded order of the two, and read back after each write. Compare inbound START/END readback to the same-beat wrap model. Then write the reset value and read it back (CHK-WRITE-LANDS, CHK-RO, CHK-RESERVED).
. For every register in the storage-touch walk, write a seed-derived value (twice for a shadowed register), read it back under the software-usable mask, and restore the reset value (CHK-BLOCK-TOUCH).
. Require the reset, write and storage-touch walks each to cover every register the configuration named, and log the walk sizes (CHK-RANDCFG).
. Log, for each block of the walk, the distinct RUSER and BUSER values that the CPU-LSU port returned on the R and B handshakes of that block (OBS-LSU-RESP-USER).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RESET
|Every reset-eligible register reads its exported reset value on every bit except the bits of fields with no RDL reset, which the compare masks out. Each masked row logs its read, reset and mask, and the PASS line gives the masked-row count.
|CHK-WRITE-LANDS
|A complement write moves exactly the software-usable mask bits: the full-mask registers over the full mask, plus the inbound START/END pairs over ``mask[31:3]`` whose ``[2:0]`` are pinned by the same-beat wrap model instead.
|CHK-RO
|Bits outside the mask stay unchanged across the write bash. Counted only where the register has out-of-mask bits. On a register with an all-ones mask the compare cannot fail, so it is not counted. The PASS line gives the count, and the check fails when the count is 0.
|CHK-RESERVED
|Reserved bits (`mask_all` minus `mask`) read back 0. Counted only where the register has a non-zero reserved field. The PASS line gives the count, and the check fails when the count is 0.
|CHK-BLOCK-TOUCH
|Every side-effect-free export RW storage register of the inventory, not one per IP: write a seed-derived ``x``, assert ``(readback & mask) == (x & mask)``, restore reset. Shadowed names take a dual write. The gate is the per-register safety predicate AND the side-effect predicate; the second is required, because the first admits registers whose write drops live beats. `TOUCH_BLOCKS` is the anti-vacuity list, so the sweep fails if any of those nine IPs stops offering a candidate. Excluded registers each carry a tallied reason and the sweep accounts for the whole inventory. Not storage: hardware-owned status and state-machine observability words, the CSRNG reseed counters, an identity register, two read ports, `ENTROPY_SOURCE.FIPS_LOCK` (a sticky lock is never a storage touch), and two selectors that refuse a random mask-legal value — `HT_WATERMARK_NUM`, whose unsupported encodings sanitize, and `NOISE_OBS_CTRL`, whose mask contains a write-only singlepulse field.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The whole seed-built configuration ran in one invocation: the reset, write and storage-touch walks each covered every register the cfg named. `TOUCH_BLOCKS` is the anti-vacuity list, not the third walk. A completeness control over the sweep itself, not evidence about the DUT -- it fails when a walk silently drops work, not when a register misbehaves. The walk sizes (`export`, `inventory`, `nometa`) are logged with it so a shrunken inventory is visible rather than inferred from a PASS.
|OBS-LSU-RESP-USER
|Logged only, no pass or fail. One line per block of the walk: the distinct RUSER values of its R handshakes and the distinct BUSER values of its B handshakes, as `0x<value>` in ascending order (`X` for an unknown value, `none` when no handshake of that channel shows). The specification states no response USER value for a target.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: This is not a UVM RAL model. `iter_register_walk` counts OFFSET symbols that lack DEFAULT/struct (`nometa`) instead of dropping them silently. `SepRegBitBashCfg` is the single source of truth for both the walk and the skip reasons. ENTROPY_SOURCE registers that hardware advances while the noise source runs are excluded from the reset walk by `sep_reg_meta.reg_hw_updating()`: every register whose fields all declare `sw = r` in the generated JSON model, minus the constants named in `_CONSTANT_RO`. Part of that set is tallied under `hw-owned; read value is not the POR value`; the `STATUS` and `RDATA` name suffixes claim the rest first. `sw = r` alone is not the criterion: COMPONENT_ID is software-read-only but hardwired (`entropy_source.rdl` `hw = na`, no storage in `entropy_source_reg.sv`), so it is opted back in and stays in the walk. The dead-space change compare uses the same accessor, so the two checks cannot drift apart. Write-lands is the anti-vacuity control: a complement write must move exactly the software-usable mask. Two skips are read off the generated IP-XACT. A register the RDL declares `sw = w` returns no storage on a read, and a register that is read-only with no RDL reset value is driven by hardware, so the generated `_REG_DEFAULT` is a field default and not a POR value. Both shapes read back 0 against a DEFAULT of 0 in most cases, so a reset compare on them passes without the DUT having shown anything. `_ipxact_access()` joins the IP-XACT to the inventory by address -- the IP-XACT spells an array as one register with a `dim` where the Python header spells one symbol per element -- and `iter_register_walk` refuses an inventory register with no IP-XACT entry rather than deciding a skip on missing data. The `sep_reg_meta` self-test pins the resulting shapes (the hardware-driven registers by name, the write-only count, and two read-only-with-reset witnesses), so a generator change that alters the `access` or `resets` element name fails there instead of silently restoring the vacuous compare. The same rule applies to each field of a read-write word. A field the RDL gives no reset value has no POR value, and the generated `_REG_DEFAULT` holds a 0 placeholder for it. `sep_reg_meta.word_unreset_mask()` reads those bits off the IP-XACT (no `resets` element on the field). A word whose every storage bit is such a field is skipped, under `no field with an RDL reset; the export DEFAULT is a placeholder`: HMAC `DIGEST_*` and `MSG_LENGTH_*`. A word with only some such bits stays in the walk, and CHK-RESET leaves those bits out of its compare: HMAC `CFG.hmac_en` and `CFG.sha_en`. The `sep_reg_meta` self-test pins the masks of HMAC `CFG` (`0x3`) and `DIGEST_0` (all ones). Read-only WITH a declared reset is a different shape and stays in the reset walk: `ABR.MLDSA_VERIFY_RES_*` is `sw = r` with `resetsignal`, so its reset value is real and only the storage touch refuses it -- software cannot write it, and a touch would compare the readback against a value that never landed. The behaviour behind the access-shaped skips is proven over the frontdoor elsewhere, not dropped: the eight ABR identity words have no RDL reset and no value in any SEP document, so no leaf grades a value for them: `sep_abr_access_size_test`, `sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test` and `sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test` read them against a reference captured from the DUT by `CHK-ABR-ID-REF`, which requires each word to answer OKAY, read the same twice, be nonzero and differ from its granule partner; `ENTROPY_POOL.DATA` by `CHK-POP-DATA` on `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test`. No document states an `ENTROPY_POOL.IRQ_CAUSE` value, so that leaf logs it only (`OBS-POOL-CAUSE-*`). The write-only registers -- keys, data-in windows, command triggers and clear registers -- have no read-side POR to relocate, and their coverage is the functional tests that write them and observe the effect. Inbound-filter START/END stay in the write sweep under the full export mask; readback is checked against the `allow_burst=0` 8-byte granule (`hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc`, `filter_ctrl.rdl`) with the peer at reset (`END_ADDR` reset `0x7`): START rounds down and END rounds up when they share a beat. The 4 KB granule is `CHK-PAGE-WIDEN` on `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`. The inventory is seed-stable. The walk line logs the live tally (export, inventory, nometa, reset, write-lands, storage touch), and the checkers assert against configuration-derived totals. Write-lands is the full-mask registers (scratch-cold, scratch-warm, `SEP_CPU_CTRL`) plus the inbound START/END pairs under the wrap model. Storage touch is every admitted side-effect-free register. `TOUCH_BLOCKS` is the anti-vacuity list (AES/HMAC/KMAC/OTBN/DMA/SPI/WDT and both mailboxes); the sweep fails if any of those nine IPs stops offering a candidate. `WDT_TIMER` threshold registers return the write on `clk_i` and stay busy until `prim_reg_cdc` finishes on `clk_wdt_i`. The eight-core-period `clk_wdt` lets that handshake retire inside the AXI timeout. The compare is the written value.

*Evidence*: each register's write leg logs a failure under CHK-WRITE-LANDS, CHK-RO or CHK-RESERVED, or under `CHK-WRITE` when an inbound START/END readback misses the same-beat wrap model (part of CHK-WRITE-LANDS). The test then stops with one `CHK-WRITE FAIL` summary that counts the failing registers. The PASS records are `CHK-WRITE-LANDS`, `CHK-RO` and `CHK-RESERVED`.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test]]
===== 2.10 `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+sep_ext_trng_axil_responder`, `+sep_trng_errslv_assert_waive`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip. After the reference-slave checks, the TRNG port responder of `tb_top` (`+sep_ext_trng_axil_responder`, set by the testlist entry) answers the TRNG AXI-Lite port `ext_trng_axil_resp_i` with the code, and for a read the word, that the leaf sets for each B and R handshake. The leaf raises `ext_trng_axil_rsp_en_i` once with the port idle, and the responder stays on to the end of the leaf. Probes: PR-TRNG (the request net of the port: address, AxPROT, write data and strobe) and PR-TRNG-RSP (its B and R replies), both observation taps on the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. The responder is a SEP top-port drive on the SEP_TB_ARCH HDL Top list. No inject.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the twelve directed wrap offsets, four directed ABR probes and six directed TRNG-window probes on every seed, plus two seed-selected dead offsets per block window. The TRNG passthrough legs draw, from the seed, the address and data of each access, the order of the single-beat accesses (OKAY, SLVERR and DECERR on a read and on a write, each once), the burst length (2, 4 or 8 beats) and its start word, and the code pair of each split-beat probe of OBS-TRNG-SPLIT-PREC. Legal range: every address lies inside the TRNG window and is aligned to its AxSIZE; a burst stays inside the window.

*Objective*: Negative control on intra-block dead space. Each block owns an address window and populates only part of it; an access past the allocated size owns no register and must be refused. Window ends come from `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc`. Every window except TRNG is graded exactly against the past-extent response `hw/sys/sep/regs/include/sep_memory_map.rdl` sets for its unit (`ocah_past_extent_resp`, no path qualifier): a read answers DECERR with the word `0xBADCAB1E`, and a write answers DECERR. Landing on a live register instead is a hard failure. Bursts are graded in the crypto region only: `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` ("Single-Beat Access Only") answers every beat of a burst there DECERR, and no beat reaches an accelerator. A burst into any other register region is outside the specification (`hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc`, "Single-Beat Register Access") and is not graded. The TRNG window, forwarded whole to an adopter endpoint and terminated in the reference integration, must refuse every access, alias no neighbouring ESRC register, and answer the code `memory_map.adoc` states for a window with no external TRNG: DECERR on a read and on a write.

*Test Coverage*: Dead-space accesses are refused and do not alias live register data or state. With the bench responder on the TRNG port: a single-beat read and write answered OKAY, SLVERR and DECERR, a split 64-bit write, a read burst and a write burst. Functional coverage: `sep_fabric_trng_reply_cg` (`cp_trng_port_reply`). Functional coverage also includes `sep_fabric_trng_reply_cg.cp_trng_forward` and the dead-space cells of `sep_aperture_reserved_access_cg.cp_row_cell` (the past-extent cells of the walked windows and `trng_unowned`; a live-word sample of this owner maps to no bin), owner window 234. The sampler holds each sample of the window and samples it only on the pass label `row_pass`: a cell-pass probe releases its row cells (value 1) after CHK-DEADSPACE-REFUSE or CHK-TRNG-UNOWNED of that probe passed; a repeated TRNG access releases its legal `sep_fabric_trng_reply_cg` sample (value 4) after CTRL-TRNG-RSP-DRIVE and its CHK-TRNG-REPLY, CHK-TRNG-SPLIT-WR or CHK-TRNG-BURST compare passed, and the rise to 4 samples no row cell. The leaf drops the held samples (value 2) before each graded access and on a FAIL. An illegal reply value samples at once.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build the seeded probe set. Require every watched window to supply its two seeded dead-offset probes (CHK-DEADSPACE-RAND). Bring up in `no_cpu`.
. Snapshot each watched window's registers twice. Require every watched register to answer OKAY and at least one register to be armed for the change compare (CHK-WINDOW-LIVE).
. Issue every directed anchor and seeded probe. A refusal that still writes the register is a failure, so every probe reads back the window's live registers as well as the response (CHK-DEADSPACE-REFUSE, CHK-DEADSPACE-NO-ALIAS).
. In the ABR window, probe every seed at the first word past the register map (read and write), at `error_intr_en_r` with offset bit 14 set, and at the last word of the window.
. In the TRNG window, read and write the first, middle and last word every seed, plus two seeded offsets. Use the neighbouring ESRC register set as this window's no-alias snapshot, which an access that drops address bit 12 reaches (CHK-TRNG-UNOWNED).
. In each crypto-region window (OTBN through ABR in the generated memory map: aes, otbn, abr, csrng, edn, entropy_src, km_mailbox, lifecycle and trng), issue a four-beat INCR read burst and, where the window takes writes, a four-beat INCR write burst. Start the burst two words before the extent where a legal burst can cross it, and at the window base otherwise. secure_dma, wdt_timer and spi_controller lie outside the crypto region and get no burst (CHK-DEADSPACE-BURST, CHK-DEADSPACE-BEAT).
. Cell pass. With the TRNG responder off (CTRL-TRNG-RSP-OFF), read the first dead word of every block window, and write it in the SPI controller and TRNG windows. Run the pass once with the coverage window closed, then once more inside owner window 234; each probe is graded on its own (CHK-DEADSPACE-REFUSE, CHK-TRNG-UNOWNED) and a FAIL closes the window first.
. TRNG window passthrough. With the port idle, raise `ext_trng_axil_rsp_en_i` once and require the responder on with both handshake counts at 0 (CTRL-TRNG-RSP-DRIVE). Open the graded window. Before each access, set the code of each port B and R handshake that the access causes. Issue a single-beat (AxSIZE 2) read and a single-beat write for each code in a seeded order (CHK-TRNG-REPLY); one 64-bit (AxSIZE 3, AxLEN 0) write with the first port write answered SLVERR and the last OKAY (CHK-TRNG-SPLIT-WR); a read burst and a write burst with the port set to answer OKAY (CHK-TRNG-BURST). Record for each access the port handshakes (PR-TRNG, PR-TRNG-RSP), the initiator code and, for a read, the initiator word. Then issue one 64-bit write whose two port writes answer a seeded pair of codes that the specification does not resolve, and one 64-bit read whose two port reads answer a seeded pair of different codes (OBS-TRNG-SPLIT-PREC). Close the graded window and stop the taps. Run every passthrough access first with the coverage window closed. Then repeat the forced-offset single accesses, the split write and both bursts inside the graded window, each graded as it completes. The forced single accesses read and write the TRNG window offsets 0x000, 0xFFC and one drawn interior word.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DEADSPACE-RAND
|Stimulus precondition. Each watched window supplies its full quota of two seed-selected, word-aligned dead-offset probes. No two probes, and no probe and directed anchor, share both address and operation. A window with no dead span counts as zero of two. The probe count is the random-coverage claim, so a short window fails the leaf before bring-up and is not absorbed into a smaller probe set. The checker has no PASS line; the `deadspace config` line records the probe count per seed.
|CHK-WINDOW-LIVE
|Each of the twelve watched windows (secure_dma, wdt_timer, aes, otbn, abr, csrng, edn, entropy_src, km_mailbox, lifecycle, spi_controller, and trng against the neighbouring entropy_src registers) has a non-empty register snapshot and at least one register armed for the change compare, so both no-alias compares can fail. Every watched register answers both snapshot reads OKAY; a register that a snapshot read refuses or times out on is left out of the change compare, so the checker fails and names each one. A register that reads two different values is self-changing and is left out of the snapshot. The line reports watched, refused or timed out, self-changing, readable, compared and armed separately. The per-probe change compare drops hardware-updating addresses. Armed counts the compared registers with at least one software read-write field in the generated IP-XACT (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/ipxact/sep.xml`): a `sw = r` field has no bus write path and a write-only field reads back nothing, so only an armed register can show a stored write. Armed is the falsifiable count, and a window with none armed fails.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-REFUSE
|Every probed address past the allocated size is refused (not OKAY), including the directed wrap anchors (KM `0x10920ff8`, DMA `0x108008a8`). In every window except TRNG each probe is graded exactly against the Past Extent response of its unit in `hw/sys/sep/regs/include/sep_memory_map.rdl` (`<unit>->ocah_past_extent_resp`, no path qualifier): secure_dma, wdt_timer, aes, otbn, abr, csrng, edn, entropy_source, sep_lifecycle_ctrl, km_mailbox_sep and the SPI controller all set DECERR, `0xBADCAB1E` / DECERR, so a read must answer DECERR with the word `0xBADCAB1E` and a write must answer DECERR. The values are read from `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/py/sep_memory_map.py` (generated from `sep.rdl`) through `env/sep_decode_resp.py`; the window build fails if a dead span is not the Past Extent of its row. For ABR (`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc:81-83`) and the SPI controller (`hw/sys/sep/doc/peripheral_subsystem.adoc:43-44`) the prose also states DECERR, and the window build checks that the RDL agrees. Every probe uses the CPU-LSU splice (`s_axi`). The TRNG window has no `sep_memory_map.rdl` past-extent response; there any refusal passes this checker and CHK-TRNG-UNOWNED grades the code. A failure line names the window, the expected code and word and the received code and word. A single accepted or wrong probe fails the checker; there is no tolerated count.
|OBS-DEADSPACE-CODE
|Logged only, no pass or fail. For every TRNG-window probe, the response code and, for a read, the read word. `sep.rdl` gives the TRNG window no past-extent response; CHK-TRNG-UNOWNED grades its code, and its read data is an open specification question. With no TRNG fitted, CHK-TRNG-UNOWNED grades the read word against the generated memory map.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-NO-ALIAS
|No allocated register in that window changes across a probe, the directed wrap anchors included, and a read of a dead offset never returns a live register's value. The past extent is the part of the aperture that no register backs (Decoded Extent, generated memory map), so a probe that moves a live register has aliased onto it. Outside TRNG the read word is graded exactly under CHK-DEADSPACE-REFUSE; in the TRNG window a read that returns a neighbouring ESRC register value fails here. A single aliased probe fails the checker.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-BURST
|In every crypto-region window, no beat of the read burst returns the value of a register in the window snapshot, the write burst answers DECERR, and no armed register moves. A burst that times out fails. At least one window must answer a single beat at a burst address OKAY, so the refusal is the burst rule and not a dead address.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-BEAT
|In every crypto-region window, the bus monitor's per-beat `RRESP` vector for the read burst is DECERR on every beat, inside the extent as well as past it. A missing vector, or one whose length does not match the beats issued, fails, since unrelated traffic on the bus would lengthen it. A write burst has one `BRESP`, so it is graded under CHK-DEADSPACE-BURST.
|CTRL-TRNG-RSP-DRIVE
|Bench control, not a DUT check. The responder is on with both handshake counts at 0 before the first access, and each reply beat on the port carries the code, and for a read the word, that the leaf set for that handshake. Each beat is read from PR-TRNG-RSP, not from the request that the leaf issued, so a reply check rests on a code seen at the port. A responder that does not give the set reply fails the leaf, so CHK-TRNG-REPLY, CHK-TRNG-SPLIT-WR and CHK-TRNG-BURST never grade an unset golden. The DUT cannot fail this control: the bench drives the net. Evidence: one `CTRL-TRNG-RSP-DRIVE LOG` line per port reply beat, and `CTRL-TRNG-RSP-DRIVE PASS`.
|CTRL-TRNG-RSP-OFF
|Bench control, not a DUT check. `ext_trng_axil_rsp_on_o` reads 0 before the cell pass, so the TRNG cell is graded with no TRNG fitted.
|CHK-TRNG-REPLY
|A single-beat (AxSIZE 2) read and a single-beat write to the TRNG window, each answered OKAY, SLVERR and DECERR at the port, reach the port as exactly one AXI-Lite access with exactly one reply beat and no other beat, and the initiator receives the port's code (`doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc`, TRNG path: an adopter endpoint's response, including an error, returns to the initiator unchanged). Anchor: PR-TRNG and PR-TRNG-RSP, with the port code read at the port. The check grades the codes; the read data is logged, because no sentence states it. Catches a window that answers the initiator with a code of its own, that maps a port error to another code, or that forwards an access twice or not at all. A response that does not arrive within a bounded wait fails the check. Control: CTRL-TRNG-RSP-DRIVE; and the port shows all three codes on a read and on a write. Evidence: `CHK-TRNG-REPLY PASS: <access> driven=<c> port=<c> init=<c> port_addr=0x<a> port_beats=1 port_rdata=<v> init_rdata=0x<v>`. The port AR or AW also carries the issued window offset: `addr[11:0]` at the port equals `addr[11:0]` of the issued access (`doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc:117-119`: SEP forwards every access of the 4 kB window and does not decode offsets inside it). Offsets 0x000, 0xFFC and one interior word are forced on every seed. Evidence adds `port_off=0x<o> issued_off=0x<o>`.
|OBS-TRNG-PORT-ADDR
|Logged only, no pass or fail. The port address bits above the 12-bit window offset. No specification states them; whether SEP drives the system address, the offset only or an unstated value is an open specification question.
|CHK-TRNG-SPLIT-WR
|A 64-bit (AxSIZE 3, AxLEN 0) write reaches the port as two AXI-Lite writes. With the first answered SLVERR and the last OKAY, the initiator receives SLVERR (`doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc`, TRNG path: a split write returns SLVERR when an earlier fragment erred and the last is OKAY). Anchor: PR-TRNG (two write address beats and two data beats) and PR-TRNG-RSP (SLVERR then OKAY). Catches a window that returns only the last fragment's OKAY and so hides an error. Evidence: `CHK-TRNG-SPLIT-WR PASS: <access> port_aw=<a,a> port=[SLVERR,OKAY] init=SLVERR`.
|CHK-TRNG-BURST
|With the port set to answer OKAY, a read burst and a write burst (AxLEN above 0) to the TRNG window answer DECERR on every R beat and on the B, and no handshake of any channel occurs on the port (`hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc`, "Single-Beat Access Only": an access with a non-zero AxLEN is answered DECERR on every beat and no beat reaches an accelerator; the TRNG window lies in the crypto region, `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` and the generated memory map). Anchor: PR-TRNG and PR-TRNG-RSP (silent) and the bus monitor's per-beat `RRESP` vector. Because the port is set to OKAY, a beat that reaches the port shows as a port handshake.
