Evidence: `CHK-TRNG-BURST PASS: read burst at 0x<a> answered [<c,...>], write burst answered <c>, <n> beats each with the port set to OKAY; port handshakes read=0 write=0`.
|OBS-TRNG-SPLIT-PREC
|Logged only, no pass or fail. The code that the initiator receives for a 64-bit write whose last port write errs (a seeded pair from OKAY+SLVERR, OKAY+DECERR, SLVERR+DECERR, DECERR+SLVERR), and for a 64-bit read whose two port reads answer different codes. `smu-sep.adoc` states only the case of an earlier error and a last OKAY. Which code wins when the fragments differ in another way is an open specification question. Each access also passes CTRL-TRNG-RSP-DRIVE.
|CHK-TRNG-UNOWNED
|Every TRNG-window probe is refused (never OKAY, no timeout), a refused read returns no neighbouring ESRC register value, and no ESRC register moves; at least one read and one write are refused. The reference integration connects no external TRNG, so no offset owns a register. For that case `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` states the code: the window ends in a DECERR slave, so every single-beat read and write answers DECERR. Each probe is graded against that code, and the `INFO-TRNG-UNOWNED-RESP` lines log each one. The window lies in the crypto region, so its burst leg, issued at the window base, is graded under CHK-DEADSPACE-BEAT and CHK-DEADSPACE-BURST. A refused read also returns the word 0xBADCAB1E, which the generated memory map states for the window with no TRNG fitted (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`, note of the External TRNG row: DECERR, 0xBADCAB1E / DECERR; codes assigned from the RTL, accepted as the golden until the specification states the data).
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0. OBS-DEADSPACE-CODE, OBS-TRNG-SPLIT-PREC and OBS-TRNG-PORT-ADDR log and take no part in the verdict, and CTRL-TRNG-RSP-DRIVE and CTRL-TRNG-RSP-OFF fail the leaf when the bench does not set the responder as required.

*Scope*: The TRNG window (`TRNG_REG_MAP_BASE_ADDR` up to the lifecycle base) allocates no SEP register. With the reference DECERR slave on the port, CHK-TRNG-UNOWNED grades the window. With the bench responder on the port, the passthrough checks grade the reply that the initiator receives. The leaf does not grade the read data of a TRNG access answered by the bench responder, the port address bits above the window offset, the code of a split beat whose last half errs, or the code of a 64-bit read whose halves differ.

*Evidence*: each probe that is not refused, or that moves a live register, logs `CHK-DEADSPACE FAIL [anchor]` or `[rand]` with the reason. The probe loop then fails once with a `CHK-DEADSPACE-REFUSE FAIL` summary that counts the accepted, the wrong-code and the aliasing probes, which covers CHK-DEADSPACE-REFUSE and CHK-DEADSPACE-NO-ALIAS.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test]]
===== 2.11 `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — region and source page from the run seed; the dest is `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` so the offset is determined and non-vacuous.

*Objective*: Scope is the sixteen programmable local-master regions ahead of the system-peripherals routing demux (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, "Address Remapping").

*Test Coverage*: Local-alias remap datapath, not the CSR bank alone.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`. Read the seeded source page through the identity path.
. Write the marker to `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` and read it back. Require the source page's value to differ from the marker.
. Park `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` at the inverse of the marker and read it back (`CTRL-OFFSET-PARK seen: addr=0x<a> wrote=0x<v> read=0x<v> resp_ok=1`; a readback other than the parked value logs `CTRL-MISSING OFFSET-PARK` and fails the leaf, because CHK-OFFSET-WRITE needs the parked value to differ from the marker).
. Program the seeded region and write the marker through the alias; the identity `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` readback must move to the marker (CHK-OFFSET-WRITE).
. Read through the alias and require the marker. Then change `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` at its identity address only; the aliased read must follow it (CHK-OFFSET).
. Write the marker to `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` at its identity address and read it back (`CTRL-VALID-GATE-RESTORE seen: addr=0x<a> wrote=0x<v> read=0x<v> resp_ok=1`; a readback other than the marker logs `CTRL-MISSING VALID-GATE-RESTORE` and fails the leaf, because CHK-VALID-GATE needs the destination to differ from the source page's value). Reprogram the same region with bounds and offset live and only the valid bit clear, and read the source address with the PR-OUT tap running. Before the tap starts, outbound filter entries 0 and 1 are programmed as a pair over the 4 KiB page at the reset `SMU_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` (`allow_ns` 0 and 1, read and write allowed, `src_id` 0); after the gated read, with the tap still running, read the word at offset `0x100` of that page, then return both entries to their RDL reset values. Require no AR handshake on the outbound port for the gated read, and OKAY and exactly one AR handshake at that address for the liveness read: the route demux sends the SMU aperture to the outbound port (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, "Fabric Topology", output fabric) (`CTRL-VALID-GATE-LOCAL seen: addr=0x<a> pr_out_ar=0 [] live_addr=0x<a> live_ar=1 [ar[addr=0x<a> ...]]`; an AR handshake for the gated read, or a liveness read with no AR handshake, more than one, or one at another address, logs `CTRL-MISSING VALID-GATE-LOCAL` and fails the leaf) and the source page's own value, not the marker (CHK-VALID-GATE).
. Program the seeded region again. Read the other source page before any region covers it and require its own value to differ from the marker (`CTRL-OFFSET-SIB-OWN seen: region=<r> addr=0x<a> read=0x<v> marker=0x<m> resp_ok=1`; a read equal to the marker, or not OKAY, logs `CTRL-MISSING OFFSET-SIB-OWN` and fails the leaf, because a second-region read that passes through would then match). Program a second upper-half region over that page, both regions to `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL`. Park `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` again (`CTRL-OFFSET-PARK`, same format and failure as above). Write the marker through the first region; the identity `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` readback must move from the parked value to the marker (CHK-OFFSET-WRITE, same format as above). Then read through the first and the second region with no write in between; each read must return the marker (`CHK-OFFSET PASS: region <r> read 0x<a> returns marker 0x<m> of CLOCK_GATE_CTRL 0x<d>`, one line per region). Sample `ext_debug_bus_o[207:192]` after each read. Then write and read through the second region and sample again. Log the three samples without a verdict (OBS-DBG-AR).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-OFFSET-WRITE
|An aliased write of the marker moves the identity `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` readback from a parked value (the marker with every implemented bit inverted) to the marker, once for the seeded region alone and once for the first region of the two-region step. A write that lands on any other address leaves the parked value.
|CHK-OFFSET
|A programmed region's live read equals input plus the programmed offset. The aliased read returns the marker, then returns the parked value after a write to `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` at its identity address only. The source page's own value differs from the marker, so a read that passes through, or lands on any other register, cannot match both. In the two-region step, the first region's write moves `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` from the parked value to the marker (CHK-OFFSET-WRITE), so it did not land on the first source page, and each source page's own value differs from the marker (the first by the vacuity guard, the second by CTRL-OFFSET-SIB-OWN). Each region's read of the marker therefore shows that region remapped.
|OBS-DBG-AR
|Logged only. `ext_debug_bus_o[207:192]` after the read in each of two regions with the write held, and after a write and a read in the second region. `port_table.adoc` states only "External debug observation bus", and no document or RDL assigns this lane, so no value is graded.
|CHK-VALID-GATE
|The region valid bit gates the remap: `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` ("Address Remapping") states that an invalid entry behaves as a transparent pass-through. The same window is reprogrammed with bounds and offset live and only the enable clear, and the beat must then read the value at the address the master issued rather than the remapped destination. Programming the window first is what separates a working gate from an address that was never in range, and the vacuity guard on `CHK-OFFSET` has already shown the two values differ. The source page's own value can be zero, which the bench outbound responder also returns, so CTRL-VALID-GATE-LOCAL requires the gated read to put no AR handshake on the PR-OUT observation tap: the read does not leave on the outbound port. The liveness read of the SMU-aperture word in the same tap window must show its one AR handshake, so the absence cannot come from a tap that records nothing. An unbacked local word also reads zero; the check does not tell it from the source page. The cacheable flag that `fabric.adoc` lists per region is not checked: this bench has no observable on the outbound cache bits.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: This entry is distinct from the CPU high-alias window (`sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test`). CSR programming stays on `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`. Local-master `REGION_ATTRS` has `valid` and `cacheable`. Cacheable overrides AXI `cache` on a hit; this vehicle does not observe that field. `ext_debug_bus_o[207:192]` is logged only (OBS-DBG-AR): no RDL, generated header or document defines what that lane carries.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_local_alias_offset_walk_test]]
===== 2.12 `sep_fabric_local_alias_offset_walk_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_local_alias_offset_walk_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master); `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (outbound responder: OKAY at any address)

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the seed selects every target, bound and the staged word. Every region runs at each seed; one seed per regression, because the offset bit coverage does not depend on the seed.

*Objective*: Every local-master alias region translates with all 56 offset bits on live traffic, and its START/END compare decides a response at the drawn bounds. Each offset bit decides the landing address. START and END are graded at the source page, at S and ~S, and at E and ~E; no single START or END bit is shown to decide a response.

*Test Coverage*: All 16 alias regions (`alias_remap.rdl` start_addr[55:12], end_addr[55:12], offset[55:12], valid[63]). The source beat is a staged START_ADDR low word of the last inbound-filter entry, which stays disabled. A hit targets an address at or above `0x1_0000_0000`, which the routing demux sends (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`) to the SMU path and through the block-by-default outbound filter.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage the source word from the CPU-LSU master.
. For each region: with offset O, then ~O, the region starts at the source page and ends at E, then ~E (both above the page); one outbound entry admits only the granule at the predicted target. Read the source beat and the next beat.
. Clear valid with the hit bounds: read the source beat.
. Program bounds starting at S, then ~S (both above the source page): read the source beat.
. Program the region to start and end at the source page (END is non-inclusive), with offset ~O and valid set: read the source beat (CHK-ALIAS-WALK-MISS).
. For the first region, program an offset whose page sum carries out of address bit 55 and read the source beat (CHK-ALIAS-CARRY). The target page is the cold scratch page and the low 12 bits keep the source offset, so the target is an unbacked word of the Reserved row 0x108020C0-0x10802FFF; the test takes the row and its cell from the generated memory map and stops if the target is not in a Reserved row.
. Restore the region and the entry to reset.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ALIAS-WALK-READBACK
|Every alias START/END/ATTRS and outbound-filter write reads back as programmed (56 bits).
|CHK-ALIAS-WALK-HIT
|The translated beat answers OKAY in the one granule at `{offset[55:12] + addr[55:12], addr[11:0]}` and DECERR one granule up, so the alias adder produced exactly that 56-bit address.
|CHK-ALIAS-WALK-MISS
|A region that starts above the source page does not translate, and a valid region that starts and ends at the source page (an empty range, END non-inclusive per `hw/ip/axi_alias_remap/regs/alias_remap.rdl:32`) does not translate: OKAY with the staged word. The END leg keeps the outbound entry open at the ~O target, so a translated beat would return the outbound responder's data, not the staged word; it fails an END compare that is ignored or stuck true.
|CHK-ALIAS-WALK-VALID
|With valid clear the region does not translate: OKAY with the staged word.
|CHK-ALIAS-CARRY
|For a hit whose page sum carries out of bit 55, the read answers DECERR with 0xBADCAB1E and no AR leaves on PR-OUT. The page sum is modulo 2^44 and the carry is discarded (`hw/ip/axi_alias_remap/regs/alias_remap.rdl:42`); every address below 0x1_0000_0000 returns to the peripheral crossbar (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc:181-182`), which decodes the `SEP_LOCAL` initiator as inbound traffic (`fabric.adoc:138-143`); the target row is Reserved with DECERR, 0xBADCAB1E (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc:47`, inbound column per `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc:66-69`). A kept carry sends the beat out on PR-OUT, a saturated sum lands elsewhere, and an untranslated beat returns the staged word with OKAY; each fails. Anchor: the LSU response and read data, and the PR-OUT AR count. Evidence: `CHK-ALIAS-CARRY PASS: region=<r> offset=0x<O> src=0x<addr> target=0x<addr> row=Reserved resp=3 rdata=0xbadcab1e pr_out_ar=0 expect=3/0xbadcab1e/pr_out_ar=0`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_axi_order_sweep_test]]
===== 2.13 `sep_axi_order_sweep_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_order_sweep_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — every (register x ordering) cell runs on every seed, and every (ordering x size) cell with it. The seed varies the staged data, the channel separation and the response backpressure, never which cell runs.

*Objective*: AXI write address and write data are independent channels with no ordering requirement between them (IHI 0022 A3.3), so AW-first, W-first and same-cycle are all legal stimulus and each must leave the written value in the addressed register without displacing its neighbour. The ordering is driven by holding the trailing channel until the leading one asserts VALID, and the ordering each cell actually presented is read off the bus and asserted against what was requested. The orderings are presented at the s_axi fabric ingress, and the contract is end to end: each one must land at every write-safe register of every swept block, not only at one scratch word. The crossbar in front of the block adapters can re-serialize a write (on the CSRNG path it delivers W one cycle after AW whatever the master presents, see <<sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test>>), so this test does not claim that each adapter sees all three orderings; W-first and same-cycle orderings at an adapter port are covered for the DRBG lane adapter module by `sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test`. The sweep configures all three orderings at every write-safe register and crosses them with the 1/2/4-byte sizes on the scratch words, where a narrow write has no side effect. The narrow cells compare the whole software-usable word against the AXI lane rule; `sep_axi_strobe_window_test` owns the same rule at default timing.

*Test Coverage*: AW/W channel ordering at every write-safe register, from the LSU splice.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Take the register inventory and the exclusion reasons from `sep_reg_bit_bash_seq`: `write_reason() is None` for the no-side-effect blocks, plus `touch_reason() is None` restricted to `TOUCH_BLOCKS` for plain RW storage inside the other major IPs.
. Drop the blocks where the access itself would decide the result. `WDT_TIMER` is that block: `clk_wdt_i` is 5 µs and the AXI timeout is 50 µs, about ten watchdog edges, so whether a `prim_reg_cdc` round trip fits depends on the phase the access arrives on. A probe there measures CDC latency against the timeout rather than channel delivery. `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test` covers that block's storage, and it runs `clk_wdt` at eight core periods so the handshake retires inside the timeout.
. Prime the register with the complement under its write mask at default timing, and read it back, so a write that never lands reads as the prime rather than looking plausible.
. Apply the cell's timing profile through the VIP master (`AxiTimingProfile`, `ocah_axi_master_config`) to hold AW or W back, write the staged value, and read it back at default timing so the compare tests the write and not the read.
. Read a second register in the same block, write it back with one software-usable bit flipped at default timing, read it again, then re-read the swept register. Deriving the value from the current contents keeps a write that never arrived from reading back correct.
. A shadowed register commits on the second identical write. The profile arms a one-shot release, so it shapes the first write only and the committing second write goes out same-cycle. The ordering under test is the one the register sees first; the commit itself is not an ordered access.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ORDER-LAND
|The written value is in the register after the ordering write. An adapter that latches a pending-write flag on AW accept and reads that flag in the same cycle the W arrives answers OKAY while dropping the data, so the compare is on the readback, never on the response. Every cell must produce a data compare; a cell that could not run is named with its reason, and the count then fails the test rather than reading as a full sweep.
|CHK-ORDER-DISPLACE
|The next write in the block lands on the register it addresses, and the swept register does not change when its neighbour is written. A pending flag left set by the ordering commits the following W handshake to the wrong target, which CHK-ORDER-LAND cannot see: it reads the register that was written, not the neighbour that was corrupted. The follow-on value is derived from what the neighbour currently holds and flips one software-usable bit, so a write that never arrived cannot read back correct. A refused follow-on write or readback fails this checker rather than being recorded as a skip. A cell in a single-register block offers no neighbour to write, so the required witness count is the number of cells that have one.
|CHK-ORDER-XSIZE
|All nine (ordering x size) cells produced a data compare, so the write lands at every transfer size and not only at the bus width.
|CHK-ORDER-STIM
|Each cell presented the ordering it requested, read off the AW/W valid assertions rather than taken from the configured profile. Asserted per cell. The slave's handshake is logged separately and is allowed to coincide: a slave that holds WREADY until AW answers a W-first presentation same-cycle, which is its own contract, not a stimulus failure.
|CHK-COVERAGE
|The cell count is asserted against a floor and against the cells the config presented, then the per-ordering tally, the blocks reached, the (ordering x size) grid and the witness count are logged. Every skipped register is counted under its reason on the configuration line the test logs first. A silent skip would let a shrinking sweep keep reporting PASS.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The cell count for the seed is logged against the configured total, so a shrunken sweep is visible.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_axi_order_sweep_m_axi_test]]
===== 2.14 `sep_axi_order_sweep_m_axi_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_order_sweep_m_axi_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- every (register x ordering) cell runs on every seed. The seed varies the staged data, the channel separation and the response backpressure, never which cell runs.

*Objective*: The same ordering contract as `sep_axi_order_sweep_test`, driven from the SMN inbound master instead of the CPU-LSU splice. Both masters reach the same IP block adapters, so an adapter that mishandles a channel ordering has to be caught from either. This path also grants a W-first handshake: the CPU-LSU register adapters hold WREADY until AW, so a W-first presentation is accepted same-cycle there, while the inbound path accepts W before AW.

*Test Coverage*: AW/W channel ordering at every write-safe register, from the SMN inbound master.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Assert the cell floor when the walk completes, and require the compare count to equal the cells the config presented, so a walk shrunk by a map or routing change fails rather than reporting a clean pass over fewer cells.
. Program the inbound filter from the CPU-LSU side: the filter denies by default, so five allow windows are opened over the swept extent. The window bounds follow `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` (Address Range Granule): with `allow_burst = 0` the granule is the data bus width, address bits [2:0] are ignored, `START_ADDR` widens down and `END_ADDR` widens up.
. Walk the cells exactly as the CPU-LSU sweep does.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ORDER-LAND
|The written value is in the register after the ordering write, compared on the readback rather than on the response, so an adapter that answers OKAY while dropping the data fails. Every cell must produce a data compare; a cell that could not run is named with its reason and the count then fails the test.
|CHK-ORDER-DISPLACE
|The next write in the block lands on the register it addresses and the swept register does not change when its neighbour is written, so a pending flag left set by the ordering cannot commit the following W handshake to the wrong target. The follow-on value is derived from what the neighbour currently holds with one software-usable bit flipped. The required witness count is the number of cells whose block offers a neighbour.
|CHK-ORDER-XSIZE
|All nine (ordering x size) cells produced a data compare, so the write lands at every transfer size and not only at the bus width.
|CHK-ORDER-STIM
|Each cell presented the ordering it requested, read off the AW/W valid assertions rather than taken from the configured profile. On this path a W-first presentation is accepted before AW, which is the inbound adapter's own contract and is logged rather than treated as a stimulus failure — the opposite of the CPU-LSU register adapters, which hold WREADY until AW and answer W-first same-cycle.
|CHK-COVERAGE
|The cell count is asserted against a floor and against the cells the config presented, then the per-ordering tally, the blocks reached, the (ordering x size) grid and the witness count are logged. Every skipped register is counted under its reason on the configuration line the test logs first, including the sixteen `INBOUND_FILTER_CTRL_WRAP_FILTER_CTRL` blocks this path cannot sweep. A silent skip would let a shrinking sweep keep reporting PASS.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The cell count for the seed is logged against the configured total, so a shrunken sweep is visible.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: the sixteen `INBOUND_FILTER_CTRL_WRAP_FILTER_CTRL` blocks are not swept here and are counted in the skip tally. Those CSRs are the filter the access is passing through, so writing one mid-walk would reprogram the path under the test and measure filter behaviour rather than adapter ordering. They are swept in full on `sep_axi_order_sweep_test`, whose bus has no inbound filter. No allow window covers the filter CSR bank, so the ownership hole `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test` asserts stays shut.

'''''

[[sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test]]
===== 2.15 `sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test`

*BFM / Model*: TB-instantiated `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter` (`u_tbadp_vehicle` in `tb/tb_top.sv`), driven pin-level from cocotb

*Randomization*: none. The three orderings are fixed and all three run on every seed; the contract is a cycle alignment, not a data pattern.

*Objective*: AW, W and AR are independent AXI channels (IHI 0022 A3.3). In idle the adapter accepts each write half independently until that half is pending, and accepts a read only when neither write half is pending. This leaf presents all three legal orderings at the port and requires both accesses to retire under each.

*Test Coverage*: All three legal AW/W/AR orderings at the TB-owned adapter port.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read the live CSRNG lane and require OKAY before the vehicle is touched (`CHK-PORT-ANCHOR`).
. On the vehicle, retire a lone write, a lone read, and an AW-then-W gap with no AR, each answering OKAY (`CHK-PORT-CONTROL`).
. Present each of the three legal AW/W/AR orderings at the vehicle port. Confirm the port presented the named ordering and, in the two gapped-write cells, that a cycle had AR valid with one write half handshaken and the other pending (`CHK-PORT-STIM`). Confirm both accesses retire in bound (`CHK-PORT-PROGRESS`). In every cell, require that no AR handshakes while a write half is pending, and in the two gapped-write cells that AR handshakes only after both write halves (`CHK-PORT-ARBITRATION`). Reset the vehicle between cells.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,4",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract

|CHK-PORT-ANCHOR
|A plain read of the live CSRNG lane answers OKAY before the vehicle is touched. The vehicle is a TB-owned instance, so without this the leaf could report a pass against a DUT that never left reset; it also puts a transaction through the AXI agent, which the scoreboard requires as positive evidence before it will allow a pass at all.

|CHK-PORT-CONTROL
|Three legs, all required to retire AND to answer OKAY, before any overlap cell runs. A lone write and a lone read show the vehicle and the AXI-Lite-32 responder behind the adapter are alive at all -- without them a broken driver would report every ordering as stalled and imitate the defect exactly. A third leg drives AW and then W four cycles later with no AR, the same separation the `aw-then-ar` and `w-then-ar` cells use, so the separation itself is excluded as the cause of their stall by observation rather than by reading the design under test. The response code carries the other half: an access the adapter rejects as unsupported is answered SLVERR straight out of `ST_IDLE` with nothing forwarded and retires just as promptly, so OKAY is what shows the forwarding leg is alive, and OKAY at the 64-bit port cannot be produced without the downstream handshake. The access itself is AMBA-legal by construction -- aligned, with the byte-lane strobe its address selects (IHI 0022), computed by `sep_spec_tables.axi_lane_strobe` -- so an adapter whose supported-access rule disagreed with the protocol answers this stimulus for itself rather than selecting it.

|CHK-PORT-STIM
|The ordering the port actually presented, read off `tbadp_chan_o` rather than taken from the offsets requested. In `aw-then-ar` and `w-then-ar` the cell must also reach the half-committed state the arbitration row grades: a cycle with AR valid, one write half handshaken and the other pending. That needs the two write halves to handshake in different cycles and AR to be valid by the second. A cell that misses either is a stimulus failure and is reported as one, never scored as coverage -- a cell that did not overlap says nothing about the arbitration in either direction.

|CHK-PORT-PROGRESS
|Both accesses retire: BVALID and RVALID are both seen within a bounded cycle count, and both responses are OKAY. The bound also records the longest run of held-valids-with-every-ready-low, so a hang that `ASSERT_KNOWN` would miss (a stable `1'b0` is not X) still fails the leaf.
|CHK-PORT-ARBITRATION
|In every ordering, no AR handshake falls after one write-half handshake and at or before the other, so no read is accepted while a write half is pending. In `aw-then-ar` and `w-then-ar`, where AR arrives while the leading half is pending, the AR handshake occurs strictly after both AW and W handshakes. In `all-same-cycle` all three may handshake together from idle. Logging the cycles without comparing them is not evidence.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: the MODULE's arbitration, not the SEP integration. The DUT's own lane adapters sit behind the crossbar, which delivers W one cycle after AW and re-serializes every write to that order whatever a master presents -- `CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL` on the two fabric-driven leaves measures the gap at both ends and reports it as fabric-introduced. A same-cycle AW/W/AR presentation and a W-before-AW presentation are therefore not producible at a DUT adapter port from the fabric side, which is why this vehicle exists. It is a second instance of the same RTL module with its own reset and no fabric in front of it: every legal ordering is presentable to the cycle, and a wedged cell is cleared without resetting the DUT, so the three verdicts are independent. It claims nothing about the integration; the two leaves that follow own that half.

*Not claimed*: the SEP integration. That a fabric master can present the one ordering the fabric can produce is proven separately.

'''''

[[sep_drbg_axil_adapter_refusal_test]]
===== 2.16 `sep_drbg_axil_adapter_refusal_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_axil_adapter_refusal_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip on s_axi for the DUT's CSRNG and EDN lane adapters, with each adapter's AXI-Lite-64 input handshakes observed on `drbg_csrng_axil_chan_o` / `drbg_edn_axil_chan_o` and its AXI-Lite-32 request on `drbg_csrng_fwd_o` / `drbg_edn_fwd_o`; TB-instantiated `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter` (`u_tbadp_vehicle` in `tb/tb_top.sv`) driven pin-level from cocotb, with its AXI-Lite-32 request observed on `tbadp_fwd_o`

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- per DUT lane, `SepSeededRng(seed)` picks the byte offset of the misaligned read (1, 2 or 3), the width of the narrow write (1 or 2 bytes), the value that write carries (any implemented `INTR_ENABLE` pattern that differs from the register's value), and the 8-byte granule of the two lane reads (CSRNG: `REGWEN` and `CTRL`, or `INT_STATE_READ_ENABLE` and `INT_STATE_READ_ENABLE_REGWEN`; EDN: `REGWEN` and `CTRL`, or `BOOT_INS_CMD` and `BOOT_GEN_CMD`). The vehicle cells are fixed and all run on every seed.

*Objective*: The supported-access rule of `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter` as `hw/ip/drbg/doc/architecture.adoc` ("Bus Protocol Adaptation") states it: the adapter forwards only aligned single-lane accesses with WSTRB `0x0F` or `0xF0`, selects the 32-bit data lane by address bit 2, and answers every other access AXI SLVERR with no downstream request (`hw/ip/drbg/doc/architecture.adoc:81-84` and `:87`). The graded refusals are a write whose strobe is not the one its address bit 2 selects (`0x0F` at bit 2 = 0, `0xF0` at bit 2 = 1), a write whose address is not 4-byte aligned, and a read whose address is not 4-byte aligned. A refused read returns zero data. The refusal must leave the adapter able to serve the next supported access. A supported read returns the downstream word in the lane address bit 2 selects and zero in the other lane (`architecture.adoc:83-84`).

*Test Coverage*: On both DUT lane adapters (CSRNG, EDN): the refused narrow write, the two lane reads of one granule (the bit 2 = 0 word and the bit 2 = 1 word) and the misaligned read. On the vehicle: a full 64-bit beat, a byte strobe, a halfword strobe, a zero strobe, the strobe of the other lane and a misaligned write address in each lane, and a misaligned read at each byte offset of each lane; the supported access after every vehicle cell, with a zero other lane on a read. Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_bus_access_cg` `x_drbg_lane` (six bins) and `cp_lane_refuse`, owner window 740. Per DUT lane three windows open: the refused write with `CHK-REFUSE-DUT-WRITE` and `CHK-REFUSE-DUT-CONTROL` (`cp_lane_refuse`), the two lane reads with `CHK-REFUSE-DUT-LANE`, and the misaligned read with `CHK-REFUSE-DUT-READ`. The leaf writes the cells of the check that passed to the `crypto_bus_pass` label, a window closes two `clk_i` edges after that write, and a FAIL closes it first, so the cell of a failing access never samples. The two wrong-lane strobe cells of `x_drbg_lane` are `ignore_bins`: no DUT port presents them, and the vehicle, which has no bins, grades that rule.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up `no_cpu`. Require the CSRNG and EDN bits of `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS` clear and read each block's `INTR_ENABLE` (`CHK-REFUSE-ANCHOR`).
. Per DUT lane, write the seeded value to `INTR_ENABLE` as a 1- or 2-byte transfer. Confirm the W strobe presented on s_axi (`CHK-REFUSE-STIM`). Require SLVERR, AW and W handshakes at the lane adapter's AXI-Lite-64 input, the forward probe low on every cycle, `INTR_ENABLE` unchanged and the status bit clear (`CHK-REFUSE-DUT-WRITE`).
. Write the same value as a supported 32-bit write and read it back, each seen on the forward probe, then restore the original value and read it back (`CHK-REFUSE-DUT-CONTROL`).
. With the graded window closed, read both 32-bit words of the seeded granule twice and require both words non-zero and equal across the two reads. Then read each word as one AxSIZE 2 read and require OKAY, one AR at the address of the word, the whole R beat `{32'h0, word}` at bit 2 = 0 or `{word, 32'h0}` at bit 2 = 1, and a read on the forward probe (`CHK-REFUSE-DUT-LANE`).
. Read one byte at the seeded misaligned offset into `INTR_ENABLE`, last on the lane so it cannot change a graded check. Confirm the AR presented on s_axi (`CHK-REFUSE-STIM`). Require SLVERR, the whole R beat 0, an AR handshake at the lane adapter's AXI-Lite-64 input, the forward probe low on every cycle and the status bit clear (`CHK-REFUSE-DUT-READ`).
. Release the vehicle. For each refused cell, require SLVERR with no AXI-Lite-32 request on any cycle, and for a read RDATA 0 on the whole 64-bit beat (`CHK-REFUSE-VEH`). After every cell, without a vehicle reset, present the supported access of the same kind on the same lane and require OKAY, a forwarded request, and for a read the responder's word in the lane address bit 2 selects and zero in the other lane (`CHK-REFUSE-VEH-CONTROL`).

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,4",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract

|CHK-REFUSE-ANCHOR
|The CSRNG and EDN bits of `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS` are clear before any refused access, and `INTR_ENABLE` reads OKAY on both blocks. A bit already set would make the "stays clear" half of every DUT check vacuous.

|CHK-REFUSE-STIM
|s_axi presented the access the cell names: a W beat whose strobe is the one AMBA assigns to the narrow write at that address (`sep_spec_tables.axi_lane_strobe`), and ARADDR at the misaligned byte with ARLEN 0 and ARSIZE 0 for the misaligned read. A mismatch is a stimulus miss, reported as one.

|CHK-REFUSE-DUT-WRITE
|The narrow write answers SLVERR, and `drbg_<lane>_axil_chan_o` shows AW and W handshakes at the adapter's AXI-Lite-64 input with no X/Z cycle, so the adapter accepted the beat it refused. The forward probe is low and not X/Z on every cycle of the access, `INTR_ENABLE` still holds its value under the block's implemented mask, and the lane's status bit is still clear. The written value differs from the register's in an implemented bit, so a forwarded write is visible.

|CHK-REFUSE-DUT-CONTROL
|A supported 32-bit write of the same value answers OKAY and is seen as a write on the forward probe, its read-back is seen as a read and returns the value, and the restore write reads back. This shows the probe sees a forwarded access and the value the refused write carried lands when the adapter forwards it, so "probe low" and "unchanged" in the two checks above can fail, and that the adapter serves a supported access after a refusal.

|CHK-REFUSE-DUT-LANE
|An AxSIZE 2 read of the bit 2 = 0 word of the granule answers OKAY with the whole R beat `{32'h0, word}`, and one of the bit 2 = 1 word answers OKAY with the whole R beat `{word, 32'h0}` (`architecture.adoc:83-84`). Each read is one AR on s_axi at the address of its word and is seen as a read on the forward probe with no X/Z cycle. `word` is the value two earlier 32-bit reads returned, both equal. The neighbour word of the same granule reads non-zero and stable the same way (`CTRL-REFUSE-DUT-LANE-NEIGHBOUR`, reported as `CTRL-MISSING` when it fails), so a lane adapter that leaks the neighbour cannot read as zero. A lane adapter that leaks the neighbour into the other lane, returns stale data, or does not forward the read fails.

|CHK-REFUSE-DUT-READ
|The misaligned one-byte read answers SLVERR with the whole R beat 0 (`architecture.adoc:87`). `drbg_<lane>_axil_chan_o` shows the AR handshake at the adapter's AXI-Lite-64 input with no X/Z cycle, so the adapter accepted the beat it refused. The forward probe is low and not X/Z on every cycle of the access and the lane's status bit stays clear. An adapter that forwards the read, answers it OKAY or returns non-zero data fails.

|CHK-REFUSE-VEH
|Every refused vehicle cell (a misaligned read at each byte offset of each lane, a strobe of the other lane, a full 64-bit beat, a byte strobe, a halfword strobe, a zero strobe, or a misaligned write address) retires with SLVERR, and `tbadp_fwd_o` is low and not X/Z on every cycle from presentation to four cycles after the response. A refused read returns RDATA 0 on the whole 64-bit beat.

|CHK-REFUSE-VEH-CONTROL
|After each vehicle cell, with no vehicle reset, the supported access of the same kind on the same lane answers OKAY and is seen on `tbadp_fwd_o`, which shows the probe can see a forwarded request. A read returns the responder's word in the lane address bit 2 selects and zero in the other lane.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: the DUT half grades the adapter as software reaches it. It grades "nothing forwarded" on the adapter's AXI-Lite-32 request into its TL-UL bridge (`drbg_csrng_fwd_o` / `drbg_edn_fwd_o`, an observation-only probe), which must be low and known on every cycle of the refused access, and through what the DUT shows: the register keeps its value, and the lane's `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS` bit, which is the bridge's sticky TL-UL error (`hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc`, "Crypto Register-Bridge Faults"), stays clear. No legal access sets the CSRNG or EDN bit in this build: their crypto-demux windows end at the last register, so an unmapped offset answers DECERR before it reaches the bridge. The status bit is therefore a second consequence only; the probe is the observable that `CHK-REFUSE-DUT-CONTROL` shows live. One AXI master transfer has a strobe AMBA derives from its address and size, so a wrong-lane strobe, a full-beat 32-bit write, a zero strobe and a misaligned write address with a lane strobe are not presentable at a DUT port; the vehicle presents them.

*Not claimed*: the `unsupported_access_pulse_o` output. It has no CSR and no SEP consumer outside assertions.

'''''

[[sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test]]
===== 2.17 `sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- the seed varies the staged register value only. The channel placement is measured per build, not drawn, so a seed replays the cell without pinning it to a latency the fabric may have changed.

*Objective*: The same arbitration contract as <<sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test>>, but on the DUT's own CSRNG lane adapter and reached the way software reaches it. The two lane adapters sit on the CSRNG and EDN control buses (`drbg.sv` `u_csrng_axil_adapter` / `u_edn_axil_adapter`, fed from `sep_crypto.sv` `csrng_axil` / `edn_axil`), which is the path every SEP-level CSRNG and EDN register access already takes. The cell writes the block's `INTR_ENABLE` and reads its `INTR_STATE` concurrently, so both accesses are behind the same adapter.

*Test Coverage*: One AW-then-AR overlap at the DUT CSRNG lane adapter. Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_bus_access_cg` `x_drbg_lane_arb`, owner window 751. The window opens after the mask probe, the calibration and the prime, one `clk_i` edge before the overlapping pair, and closes after `CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND` passes or at once on a FAIL. The legal cell `ar_held_until_w` samples only when the leaf writes its bit of the `crypto_bus_pass` label after `CHK-CONCURRENT-STIM`, `CHK-CONCURRENT-ARB` and `CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND` pass; the illegal cell samples at the AR handshake.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Measure the CSRNG `INTR_ENABLE` writable width and require it to equal the union of the register's fields in the generated register map (`0xF`), then measure a lone write and a lone read, and place the AW-then-AR overlap from the measured crossbar latencies (`CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL`).
. Present that overlap on `INTR_ENABLE` / `INTR_STATE`. Confirm the CSRNG adapter port (`drbg_csrng_axil_chan_o`) saw the named ordering (`CHK-CONCURRENT-STIM`).
. Require AR to handshake at the adapter port only after both AW and W have handshaken (`CHK-CONCURRENT-ARB`).
. Require both accesses to retire and the write to land (`CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND`).

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,4",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract

|CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL
|The register's writable width is measured, not assumed: all-ones is written and read back, and the seed's pattern is narrowed to the bits that stick. The measured mask must equal the union of the `INTR_ENABLE` fields of the block in the generated register map (CSRNG `0xF`), so an `INTR_ENABLE` that misses a field bit fails; without that compare the measured mask would also narrow the data compare of `CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND` and hide the missing bit. The two blocks implement different interrupt counts, so a hardcoded width is wrong for one of them and a non-existent bit reads back 0 -- indistinguishable from a dropped write. A lone write and a lone read then measure when AW, W and AR arrive at the adapter port through the crossbar. The overlap is placed from those measured latencies: a placement the measured latency cannot reach is reported unreachable rather than fired blind. The same measurement records the AW-to-W gap at the master and at the port and states which side introduced it. Plain untimed traffic already puts AW and AR in the same cycle whenever a store and a load are in flight together.

|CHK-CONCURRENT-STIM
|The ordering the ADAPTER PORT presented, read off `drbg_csrng_axil_chan_o`, not the ordering requested and not the presentation at `s_axi`. The crossbar restaggers the channels, so a presentation at the TB master is not evidence of one at the arbitration point. The leaf is covered only when that presentation is the named ordering. A different overlap, or a cell that missed its overlap, is reported unreachable and does not count as this leaf.

|CHK-CONCURRENT-ARB
|The arbitration rule, read off the handshakes at `drbg_csrng_axil_chan_o`: AR handshakes only after both AW and W have handshaken, so the adapter accepted no read while a write half was pending.

|CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND
|Both accesses retire and the write lands. The check is a bounded timeout plus a data compare against a complement prime; the prime makes a dropped write read back as the prime rather than as a plausible value. The check also records the longest run of held-valids-with-every-ready-low, so a hang that `ASSERT_KNOWN` would miss still fails the leaf.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: one lane, one ordering, one leaf. A stalled adapter cannot be recovered inside a simulation -- the port stays held and the master stays stuck on it -- so a second cell after a wedge would measure the wedge rather than itself.

*Not claimed*: the other two orderings. A true same-cycle AW+W+AR presentation and a W-before-AW presentation are not reachable at a DUT adapter port from a SEP master, for the reason `CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL` measures. They are graded on <<sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test>> instead, at the module's port. `CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL` logs the measured arrivals.

'''''

[[sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_edn_aw_then_ar_test]]
===== 2.18 `sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_edn_aw_then_ar_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_edn_aw_then_ar_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- as <<sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test>>.

*Objective*: The same contract on the second lane adapter, the EDN control path. `drbg.sv` instantiates `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter` twice, so the two lanes are separate hardware and a defect in one instance is not evidence about the other. The checks are those of <<sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test>>, with the probe on `drbg_edn_axil_chan_o` and the access pair on the EDN block's own registers.

*Test Coverage*: One AW-then-AR overlap at the DUT EDN lane adapter. Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_bus_access_cg` `x_drbg_lane_arb`, owner window 752, with the window and the pass label of <<sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test>>.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Measure the EDN `INTR_ENABLE` writable width, then a lone write and a lone read, and place the AW-then-AR overlap from the measured crossbar latencies (`CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL`).
. Present that overlap on the EDN block's own registers. Confirm the EDN adapter port (`drbg_edn_axil_chan_o`) saw the named ordering (`CHK-CONCURRENT-STIM`).
. Require AR to handshake at the adapter port only after both AW and W have handshaken (`CHK-CONCURRENT-ARB`).
. Require both accesses to retire and the write to land (`CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND`).

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,4",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract

|CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL
|Same contract as <<sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test>>, on the EDN interrupt registers. The writable width is measured on this block and must equal the union of its `INTR_ENABLE` fields in the generated register map (`0x3`); the lone write and lone read measure arrival at `drbg_edn_axil_chan_o`.

|CHK-CONCURRENT-STIM
|The ordering the EDN adapter port presented, read off `drbg_edn_axil_chan_o`, not the ordering requested and not the presentation at `s_axi`.

|CHK-CONCURRENT-ARB
|Same contract as <<sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test>>, read off the handshakes at `drbg_edn_axil_chan_o`.

|CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND
|Both accesses retire and the write lands. The stall signature is the same held-valids-with-every-ready-low bound as the CSRNG leaf.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_axi_id_routing_test]]
===== 2.19 `sep_axi_id_routing_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_id_routing_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: none. The proof is a value compare between an ID and the word
it asked for; a random ID or a random value on either side would remove it.

*Objective*: a response comes back to the request that owns it. The crossbars
prepend the master index to the incoming `AxID` and the demux keeps one
outstanding counter per ID, so the ID field is address-like state on the
response path, and a master matches its responses by it.

*Test Coverage*: the transaction-ID dimension of the SEP slave-port crossbar
path, with several reads outstanding at once on distinct IDs, including the
top ID the port's width allows.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Prime the eight `SCRATCH_COLD` words, each with a value that names its own
word, and read each back sequentially.
. Issue eight reads, one per word, each on its own `AxID`, and hold them all
outstanding together by driving the VIP master sequence directly -- the SEP
AXI sequencer awaits each item to completion, so nothing overlaps through it.
. Require each read to return the value of its own word. A response carrying
another word's value is reported with the word it actually came from.
. Grade separately that the IDs issued covered the whole width of the field,
so a shortened list cannot pass the routing compare silently.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ID-PRIME
|The eight scratch words are independent storage each holding its own distinct value. Without this a block that aliased every word onto one register would let the routing compare pass for whichever value was written last.
|CHK-ID-ROUTE
|With eight reads outstanding together on eight distinct IDs, each returns the value of its own word. An ID that is mangled, truncated or dropped delivers a read's data to a different outstanding request, and every access still answers OKAY while it happens -- only the value compare sees it.
|CHK-ID-OVERLAP
|All eight reads are outstanding on the bus at once. A bus that retires each read before it accepts the next returns every value correctly whatever it does to the ID, so without this the routing compare passes on a truncated ID field.
|CHK-ID-TOP
|A breadth guard on the stimulus, not a second routing claim: the set of IDs the routing compare was given covered the whole width of the port's ID field. `CHK-ID-ROUTE` only compares the IDs it was handed, so an edit that shortened the list would keep passing over a subset; an ID field carried in too few bits only surfaces as a data mismatch if a read was actually issued on the top ID.
|OBS-ID-RETURN-ORDER
|Logged only, no pass or fail. The order in which the eight RIDs return, against the issue order. `fabric.adoc` states only that the AXI IDs are retained; whether SEP may return responses on different IDs out of AR order is an open specification question.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Functional coverage*: `sep_axi_decode_cg.cp_id_return`, owner window 759 from after CHK-ID-PRIME to CHK-ID-TOP. A FAIL closes the window before the leaf fails. `rid_matches_word_out_of_order` is graded and stays at 0: the reads of the owner all go to one in-order AXI4-Lite target, and no specification text states whether SEP may return responses on different IDs out of AR order, so no stimulus targets it (see OBS-ID-RETURN-ORDER).

*Scope*: the SEP slave port only, whose `AxID` is three bits wide. The wider
inbound port and write-response (`BID`) routing are not claimed here.

*Anti-vacuity*: the prime leg establishes eight distinct values in eight
independent words before any routing claim is made, so a misrouted response is
distinguishable from a correct one. The concurrency is what gives the fabric
several responses to place: with one access in flight there is one response to
deliver and any routing would look correct. A read that never returns is
reported as such rather than counted as a pass.

'''''

[[sep_axi_strobe_window_test]]
===== 2.20 `sep_axi_strobe_window_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_strobe_window_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the size and lane axes are walked exhaustively rather than seeded, so every AxSIZE at every legal lane offset runs on every seed and no run can issue zero narrow writes. The seed varies the data.

*Objective*: A write narrower than the bus beat must change only the byte lanes its strobe selects (IHI 0022 A3.4.3), and a memory window must return the data that was written to it. A stage that widens the strobe, or writes the whole beat, changes bytes the master never asked for; only a data compare sees it, because the response is OKAY either way.

*Test Coverage*: Write-strobe windows on the LSU splice.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Prime a scratch word with a known value.
. Issue a write narrower than the bus beat at each legal lane offset for each AxSIZE, and read the whole word back.
. Compare against the AXI lane rule: the addressed lanes take the new data, every other lane keeps the primed value.
. Write and read back the OTBN IMEM and DMEM windows.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-STROBE
|Each narrow write changed only its own byte lanes, at every AxSIZE and every legal offset. The line reports the count, so a run that issued no narrow write cannot read as a pass. The compare is the addressed 32-bit word: the generated SEP C headers contain no 4-byte-adjacent register pair, so a 64-bit beat has no neighbour CSR in bytes 4–7 and a widened strobe has no second register to corrupt.
|CHK-WINDOW-DATA
|Each memory window returned the value written to it. A window behind an SRAM-style TileLink adapter applies the mask on reads, unlike a register behind a register adapter which ignores it, so a bridge driving that read mask from the write strobe returns zeros with a correct response. A window not writable in this configuration is named with its reason. At least one window must have been verified: a walk where none was writable proves nothing and fails.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Functional coverage*: `sep_axi_decode_cg.cp_strobe_merge`, owner window 758, one window per narrow write: it opens after the prime word read back as written and closes after the CHK-STROBE compare of that write. The window-probe legs run with the window closed. No window opens after a CHK-STROBE FAIL.

'''''

[[sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test]]
===== 2.21 `sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — `SepAxiPartialLaneReadCfg` is the single source of truth for stimulus and goldens. Required cells walk every seed: all 36 contiguous (offset, length) byte-run slices of a 64-bit word (`8+7+…+1`), all 14 aligned narrow-ARSIZE cells (AxSIZE 0/1/2 at every legal lane offset: `8+4+2`), and the all-zeros / all-ones CSR patterns. The seed varies walk order, SRAM address, the 64-bit word, and extra CSR patterns only.

*Objective*: A read narrower than the 64-bit CPU-LSU beat only obligates the responder to drive the addressed byte lanes: a 32-bit CSR endpoint leaves RDATA[63:32] undriven, and a narrow-ARSIZE beat leaves every non-addressed lane undriven. The VIP master samples the full beat and extracts the addressed bytes, so the value a test receives must be a pure function of the addressed lanes — exact against an independent golden and identical on re-read — regardless of what the undriven lanes carry. `sep_axi_strobe_window_test` owns the write-side lane rule; here the write is trusted and the read extraction is on trial. The contract is pinned through the public VIP read path alone, with no cocotb or cocotbext-axi global state involved.

*Test Coverage*: Partial-lane reads on the LSU splice.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Log the seed, word, addresses and cell counts, and hold the walked counts against the 8-byte-beat floors (36 slices, 14 ARSIZE cells) plus the two required CSR extremes.
. Write a pattern to the 32-bit `SW_DEBUG` CSR, read it back twice as 4-byte reads with a checked read of the non-zero RO `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR` register between them; repeat for the all-zeros, all-ones and seed-random patterns.
. Prime a seed-random 64-bit SRAM word and its neighbour with the complement value.
. Read every contiguous (offset, length) byte run of the primed word, re-reading each slice after a checked full-width read of the complement neighbour.
. Read the primed word at every aligned narrow-ARSIZE cell (AxSIZE 0/1/2 at each legal lane offset), re-reading each cell after the same complement-neighbour disturb.
. After the CSR walk has passed, write 0 to `SW_DEBUG` and read it back exact as a 4-byte read, outside the window. Then open the FCOV owner window, write all-ones to `SW_DEBUG` as one AxSIZE-2 beat with all four strobes, read it back once as one AxSIZE-2 beat, grade the read, and close the window.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RANDCFG
|Walked slice and ARSIZE counts equal the combinatorial floors (`36`, `14`), and the CSR walk starts with all-zeros then all-ones. Seed, word, addresses and counts are logged, so a shrunken walk fails rather than reporting PASS over fewer cells.
|CHK-CSR-LANE
|Each pattern reads back exactly and identically on the repeat, through a beat whose upper four lanes the CSR endpoint never drives. All-zeros always runs next to all-ones and random patterns on the same register, so a dead write path or a zero-filled read cannot pass the set.
|CHK-CSR-LANE (window)
|The in-window AxSIZE-2 read equals the all-ones word written in the window by an AxSIZE-2 write. The register holds 0 before the window (written and read back exact outside it), so a dropped window write reads 0 and fails on every seed. The window closes on the first FAIL.
|CHK-SRAM-SLICE
|Every byte-run slice equals the Python slice of the primed word, and re-reads it exactly after the disturbing read. An extraction that starts at the wrong byte, takes the wrong length, or depends on leftover channel payloads fails the compare; the disturbing read is itself compared against the complement word, so the disturbance is proven to have happened.
|CHK-ARSIZE
|Every aligned AxSIZE-0/1/2 cell returns exactly the addressed lanes of the primed word, and re-reads them identically after the complement-neighbour disturb, on beats where the responder legitimately drives nothing else.
|===

*Functional coverage*: `sep_cpu_ctrl_reg_cg.cp_sw_debug[lane_read_exact]`. Owner window: the single write and read after the CHK-CSR-LANE walk has passed. The bin samples only there, on every seed, so a failing run holds no sample.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Testlist*: `system.toml` (one seed per regression), also in `all.toml` / `cpu_stub`

*Not claimed*: write-side strobe masking (`sep_axi_strobe_window_test`); AxSIZE=3 (that is the slice walk's full-width baseline).

'''''

[[sep_crypto_csr_wide_access_test]]
===== 2.22 `sep_crypto_csr_wide_access_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_csr_wide_access_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves that a 64-bit read on each crypto host path returns the data that path's spec defines, and that concurrent 64-bit reads each return their own data. `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (the `sep_crypto_axi_interconnect` paragraph) decodes one 64-bit AXI slave port to the host paths for OTBN, HMAC, AES, KMAC, ESRC, CSRNG and EDN, "with data-width conversion where required by the endpoint". Every path it names is walked. At CSRNG and EDN, `hw/ip/drbg/doc/architecture.adoc` ("Bus Protocol Adaptation") defines a lane adapter that narrows a 64-bit access to the 32-bit lane selected by address bit [2] and reads the other lane as zero (`architecture.adoc:83`), so the whole 64-bit beat is graded there as `{32'h0, register}`. At the other five paths the 64-bit read must return both words. The shared register driver issues 32-bit beats, so this leaf issues its 64-bit beats directly.

*Test Coverage*: The seven crypto host paths named in `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (the `sep_crypto_axi_interconnect` paragraph) -- OTBN, HMAC, AES, KMAC, entropy source, CSRNG and EDN -- each presented one 64-bit read and its 32-bit equivalents, then several 64-bit reads held in flight at once. Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_bus_access_cg` `cp_wide_split` and `cp_wide_lane`, owner window 748. The window opens right before the one 64-bit read of an aperture and closes one `clk_i` edge after the check of that aperture passes, or at once on a FAIL. The 32-bit golden reads, the neighbour control, the outstanding phase and the slot spread run with the window closed.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For each aperture, read its chosen 8-byte aligned register pair as two 32-bit single beats.
. Read the same two words again, single-beat, and require both passes to agree. A word that changes between them is live state rather than a comparand, and its aperture is reported instead of compared.
. Read the same 8 bytes as ONE 64-bit beat.
. Compare the 64-bit value against the two 32-bit values concatenated.
. At the entropy source, issue `CRYPTO_CONCURRENT_READS` 64-bit reads at that many DISTINCT addresses with distinct `ARID`s, presenting every `AR` before consuming any `R`, and require each to return its own golden with an OKAY response.
. At every aperture, issue the same number of 64-bit reads of the one address already proved readable, distinct `ARID`s, all in flight at once, and require each to return the golden.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WIDE-SPLIT
|The 64-bit read answers OKAY with the AR the testbench master presented at the base address (`ARLEN` 0, `ARSIZE` 3), and equals the two 32-bit reads of the same words, at OTBN, HMAC, AES, KMAC and ESRC. The golden is the DUT's own single-beat path, so this is wide-against-single-beat and never a recomputation. A narrower transfer graded as wide fails on the AR fields.
|CHK-WIDE-LANE
|At CSRNG and EDN, the aligned 64-bit read answers OKAY with the same AR probe, and the whole 64-bit beat equals the 32-bit register at its address, the other lane reading zero (bits [63:32] zero), as the DRBG lane adapter defines (`hw/ip/drbg/doc/architecture.adoc:83`). The 32-bit golden must be non-zero. The register of the other lane of the granule reads non-zero and stable (`CTRL-WIDE-LANE-NEIGHBOUR`, read from the DUT before the window opens), so a lane adapter that leaks the neighbour into bits [63:32] fails the compare.
|CHK-WIDE-NONVAC
|Every aperture in the table was presented, at least three compared a non-zero value, and at least two had distinct non-zero halves. A pair that reads zero compares 0 against 0, and equal or zero halves hide some mutation classes; the two floors make the exact comparisons and the representative zero/duplicate/swap sensitivity explicit. Also requires that the slot spread ran at every aperture.
|CHK-WIDE-OUTSTANDING
|Concurrent 64-bit reads at the entropy source, distinct ids AND distinct addresses, each returning its own data and an OKAY response. The goldens must hold at least two distinct values, else a read answered with another read's data would compare equal and go unseen.
|CHK-WIDE-SLOTS
|Concurrent 64-bit reads at every aperture, distinct ids, each returning the golden; at CSRNG and EDN the golden is the whole beat `{32'h0, register}`. The same integrity claim as CHK-WIDE-OUTSTANDING, carried to every host path in the table rather than only the entropy source, so an aperture answering a concurrent read with zeros or stale data fails. A read answered with another read's data is not visible here because every id reads the same word; CHK-WIDE-OUTSTANDING owns that case with distinct addresses.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Outstanding reads*: a read issued after the previous one retired is answered by an otherwise idle host path, so serial traffic exercises one path however much of it there is. Concurrency is the only shape under which a 64-bit read can be answered with a DIFFERENT read's data, and that is the failure this phase grades. The ids are distinct because a slave may legitimately order same-id reads behind one another, which would serialise the traffic and undo the concurrency. AXI permits the shape: several outstanding reads with different ids, each answered with its own data (IHI 0022 A5.3). The depth is DV-owned (`CRYPTO_CONCURRENT_READS` in `env/sep_spec_tables.py`): the graded claim is data integrity under concurrency, which holds at any depth.

*Evidence*: every pair has a non-zero generated reset value. CSRNG (`INT_STATE_READ_ENABLE`) and EDN (`BOOT_INS_CMD`) have a non-zero low word for the lane check. KMAC (`CFG_REGWEN` / `CFG_SHADOWED`) and the entropy source (`COMPONENT_ID` / `CTRL`) have distinct non-zero halves and carry the full zero/duplicate/swap mutation sensitivity. AES has equal non-zero halves; HMAC and OTBN have one zero half. Those three still require the 64-bit value to equal their two 32-bit reads exactly, but do not claim that every half mutation is observable on that pair. Addresses come from the generated register header by symbol, never a literal.

*Scope note*: the walked set is the host-path list in `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (the `sep_crypto_axi_interconnect` paragraph); the per-path contract comes from that path's spec. The write direction is out of scope: these are status and configuration registers, and writing them to obtain a comparand would test the register rather than the 64-bit read path.

'''''

[[sep_axi_map_refuse_test]]
===== 2.23 `sep_axi_map_refuse_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_map_refuse_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — every directed anchor that is not on the exclude list runs on every seed, plus seed-selected addresses from the reserved rows. Excluded spans are skipped with a counted reason, and a row that could not yield its full quota is reported in the per-row shortfall line.

*Objective*: Rows marked reserved in the DV-owned table in `env/sep_axi_decode_map.py` allocate nothing, so an access there must not answer OKAY. The refusal expectation is taken from that table. Named reserved spans are excluded from the probe set with a counted reason. The walk grades refusal only, so any refusal passes and the code is logged; the code `sep.rdl` states is graded by `sep_unmapped_access_policy_test` at the points that test probes. Scope is the gaps BETWEEN block windows; `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` walks the dead tail INSIDE one, and neither subsumes the other.

*Test Coverage*: Reserved addresses complete with an AXI error response (DECERR or SLVERR). The code `sep.rdl` states for each is not graded here.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Classify from the DV-owned table in `env/sep_axi_decode_map.py`; a reserved row, and any address in no row at all, allocates nothing. Inside the dual scratch bank (`0x1080_2000`-`0x1080_2FFF`), OTP (`0x1093_0000`-`0x1093_FFFF`) and system-bus (`0x10A0_0000`-`0x10A3_FFFF`) windows, each allocated row is one register block's decoded extent in `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/py/sep_reg.py` (an RDL array spans all its elements at the array stride). Every span between those blocks is owned by no RDL block and is a reserved row. The OTP anchor is the first byte above the `EFUSE_MMR` block.
. Drop the named excluded spans from the probe set: the CPU TCM aperture, the external chiplet and SMU apertures (TB-terminated), the SEP External window (adopter-defined) and the system-bus reserved span (graded by `sep_unmapped_access_policy_test`). The crypto (`0x1091_4000`), Key Manager (`0x1092_1000`) and OTP reserved gaps are walked, each with a directed anchor.
. Read and write the surviving anchors and the seed-selected reserved addresses, and report the per-row shortfall where a row yielded fewer probes than its quota.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-MAP-REFUSE
|Every probed address is refused. This checker grades refusal only, so DECERR and SLVERR both satisfy it and the line reports the split. OKAY, EXOKAY or a timeout fails the probe; the code `sep.rdl` states is graded by `sep_unmapped_access_policy_test` at the points that test probes. The directed anchors include the exact first byte above the generated boot-ROM extent; remaining probes come from map-reserved addresses this test asserts. Excluded spans are skipped before the walk with a counted reason.
|CHK-MAP-REFUSE-DATA
|A refused read returns none of the live words sampled on the same bus over the ordinary path: the live-bus control (`SEP_NMI_VEC`), `SW_RESET_N` (the block below the `0x10803008` anchor) and boot-ROM word 0 (the block below the one-past-ROM anchor). A refused access never reaches a unit (`hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc`), so a refusal that hands back one of these values has reached its owner. Zero words are dropped from the set because they cannot discriminate. At least one refused read must be compared. Other live words are not claimed.
|CHK-NONVAC
|The DECERR beats the monitor observed on the bus equal the count the master reported, so the refusals are evidence from the pins and not only from the driver.
|CHK-OKAY
|A known-mapped CSR on the same bus returns OKAY and its generated reset value, read before any refusal probe. Without it a wedged or dead bus would refuse everything and read as a clean pass. The address and the expected value come from the generated SystemRDL export, and the register's reset is non-zero so the compare cannot pass against a tied-off decode.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The probe and anchor counts for the seed are logged, so a shrunken walk is visible.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_unmapped_access_policy_test]]
===== 2.24 `sep_unmapped_access_policy_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_unmapped_access_policy_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — every address is derived from the generated register export and walked on every run.

*Objective*: The SEP components view of the SystemRDL memory map (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/py/sep_memory_map.py`, generated from `hw/sys/sep/regs/sep.rdl` by `tools/regs/rdlmap.py`, the same model that renders `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`) gives each unit's Decoded Extent and states how a 32-bit access that no register backs answers, as seen at the SEP inbound port: in a hole inside the extent, past the extent, and in a reserved row between apertures. Each response gives the read response, the RDL read data and the write response. `doc/trm/src/memory_map.adoc` (Decode Response Codes) defines the cells. This leaf holds the DUT to that table at the points that sit next to live registers and that `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` and `sep_axi_map_refuse_test` do not probe. `env/sep_decode_resp.py` imports that view, so no expected code is typed into the test. `rdata` is a 64-bit field in `sep_memory_map.rdl`: its low word answers a read with address bit 2 clear and its upper word a read with address bit 2 set. A 32-bit RDL rdata (no upper word) is the word a 32-bit read returns on either half of the bus (`ocah_resp.rdata` in `hw/common/regs/regblock_udps.rdl`; Decode Response Codes in `doc/trm/src/memory_map.adoc`). The expected codes and read data are the `ocah_resp` values `sep_memory_map.rdl` states. `sep_memory_map.rdl` is the specification of record for these codes, so `CHK-UNMAPPED-CODE` holds the DUT to the specification. Where the map and the RDL allocation of an array disagree, `CHK-ARRAY-TAIL` grades against the RDL allocation.

*Test Coverage*: System-CSR holes, the reserved row above SEP CPU control, the mailbox page's upper half, the space past each eFuse sibling block and past the SPI host, the end of the eFuse token MMR extent, and every remap and filter array slot tail answer the response code and read data the map states, and none of them aliases a live register. Also the holes inside the mailbox extent (the first word past outbound mailbox 0 and the last word before inbound mailbox 0) and inside the SEP CPU control extent (the first word past `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_CLEAR` and the last word before `SEP_VERSION_ID`), both bus lanes per unit, each read, written and read again. Functional coverage: `sep_aperture_reserved_access_cg.cp_row_cell` (cells of the LSU-graded rows) and `cp_array_tail`, owner window 755. The window opens before the probe walk only when nothing failed before it, closes at the first access that records a failure, and closes after the tail walk, before the closing re-read and the restore. The sampler holds the row and array-tail cells of each access and samples them only on the pass label `row_pass` (value 1), which the leaf writes after every per-access check of that probe or tail access (CHK-UNMAPPED-CODE, the group refusal, CHK-ARRAY-TAIL with its re-read, CHK-UNMAPPED-NO-ALIAS of that access) recorded no failure. The leaf drops the held cells (value 2) before each access and at the first failure.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program a distinct non-zero value into one live word of every array slot, the first and last AP / STEE output-remap entries, `SEP_NMI_VEC`, three mailbox thresholds, both eFuse timeouts, two eFuse token inputs and the SPI host `CSID`, and read each back. `CSID` takes effect only when a command is issued, and this leaf issues none. Each programmed word is read before its write, for the restore, except a word with a field that has no RDL reset. The two eFuse token inputs are such words: `hw/ip/efuse/regs/efuse_mmr.rdl` gives the `token` field of `RMA_TOKEN_I` and `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_I` no reset value, so the register has no value before its first write. The test reads this from the generated IP-XACT (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/ipxact/sep.xml`, no `resets` element on the field), not from the generated `_REG_DEFAULT`, which reads 0 for a field without a reset. These words are written before any read of them. Snapshot every other word of those slots, every mailbox `STATUS`, `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` and the SPI host `CONFIGOPTS`.
. Read `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` through its generated symbol.
. Read and write each probe with 32-bit singles. After each write, re-read every programmed word and the non-array snapshot words. For system-CSR and reserved-row probes, record whether the access reached the system-CSR AXI-Lite port and whether it arrived at the local crossbar's `ext` initiator. Before the probes, write and read the first address of the External row of the SEP CPU logical map (`0x0000_0000`) as the control for the `ext` initiator watcher.
. Read and write each array slot tail and the first word past each filter array, and after each write re-read the words of that slot and of the next slot. After each write to a tail inside the array extent, read that tail again.
. Re-read every live word, then restore the programmed words that were read before their write. The eFuse token inputs are not restored.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-UNMAPPED-LIVE
|Every programmed word reads back its value with OKAY, and every snapshot word reads OKAY. A word with a field that has no RDL reset is written before its first read; the config does not build if a snapshot-only word has such a field. This is the positive control: it shows the bus reaches every block that owns a probed hole, and the distinct values are what make an alias visible.
|CHK-SYSCSR-HOLE-REFUSE
|Every read and write in the system-CSR holes is refused: not OKAY, and not a timeout. For this checker and the five below it, the config does not build unless the map states a refusal at every probe in the group.
|CHK-SYS-RESERVED-REFUSE
|Every read and write in the reserved row above SEP CPU control is refused, the `0x10A5_0000` page included.
|CHK-MBOX-UPPER-REFUSE
|Every read and write in the upper half of the mailbox page is refused.
|CHK-EFUSE-CTRL-PAST-REFUSE
|Every read and write past the `efuse_interface_ctrl` extent is refused.
|CHK-EFUSE-MMR-PAST-REFUSE
|Every read and write past the `efuse_mmr` extent is refused.
|CHK-SPI-PAST-REFUSE
|Every read and write past the `spi_controller` extent, to the end of its aperture, is refused.
|CHK-SYSCSR-HOLE-DECODE
|No system-CSR hole or reserved-row probe reaches the system-CSR AXI-Lite port: the system-peripherals crossbar refuses each one before the AXI-to-AXI-Lite converter, which answers every write error behind it with SLVERR. At least one probe must be watched. A watched write and read of the programmed `SEP_NMI_VEC` word must each show their handshake at that port, so a watcher that sees nothing fails.
|CHK-SYS-RESERVED-NO-LOOPBACK
|No system-CSR hole or reserved-row probe arrives at the local crossbar's `ext` initiator, the input on which `sep_system_peripherals` forwards an address that its peripheral crossbar does not decode (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`). Each probe is refused at its first decode, by the local crossbar or the system-peripherals crossbar, and is not sent back into the local crossbar. At least one probe must be watched. A watched write and read of `0x0000_0000`, the first address of the External row, which the local crossbar sends to `sep_system_peripherals` and the peripheral crossbar returns on that initiator, must each show their handshake there, so a watcher that sees nothing fails. Their response is not graded.
|CHK-TOKEN-FAULT-EXTENT
|`TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT`, the last register `hw/ip/efuse/regs/efuse_mmr.rdl` places in the token MMR (`@0x70`), reads OKAY with its RDL reset value, and the first word after it (`EFUSE_MMR` base + `REG_MAP_SIZE`) is refused. The extent therefore ends at `0x1093_0573`, where the RDL ends it. That the register holds live fault state is graded by `sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test`, which reads a raised fault bit through the same address.
|CHK-ARRAY-TAIL
|The array extent is the array base + `SEP_TOP_<ARRAY>_TOTAL_SIZE`, the RDL allocation. Every tail inside it, the last slot's tail included, answers OKAY, and a read of it returns zero. A re-read of such a tail after its write answers OKAY with zero, so the write was discarded. The first word past the extent is refused where no other RDL block owns it. The config does not build unless the array's map row spans exactly that extent and the map states a refusal for every word past it. A tail inside the extent where the map states anything other than OKAY with zero is a map disagreement: it is graded here only and logged as `INFO-UNMAPPED-MAP-DISAGREE`. A tail write never changes a word of its own slot or of the next slot. The walk must grade at least one word of each kind. A failure names the tail address, the response and the extent.
|CHK-UNMAPPED-CODE
|Every probe and tail access that completes, except the map-disagree tail words, answers the response code the map states for its address and channel, and every read returns the stated read data: the low word of the RDL rdata, or its upper word when address bit 2 is set. The codes and read data are the `ocah_resp` values `sep_memory_map.rdl` states. A 32-bit RDL rdata is graded as that word on both bus halves. Past `efuse_interface_ctrl` and `spi_controller`, reads cover both 32-bit bus lanes.
