Every refusal read is issued inside the monitor's error-read-data window, so an X or Z in an error payload fails the run instead of reading as zero; every error-response read must be lane-checked. A failure names the address, the response and read data seen, and the map row, column and cell. The walk must grade at least one access. The golden is the generated memory map (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`), whose codes were assigned from the RTL: the token span past the 116-byte `efuse_mmr` extent at `:64` (DECERR, 0xBADCAB1E; `otp_fuse_controller.adoc:45-46` states SLVERR for that span, an open specification question, so the leaf also logs the code and read data of each access of that span as `OBS-EFUSE-TOKEN-PAST-CODE`), the mailbox holes in extent at `:69` (SLVERR, 0x0) and the SEP CPU control holes in extent at `:79` (OKAY, 0x0). The second read of an in-extent hole after its write must return the same value, so the write was discarded. All 12 in-extent hole accesses must complete and be graded.
|CHK-UNMAPPED-NO-ALIAS
|No refused read returns the value of a programmed word. No probe write changes a watched live word. A closing re-read of every live word matches the snapshot.
|CHK-UNMAPPED-PINS
|The DECERR beats the bus monitor saw equal the DECERR responses the master reported, and the run reported at least one.
|===

The `INFO-UNMAPPED-CODE` lines tally the codes `CHK-UNMAPPED-CODE` graded, per group and channel, and count the system-CSR and reserved-row probes that reached the system-CSR AXI-Lite port, which `CHK-SYSCSR-HOLE-DECODE` requires to be none.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. `UVM_ERROR` is 0.

*Scope*:

* System-CSR holes: the gaps between the AP and STEE output-remap arrays, after the STEE array, between the outbound and inbound filter arrays, before SEP CPU control, past the SEP CPU control extent, one SEP CPU control image up, past the cold scratch bank, before and past the warm scratch bank, and the last word below one scratch-pair image up. The first and last `SCRATCH` word of each bank are programmed live words.
* Reserved row: the generated map row from the end of the SEP CPU control aperture to the external IO bridge, first and last word of each 64 KiB page. The row has two refusal points. The system-peripherals crossbar refuses the `0x10A4_0000` page itself, because its system-CSR rules cover only the register blocks' extents, so the access never reaches the system-CSR AXI-Lite port. The local crossbar refuses `0x10A5_0000` and above at its own decode: its system-peripherals CSR window ends where the system-peripherals crossbar's system-CSR window ends. An address that the local crossbar sent into that window and that the peripheral crossbar does not decode would return to the local crossbar on its `ext` initiator (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`), so no reserved-row probe may arrive there.
* Mailbox upper half: from `AXIL_MAILBOX` base plus its size rounded up to a power of two (`0x10A0_8000`) to the end of the 64 KiB mailbox aperture. All of it is past the mailbox's Decoded Extent. The probes include the images of outbound mailbox 0 `WRITE_DATA`, `WIRQT`, `RIRQT` and `STATUS` and of the last inbound mailbox `WIRQT`.
* eFuse: past `efuse_interface_ctrl` (28 B extent) and past `efuse_mmr` (116 B extent), each to the end of its 256 B window, including the image of a live register one extent up and half a window up. Past `efuse_interface_ctrl`, reads cover both 32-bit lanes of the 64-bit bus (address bit 2 set and clear).
* SPI host: past `spi_controller` (56 B extent) to the end of its 1 MiB aperture: the first two words past the extent (both bus lanes), the `CSID` image one extent up, the `CONFIGOPTS` image half an aperture up, and the last word of the aperture.
* Array tails: every word between the end of a slot's registers and the next slot, for the 16 alias-remap, 32 outbound-filter and 16 inbound-filter slots. The array extent is the array base plus `SEP_TOP_<ARRAY>_TOTAL_SIZE` from the generated `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/c/sep_addr.h` (`0x200`, `0x400` and `0x200`): the RDL allocates the full slot stride to every slot, the last one included. That size must equal the aperture of the array's map row. Every tail inside the extent, the last slot's tail included, must read zero and discard a write with OKAY. `sep_memory_map.rdl` sets `ocah_full_stride_extent` on these arrays, so the map row's Decoded Extent is the full allocation and the map states OKAY with zero for every tail, the last one included. The first word past the outbound and inbound filter apertures (`0x10A2_0400`, `0x10A2_1200`) is in a reserved row and must be refused. The first word past the alias-remap array is `AP_OUTPUT_REMAP_CTRL_WRAP_OUTPUT_REMAP[0]`, a live word, so it is not a refusal point.
* The Key Manager's own view of the token MMR (`0x0001_1570`) needs KM firmware and is not probed here.

'''''

[[sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap]]
==== 2. Fabric and remap, Phase 3

This bucket grades the fabric and remap contracts that the specification states. Each test is written from the specification and the RDL. A test extends an open test only with the cells that the open test does not grade, and the Test Coverage field of the entry names the open test. Legs that share a stimulus and a run mode are legs of one test.

Initiators:

* LSU is the CPU load/store splice on `s_axi`. It does not pass the inbound filter.
* SI is the SMN-inbound master on `m_axi`. It passes the inbound filter.
* DMA and IFU exist only in the `cpu` run mode.

Probes and hook. A probe only reads. Every probe or hook has one status:

* approved: the probe exists in `tb/tb_top.sv` and `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` lists it.
* observation tap: the probe is not in the closed set of probes that `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` holds, and the test adds it to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. A check on an observation tap is graded.
* added hook: a top-port drive or an error inject that `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` does not list (<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>). The test adds a top-port drive to the port table of the HDL Top section of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`. An added hook blocks no check, and a check that needs it is graded.

The frontdoor anchors are approved: the LSU and SI responses and read data, the read-back of a register or an SRAM word, and the DMA status registers.

[cols="1,4,3",options="header"]
|===
|Probe |Watches |Status
|PR-CSR |The AW and AR handshakes and the low 32 address bits at the system-CSR AXI-Lite port. |approved: `sys_csr_axil_*_o`; AXI-Lite split handshakes
|PR-XEXT |The AW and AR handshakes and the low 32 address bits at the `ext` initiator of the local crossbar. It carries no data, ID or AxUSER. |approved: `xbar_ext_in_*_o`
|PR-OUT |Every AW and AR handshake on `smn_outbound_axi_req_o` (address, ID, AxUSER, AxCACHE, AxPROT, AxLEN, AxBURST) and every W beat. |observation tap: the net `smn_outbound_req_w` exists in `tb_top` and no port exports it
|PR-EXT |`axi_extension_axi_req_o`: address, AxSIZE, AxLEN, strobe and data. |observation tap
|PR-SRAM |The request stream inside `u_sram_memory_interface`, ahead of `sep_sram_req_o`. |observation tap
|PR-ROM |The request stream inside `u_boot_rom_memory_interface`, ahead of `sep_boot_rom_req_o`. |observation tap
|PR-SMC |`sep_ext_to_smc_axi_req_o`. |observation tap
|PR-DMACSR |The request at the DMA CSR target port. |observation tap
|PR-ALIAS |The request at the input and at the output of the local-master alias remap: address and AxCACHE. |observation tap
|PR-EXT-RSP |The B and R reply of the AXI Extension port: response, ID and, on R, RLAST and data. |observation tap
|PR-TRNG |The request net of the TRNG AXI-Lite passthrough port (`u_dut.ext_trng_axil_req`): AW and AR address and AxPROT, W data and strobe, with READY from the response net. |observation tap
|PR-TRNG-RSP |The B and R reply of the TRNG AXI-Lite passthrough port (`u_dut.ext_trng_axil_resp`): response and, on R, data. |observation tap
|PR-INFLT |The AW and AR address out of the inbound filter (`smn_inbound_filtered_axi_req`), before the global-to-local remap. |observation tap
|PR-CPU |The AxPROT of the raw CPU LSU (AR, AW) and IFU (AR) requests in `u_sep_cpu`, before any remap. |observation tap
|PR-DMA-RAW |The AxPROT of the raw secure DMA master request (`u_sep_dma_wrap.dma_axi_req_raw`), AR and AW. |observation tap
|BH-SMC |The drive of `smc_global_base_addr_i` and `smc_region_size_i`, from plusargs or from the leaf before reset release, and an SMC responder on `sep_ext_to_smc_axi`, in the `lsu_stub_all_live` target of `sep_fabric_smc_route_test` and in the `cpu` run mode of `sep_fabric_dma_endpoint_matrix_test`. The default bench ties both inputs to 0 and the SMC responder exists in the `rom_boot` target only. |added hook: top-port drive of `smc_global_base_addr_i`, `smc_region_size_i` and `sep_ext_to_smc_axi_resp_i`
|===

No test of this bucket injects a fault. The CPU-LSU splice is the one stimulus exception that the tests use. It is approved.

Bring-up modes:

* Real PROD fuse sense: the inbound filter is active.
* Real PROD fuse sense, then one DEMOTE_1 write: the inbound filter is bypassed.
* `+skip_fuse_sense`: used only for outbound and decode legs.

Every leaf reads FEAT_CTRL and logs it, so the log names the leg.

Shared rules:

* AxSIZE is the AXI size encoding: 0 is 1 byte, 1 is 2 bytes, 2 is 4 bytes and 3 is 8 bytes on the 64-bit bus. An access is aligned to its size and never crosses the end of its window.
* A read of a 32-bit register at AxSIZE 3 compares bits [31:0] only. Bits [63:32] of a `SCRATCH` word and of `SEP_SW_DEBUG` are reserved, and the specification states no read value for reserved bits, so the leaf logs them and does not grade them.
* Every graded register compare follows the shared reserved-bit rule (<<sep-vplan-reserved-bits>>): it uses the RDL field bits of the register only, and the bits that the RDL marks Reserved with no reset value are masked out of the compare and logged in the same line.
* A never check pairs with a control in the same leaf. In the control a healthy design drives the same probe or answers the same response. A leaf whose control stays silent fails.
* A deny cell pairs with an allow cell that differs in one attribute. A dead bus cannot pass the pair.
* The filter reference model is written from the `axi_filter` document (`hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc`, "Instances", "Match, Then Permit" and "Address Range Granule") and from `hw/sys/smc/doc/assets/smc-traffic-filters.svg`. It never reads the RTL.
* The source ID that the filter compares with `src_id` is AxUSER[3:0] (`smc-traffic-filters.svg`, description of `axi_filter_wrap`: `src_id` is 0 or equals the source ID in AxUSER[3:0]). The filter document states that the decode is the same in every instance. OTHERS is source ID 0. Outside the source-ID legs every entry has `src_id` 0 and every probe carries AxUSER 0, so the source ID does not decide a verdict.
* Approved exception "Source ID carrier": the figure `hw/sys/smc/doc/assets/smc-traffic-filters.svg` (TRM doc tree) is the accepted source for "the filter compares `src_id` with AxUSER[3:0]" until a TRM sentence states it. The record, with its scope, the revisions it was approved against and its review and expiry trigger, is the stand-in entry "Source ID carrier" in `hw/sys/sep/dv/docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` (`sep-standin-src-id-carrier`). The source-ID cells of `sep_fabric_filter_match_priority_rand_test` are graded on it.
* Verdict anchor. The filter error subordinate answers DECERR to a refused request. An admitted outbound request reaches the bench responder, which answers OKAY, and the OKAY answer of the paired allow cell is the control. The response code is therefore the outbound verdict and needs no PR-OUT.
* Randomized stimulus is a pure function of the seed. `--stage sim --seed N` replays a leaf. A per-seed value is not evidence.
* A read-back of each programmed filter register sits between its write and the first dependent transaction, because the specification states no ordering between a CSR write and the next transaction.
* Every check logs `CHK-<NAME> PASS: ...` or `CHK-<NAME> FAIL: ...` with the values it compared. A leaf fails when a named check line is missing from `results.xml`.
* A `no_cpu` leaf runs on Verilator on target `lsu_stub_all_live` and on VCS on `--target default` (the shared Phase 3 testlist-entry rule), group `cpu_stub` and `testlists/system.toml`. A `cpu` leaf uses a firmware image, group `cpu` and `testlists/cpu.toml`.
* Tool "Both" means Verilator and VCS. Covergroup figures are graded on VCS.

[[sep_fabric_inbound_rebase_test]]
===== 2.25 `sep_fabric_inbound_rebase_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (Verilator on `lsu_stub_all_live`, VCS on `--target default` per the shared Phase 3 testlist-entry rule, group `cpu_stub`, `testlists/system.toml`), real PROD fuse sense (no `+skip_fuse_sense`) with a pinned image: PROD, SEC_DIS 0, and fixed `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` values whose DBG_1 bit 0 is clear in both, 3 seeds. Tool: Verilator and VCS.

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_inbound_rebase_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (LSU master on `s_axi`, SI master on `m_axi`); `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (outbound responder: OKAY and zero read data for any address), used by the unit leg only. Probes: PR-XEXT (approved: rebased request of an SRAM access), PR-CSR (approved: rebased request of a scratch access), the SI and LSU responses and read data, the LSU read-back (approved). PR-SRAM (an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list) only supplements the LSU read-back, and no check needs it. The rebase model is written from `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` and never reads the RTL. No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, RAND-REP over a directed configuration. Three fixed (base, size) pairs: base `0x40_0000_0000` with sizes `0x2000_0000` (A), `0x1000_0000` (B) and `0x8000_0000` (C), each a power of two and a 4 KiB multiple. The seed samples the SRAM read offset `o` and the SRAM write offset `o2` (8-byte aligned, inside `0x0` to `0x3_FFF8`, `o2` different from `o`) and the cold scratch word offset `s` (8-byte aligned, inside the 64-byte bank). `reseed = 3` samples different offsets. The pairs, the cells and their order are the same on every seed. The test makes no claim about other base or size values, because the specification states no legal values.

*Objective*: An inbound request meets the inbound filter first on the chiplet-global address. The global-to-local remap then rebases the `SEP_REGION_SIZE`-byte window at `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` to address 0 and passes every other address unchanged. The peripheral crossbar forwards a local address below `0x4000_0000` to the local crossbar and answers DECERR at or above `0x4000_0000`. The inbound port has no connection to the Boot ROM, Reset Control, ICCM, DCCM, PIC and the AP and STEE regions, and answers DECERR for them at any aperture. Cites `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` ("Fabric Topology", input fabric; "Address Remapping"), `hw/sys/sep/doc/assets/sep-input-fabric.svg`, `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc` (`SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR`, `SEP_REGION_SIZE`), `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` ("Blocked Transactions") `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` ("Units Unreachable from the Inbound Port"), `hw/sys/sep/doc/assets/sep_axi_connectivity.svg` and `hw/sys/sep/doc/lifecycle_controller.adoc` ("Per-LC-state Feature Control Profile": the PROD state with no demotion keeps the inbound filter active).

*Test Coverage*: In-window SRAM read, SRAM write and scratch read behind entries over the global ranges; the filter-first corner; the bottom word, the top word and the last byte of the window, with the first address above and the first address below the window; the crossbar limit at `0x4000_0000` with the word below it; a local address at `0x4000_0100` and the CPU Resources addresses (ICCM, DCCM, PIC and the two Reserved rows) that the filter admits; and the first word of the Boot ROM, Reset Control, AP region and STEE region at the two rebased apertures A and C. Extends `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`, which grades the per-entry inbound allow and block cells, `sep_address_map_test` and `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`, which grade the storage of the two aperture registers, and `sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test` (CHK-DBG-UNREACHABLE), which grades the first and last word of the six unreachable units at the reset aperture with the inbound filter bypassed. Only the new cells are graded here: the rebase, the filter-first order, the window edges on both sides, the crossbar limit, the four in-window units at the rebased apertures and the CPU Resources addresses behind an active filter. The open tests keep the rest. This test programs the aperture away from its reset values; `sep_fabric_filter_match_priority_rand_test` and `sep_fabric_row_response_matrix_test` run at the reset aperture. Functional coverage: `sep_fabric_inbound_aperture_cg` (`cp_in_ap_class`, `cp_out_ap_class`, `cp_translation`, `cp_rebased_limit`, `cp_dest_class_verdict` for the loopback admitted cells and the unreachable-unit cells), `sep_fabric_port_silence_cp.sil_inbound_refused` (cause `rebase_ge_0x4000_0000`), existing `sep_inbound_filter_allow_cg`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up with real PROD fuse sense of the pinned image, so the inbound filter is active. The LSU reads `FEAT_CTRL` (64 bits) and compares it with the golden decode that the model computes from the profile table of `lifecycle_controller.adoc` for that image. The golden holds the SEP_DBG state that keeps the filter active. A mismatch prints `CTRL-REBASE-FILTER-ACTIVE FAIL` and stops the leaf.
. Read `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` and `SEP_REGION_SIZE` and log the reset values.
. Draw `o`, `o2` and `s`. The LSU stages a non-zero marker `M1` at SRAM word `0x1000_0000` + `o`, a non-zero marker `M3` at SRAM word `0x1000_0000` + `o2` and a non-zero marker `M2` at cold scratch word `0x1080_2000` + `s`. The scratch register holds 32 data bits (bits [63:32] are reserved, and the specification states no read value for them), so `M2` fits in bits [31:0] and every compare of a scratch read uses bits [31:0]. The three markers and the write data `W` of step 8 are pairwise different. The LSU reads each staged word back.
. Configuration A. The LSU writes `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` = `0x40_0000_0000` and `SEP_REGION_SIZE` = `0x2000_0000`, then reads both back. `G` is the base.
. The LSU programs inbound entry 0 over the global range `G` + `0x1000_0000` to `G` + `0x1003_FFFF` and inbound entry 1 over `G` + `0x1080_2000` to `G` + `0x1080_203F`. Both entries are enabled and allow read and write, with `allow_burst` 0, `src_id` 0 and `allow_ns` equal to `prot[1]` of the SI probe. The LSU reads back every programmed filter register.
. SI reads 8 bytes at `G` + `0x1000_0000` + `o`. The response is OKAY and the data is `M1`. PR-XEXT shows the AR address `0x1000_0000` + `o`.
. SI reads 8 bytes at `G` + `0x1080_2000` + `s`. The response is OKAY and bits [31:0] of the data are `M2`. Bits [63:32] are logged and not graded. PR-CSR shows the AR address `0x1080_2000` + `s`.
. SI writes `W` as 8 bytes at `G` + `0x1000_0000` + `o2`. The response is OKAY. PR-XEXT shows the AW address `0x1000_0000` + `o2`.
. The LSU reads the word `0x1000_0000` + `o2` at its direct address and compares it with `W`.
. Unreachable units, configuration A. The LSU programs inbound entry 3 over `G` + `0x1000_0000` to `G` + `0x11FF_FFFF` (enabled, read and write allowed, `allow_burst` 0, `src_id` 0, `allow_ns` equal to the SI `prot[1]`) and one outbound entry pair over `0x1100_0000` to `0x11FF_FFFF` (one entry with `allow_ns` 0 and one with `allow_ns` 1, enabled, read and write allowed, `src_id` 0), so a request that reached the output fabric would be admitted and answered OKAY by the bench responder. The LSU reads every programmed register back. The LSU reads `SW_RESET_N` (`0x1080_3000`) and the first Boot ROM word (`0x1004_0000`) at their direct addresses.
. SI reads 8 bytes at `G` + `0x1000_0000` + `o`. The response is OKAY and the data is `M1`. This read is the control of the unit cells: the same entry admits a live unit.
. For each unit U of the Boot ROM (`0x1004_0000`), Reset Control (`0x1080_3000`), AP region (`0x1100_0000`) and STEE region (`0x1180_0000`): error injection at `smn_inbound_axi_req_i`: SI reads 4 bytes (AxSIZE 2) at `G` + U and SI writes 4 bytes at `G` + U. The write data is the value that the LSU read at U for the Boot ROM and Reset Control, so a unit that a faulty fabric connected keeps its value, and a seeded non-zero word for the AP and STEE regions. The expected response is DECERR on RRESP and on BRESP. The PR-XEXT handshake is logged and not graded, because the crossbar forwards these addresses to the local crossbar, which refuses them.
. The LSU disables inbound entry 3 and the two outbound entries and reads them back.
. Filter-first corner. The LSU disables entries 0 and 1, programs inbound entry 2 over the local range `0x1000_0000` to `0x1003_FFFF` (enabled, read and write allowed, `allow_burst` 0, `src_id` 0, `allow_ns` equal to the SI `prot[1]`) and reads entry 2 back.
. Error injection at `smn_inbound_axi_req_i`: SI reads `G` + `0x1000_0000` + `o`. No enabled entry covers the global address. The expected response is DECERR on RRESP, and PR-XEXT shows no handshake.
. SI reads the plain address `0x1000_0000` + `o`. It lies below `G`, so it passes the remap unchanged and the entry 2 range covers it. The response is OKAY and the data is `M1`.
. The LSU disables entry 2.
. Configuration B. The LSU writes `SEP_REGION_SIZE` = `0x1000_0000` and reads it back. The LSU programs entry 0 over `G` - `0x10` to `G` + `0xF` and entry 1 over `G` + `0x0FFF_FFF0` to `G` + `0x1000_000F`. Both are enabled with read and write allowed, `allow_burst` 0, `src_id` 0 and `allow_ns` equal to the SI `prot[1]`. The LSU reads the entries back.
. SI reads 8 bytes at `G` (bottom word). PR-XEXT shows the AR address 0. The response is logged and is not graded, because the specification states no response for this local address. The leaf waits for the response in a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-REBASE-EDGE.
. SI reads 8 bytes at `G` + `0x0FFF_FFF8` (top word). PR-XEXT shows the AR address `0x0FFF_FFF8`. The response is logged and is not graded. Same bounded wait.
. SI reads 1 byte (AxSIZE 0) at `G` + `0x0FFF_FFFF` (last byte). PR-XEXT shows the AR address `0x0FFF_FFFF`. The response is logged and is not graded. Same bounded wait.
. Error injection at `smn_inbound_axi_req_i`: SI reads 1 byte at `G` + `0x1000_0000` (first address above the window). Both entries admit it, the remap passes it unchanged, and the address is at or above `0x4000_0000`. The expected response is DECERR on RRESP, and PR-XEXT shows no handshake.
. Error injection at `smn_inbound_axi_req_i`: SI reads 1 byte at `G` - 1 (first address below the window). The expected response is DECERR on RRESP, and PR-XEXT shows no handshake.
. Configuration C. The LSU disables entries 0 and 1, writes `SEP_REGION_SIZE` = `0x8000_0000`, reads it back, programs entry 0 over `G` + `0x3FFF_FFF0` to `G` + `0x4000_00FF` (enabled, read and write allowed, `allow_burst` 0, `src_id` 0, `allow_ns` equal to the SI `prot[1]`) and reads entry 0 back.
. Unreachable units, configuration C. Repeat steps 10 to 13. The window ends at `G` + `0x7FFF_FFFF`, so the four units and the SRAM control word rebase to their local addresses.
. SI reads 8 bytes at `G` + `0x3FFF_FFF8`. It rebases to `0x3FFF_FFF8`. PR-XEXT shows the AR address `0x3FFF_FFF8`. The response is logged and is not graded. The leaf waits for the response in a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-REBASE-XBAR-LIMIT. The next step starts after the response.
. Error injection at `smn_inbound_axi_req_i`: SI reads 8 bytes at `G` + `0x4000_0000`. It rebases to `0x4000_0000`. The expected response is DECERR on RRESP, and PR-XEXT and PR-CSR show no handshake.
. Above-limit corner. The LSU disables entry 0, programs entry 0 over `0x4000_0100` to `0x4000_01FF` and entry 1 over `0xC000_0000` to `0xCFFF_FFFF` (same attributes) and reads both back. The window of configuration C includes none of these addresses, so the remap passes them unchanged.
. Error injection at `smn_inbound_axi_req_i`: SI reads 8 bytes at `0x4000_0100`. The filter admits it, and the local address is at or above `0x4000_0000`. The expected response is DECERR on RRESP, and PR-XEXT shows no handshake.
. Error injection at `smn_inbound_axi_req_i`: SI reads 4 bytes and writes 4 bytes at each CPU Resources address: the ICCM base `0xC000_0000`, the DCCM base `0xC004_0000`, the Reserved row `0xC006_0000`, the PIC base `0xC008_0000` and the Reserved row `0xC008_8000`. The filter admits them, and each local address is at or above `0x4000_0000`. The expected response is DECERR on RRESP and on BRESP, and PR-XEXT and PR-CSR show no handshake.
. The LSU disables entries 0 and 1, writes `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` = `0x0` and `SEP_REGION_SIZE` = `0x0100_0000`, reads both back and logs them.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CTRL-REBASE-FILTER-ACTIVE
|(Precondition of every check of this leaf. It is not a counted check.) The bring-up image is PROD with SEC_DIS 0 and no demotion, so `FEAT_CTRL` equals the golden decode of the profile table and the SEP_DBG state keeps the inbound filter active (`lifecycle_controller.adoc`, "Per-LC-state Feature Control Profile"). `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test` and `sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test` (CHK-PROD-FEAT) grade this value, so this leaf uses the compare only to stop on a wrong bring-up. A mismatch prints `CTRL-REBASE-FILTER-ACTIVE FAIL: feat_ctrl=0x<v> golden=0x<g>` and stops the leaf, so the named checks of the leaf are missing and the leaf fails. Anchor: the LSU read of `FEAT_CTRL` on the control bus. Evidence: `CTRL-REBASE-FILTER-ACTIVE LOG: feat_ctrl=0x<v> golden=0x<g> sep_dbg=<n>`.
|CHK-REBASE
|A request at `G` + n inside the window reaches the local fabric at address n (`fabric.adoc`, "Fabric Topology"; `sep-input-fabric.svg`). In configuration A the model computes n from the programmed base and size only. The SRAM read returns the marker `M1` that the LSU staged, so the data is a real readback of the staged word, and PR-XEXT shows the AR address `0x1000_0000` + `o`. The scratch read returns `M2` in bits [31:0] (bits [63:32] are logged and not graded), and PR-CSR shows `0x1080_2000` + `s`. The SRAM write shows the AW address `0x1000_0000` + `o2` on PR-XEXT, and the LSU direct read of that word returns `W`, which differs from the staged `M3`. Every response is OKAY. A DUT with no rebase or a wrong base answers DECERR (the global address is at or above `0x4000_0000`) or reaches another word and shows another address. Anchor: the SI read data, the LSU read-back, PR-XEXT and PR-CSR, all approved. PR-SRAM, an observation tap, may supplement them, and no verdict depends on it. Control: the three legs each drive their own probe in the same leaf. Evidence: one line per leg, `CHK-REBASE PASS: leg=sram_rd\|sram_wr\|scratch_rd base=0x4000000000 size=0x20000000 global=0x<g> local_seen=0x<l> resp=OKAY rdata_lo=0x<r> staged=0x<m> wdata=0x<w>\|na lsu_direct=0x<d>\|na`.
|CHK-REBASE-FILTER-FIRST
|The inbound filter compares the global address before the remap (`fabric.adoc`, "Fabric Topology"). With only the local-range entry 2 enabled, the read of the global address answers DECERR and PR-XEXT shows no handshake, and the read of the plain address, which lies outside the window, answers OKAY with the staged marker `M1`. A filter that compares the rebased address admits the first read and fails the check. Anchor: the SI responses and read data, PR-XEXT (approved). Control (differential cell): the plain-address read answers OKAY with data and drives PR-XEXT. Evidence: `CHK-REBASE-FILTER-FIRST PASS: global_resp=DECERR plain_resp=OKAY plain_rdata=0x<m1> staged=0x<m1> xext_global=0 xext_plain=<n>`. Functional coverage: `sep_fabric_inbound_aperture_cg.cp_filter_order`; the codes of the two reads are held and sample only on the pass label `rebase_pass`, which the leaf raises inside the window after this check passed, before it closes the window.
|CHK-REBASE-EDGE
|For the bottom word, the top word and the last byte, the model computes the rebased address n from the programmed base and size. When n is below `0x4000_0000`, PR-XEXT shows n. Otherwise the response is DECERR and PR-XEXT shows no handshake. The global addresses `G` + size (first address above) and `G` - 1 (first address below) lie outside the window, pass the remap unchanged and are at or above `0x4000_0000`, so both answer DECERR with PR-XEXT silent. Entries 0 and 1 admit both words, and each shows exactly one PR-INFLT request at its issued global address, so the refusal comes from past the filter, not from the filter. The in-window responses are logged and not graded. A DUT with a window that is one byte too large or too small, or one that rebases the first address above the window, shows the wrong PR-XEXT address or a non-DECERR answer. Anchor: PR-XEXT and the SI responses (approved). Control: the in-window cells drive PR-XEXT in the same leaf, so a dead tap fails the check. Evidence: `CHK-REBASE-EDGE PASS: size=0x<s> bottom_seen=0x<n>\|none top_seen=0x<n>\|none last_byte_seen=0x<n>\|none control_seen=<n> above=[addr=0x<a> dir=R resp=DECERR xbar_ext_in=0 ...] below=[... resp=DECERR xbar_ext_in=0 csr_seen=<n> inflt=[0x<a>]]`.
|CHK-REBASE-XBAR-LIMIT
|The peripheral crossbar answers DECERR at or above `0x4000_0000` (`fabric.adoc`, "Fabric Topology"). In configuration C the read that rebases to `0x4000_0000` answers DECERR, and PR-XEXT and PR-CSR show no handshake. A DUT that forwards `0x4000_0000` to the local crossbar shows a PR-XEXT handshake. Anchor: the SI response, PR-XEXT and PR-CSR (approved). Control: the read that rebases to `0x3FFF_FFF8` shows on PR-XEXT. Evidence: `CHK-REBASE-XBAR-LIMIT PASS: below_seen=0x3FFFFFF8 local=0x40000000 [addr=0x<a> dir=R resp=DECERR xbar_ext_in=0 csr_seen=0 inflt=[0x<a>]]`.
|CHK-REBASE-ABOVE
|A local request at `0x4000_0100`, outside the window and admitted by the filter, answers DECERR and PR-XEXT shows no handshake. The same holds for a read and a write at each CPU Resources address of the step (`fabric.adoc`, "Fabric Topology": the crossbar answers DECERR at or above `0x4000_0000`; `memory_map.adoc`, "Units Unreachable from the Inbound Port"). A DUT that truncates the address to 32 bits and forwards it fails the check. Anchor: the SI response, PR-XEXT and PR-CSR (approved). Control: the `0x3FFF_FFF8` read of configuration C drives PR-XEXT in the same test. Evidence: `CHK-REBASE-ABOVE PASS: addr=0x<a> dir=R\|W resp=DECERR xbar_ext_in=0 csr_seen=0 control_seen=<n>`.
|CHK-REBASE-UNREACH
|A read and a write of the first word of the Boot ROM, Reset Control, AP region and STEE region at `G` + U, in configurations A and C, answer DECERR through an entry that admits them (`memory_map.adoc`, "Units Unreachable from the Inbound Port"; `sep_axi_connectivity.svg`: the System Interface has no path to these units). A DUT that connects the inbound port to one of these units answers OKAY, and for the AP and STEE regions the enabled outbound pair would admit a request that reached the output fabric. Anchor: the SI responses (approved). Control: the read of the staged SRAM word through the same entry answers OKAY with `M1` in each configuration, so the entry and the path are alive. Evidence: `CHK-REBASE-UNREACH PASS: cfg=A\|C unit=boot_rom\|reset_ctrl\|ap_region\|stee_region dir=R\|W global=0x<g> resp=DECERR control_resp=OKAY control_rdata=0x<m1>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The test does not grade the per-entry inbound allow and block cells, the storage of the two aperture registers, other base or size values, or the legal values of the two registers (the specification states none). Those cells stay with `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`, `sep_address_map_test` and `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`. The DECERR of the six unreachable units at the reset aperture and the last word of each unit stay with `sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test`. The responses of the in-window edge reads and of the read at `0x3FFF_FFF8` are logged only, because the specification states no response for those local addresses. The inbound filter bypass is not used: the filter is active on every request.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_filter_match_priority_rand_test]]
===== 2.26 `sep_fabric_filter_match_priority_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (Verilator on `lsu_stub_all_live`, VCS on `--target default` per the shared Phase 3 testlist-entry rule, group `cpu_stub`, `testlists/system.toml`), real PROD fuse sense (no `+skip_fuse_sense`) with a pinned image: PROD, SEC_DIS 0, and fixed `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` values whose DBG_1 bit 0 is clear in both, 4 seeds. Tool: Verilator and VCS.

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_filter_match_priority_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (LSU master on `s_axi`, SI master on `m_axi`); `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (outbound responder: OKAY and zero read data for any address); the filter reference model of this bucket, written from `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` and `hw/sys/smc/doc/assets/smc-traffic-filters.svg`, which reads only the programmed entry fields and the probe attributes and never the RTL. Probes. Approved: PR-XEXT and PR-CSR (an inbound refusal shows no handshake), the SI and LSU responses and read data, the LSU read-back of the SRAM and scratch words. PR-OUT, PR-EXT, PR-SRAM and PR-INFLT are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list: PR-OUT supplements the outbound DECERR verdict and shows the source ID that the SMU path sets, PR-EXT serves the extension-window probe of leg 3, PR-SRAM supplements the LSU read-back, and PR-INFLT shows the register-class reads of the wide-range control pass the inbound filter and carries the AxUSER the filter decoded for the group and upper-bits probes (CTRL-FILT-USER). No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, two classes. Legs 1, 2 and 4 are RANDCFG. Dimensions: entry count (3 to 6 per instance), entry indices (inbound 0 to 15, outbound 0 to 31), overlapping range placement, `entry_enabled`, `allow_ns`, `allow_burst`, `src_id` (0 to 15), AxUSER[3:0] of the probe (0 to 15), the two LSU AxUSER values of the SMU source-ID probes (12 bits, bits [11:4] non-zero, bits [3:0] non-zero and different), `read_allowed`, `write_allowed`, `group_id` (0 to 15), probe attributes, granule index, in-granule offsets of START and END, and the shape. Leg 3 is RAND-REP. Dimensions: probe address, direction, AxSIZE, probe `prot[1]` (0 or 1) and the control entry index; every destination class is probed in the directions that leg 3 lists for it, on every seed. The armed range of leg 3 is the directed value START_ADDR 0, END_ADDR 2^48-1, which covers every probed address and ends below 2^56-1. Legal range per `hw/ip/axi_filter/regs/gen/adoc/filter_ctrl.adoc` (`FILTER_CONFIG`, `START_ADDR`, `END_ADDR`). Rejection list: an inverted range; a range that touches 2^56-1; a range where START is in a higher granule than END; any outbound burst; any inbound entry that covers the filter-bank page or `SEP_CPU_CTRL` in legs 1, 2 and 4; the `locked` bit; any draw of the `SEP_DBG` bit; probe addresses of units that the inbound port does not reach (their DECERR comes from decode); in a leg 1 (entry state, attribute) cell, any other stack entry that matches the probe in the deny cell or in the allow cell (each other entry does not cover the probe address or fails a match term, so the covering entry alone decides both verdicts). In the granule leg a range is whole granules inside SRAM (inbound) or inside the SMU window above `0x8000_1000` (outbound, `src_id` 0). `reseed = 4` samples different stacks, values and probe attributes. The cell classes of each leg are the same on every seed. Leg 1 draws the destination class of each (entry state, attribute) cell from the classes of its instance, and every class gets at least one cell per instance on every seed. The cross of attribute and destination class is seed-dependent and has no bin. Leg 2 runs the source-ID values 1, 2, 4, 8 and 15 (each bit alone and all four bits) and the wildcard probes with AxUSER[3:0] 0 and 15 on every seed. It draws one more non-zero source ID from the other ten values and two more wildcard AxUSER[3:0] values from 1 to 14. No bin records a value (`cp_src_id_class` bins the classes), and no regression claims the full value sets. The other values come only over a merge of regressions.

*Objective*: A filter entry matches only when it is enabled, the address is in the widened range, `prot[1]` equals `allow_ns`, the source ID equals `src_id` or `src_id` is 0, and `allow_burst` is 1 or AxLEN is 0. The source ID is AxUSER[3:0]. On the outbound instance the filter checks the NS and the source ID that the path sets (`fabric.adoc`, `sep-outbound-attributes-table`), not those of the LSU, so the model takes the path values and every outbound entry the probe should match takes the NS of the probe at the filter. The lowest-numbered match wins and only its permission bit counts. A failed match falls through. A clear permission bit on the lowest match blocks. A transaction that no entry matches is blocked with DECERR. AW and AR decode independently. `group_id` is programmed with a random value; group ID matching is disabled on every instance, so it has no effect on a verdict (CHK-FILT-GROUP). After reset every entry is disabled, so unmatched traffic is blocked. Default deny rests on `entry_enabled`: an entry that holds a range, permission bits and an `allow_ns` equal to the probe `prot[1]` does not match while it is disabled. The granule is 4 KB with `allow_burst` 1 and 8 bytes with `allow_burst` 0. START_ADDR widens down and END_ADDR widens up, and a range that straddles a boundary admits both granules. The test grades the admitted set through the verdicts, and the START_ADDR and END_ADDR read-back after the entry write: the rounded values when both ends land in one granule, otherwise the programmed values (`filter_ctrl.adoc`, `START_ADDR`). Cites `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` ("Instances"; "Match, Then Permit"; "Address Range Granule"; "Blocked Transactions"), `hw/ip/axi_filter/regs/gen/adoc/filter_ctrl.adoc` (`FILTER_CONFIG`, `START_ADDR`, `END_ADDR`), `hw/sys/smc/doc/assets/smc-traffic-filters.svg` (the five match conditions of `axi_filter_wrap`, including `src_id` against AxUSER[3:0]) `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` ("Top Level AXI4"; "Filtering and Protection"; "Transaction Routing") and `hw/sys/sep/doc/lifecycle_controller.adoc` ("Per-LC-state Feature Control Profile": the PROD state with no demotion keeps the inbound filter active).

*Test Coverage*: Four legs that share one stimulus family (programmed entries and one probe per cell), one run mode and one tool.

* Leg 1, match and priority: entry state (enabled, disabled) by attribute (range, `prot[1]`, burst, read permission, write permission) on each instance; the corner cells fall-through (a), permission block (b), burst fall-through (c, inbound only), disabled lowest entry (d), read and write that choose different entries (e, inbound only: an outbound path sets NS from the address, so a read and a write of one address reach the outbound filter with the same NS) and no match (g); the destination classes SRAM and scratch inbound and the SMU window and the AP region outbound, each as a read and as a write.
* Leg 2, source ID: the non-zero values 1, 2, 4, 8 and 15 and one drawn non-zero value, the wildcard against AxUSER[3:0] 0 and 15 and two drawn values, the fall-through on a source-ID mismatch, an upper AxUSER bit, the source ID that the SMU path sets (read off PR-OUT for two distinct LSU AxUSER values), and the outbound exact and mismatch cells against that ID.
* Leg 3, default deny: the reset state, the armed-but-disabled state of all 48 entries and the enabled control entry per instance.
* Leg 4, granule: seven cells. Outbound 4 KB granule by the one, equal and straddle shapes. Inbound 4 KB granule by the equal and straddle shapes. 8-byte granule by the straddle shape on both instances.

Extends `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`, which grades the per-entry inbound cells (enabled, range, burst, read and write permission), one `src_id` match value and one mismatch on entry 0, the 4 KB widening of one entry and the reset-state block. It also extends `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_window_walk_test` and `sep_fabric_outbound_remap_filter_walk_test`, which grade the one 8-byte granule that holds the probe address on both instances, `sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test`, which grades the inbound block with the debug bit clear, `sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`, which grades one outbound entry whose enable is cleared after it admitted an access, and `sep_fabric_slot_csr_strobe_rand_test`, which reads the reset values of all 48 entries. Only the new cells are graded here:

* leg 1: the outbound instance, the fall-through, permission-block and no-match corner cells, the inbound read and write divergence, the permission block that hides an allow, and the inbound `prot[1]` mismatch cell (`allow_ns` different from the probe `prot[1]`) with its paired allow cell;
* leg 2: every source-ID cell beyond the single inbound match value, namely the non-zero values 1, 2, 4, 8 and 15 and one drawn non-zero value, the wildcard against AxUSER[3:0] 0 and 15 and two drawn values, the fall-through on a source-ID mismatch and the outbound exact and mismatch cells;
* leg 3: the armed-but-disabled sweep over all 48 entries, the outbound reset state and the enabled control entry per instance;
* leg 4: the outbound 4 KB granule, the equal shape on the 4 KB granule and the straddle shape on both granules and both instances.

The open tests keep the rest. Functional coverage: `sep_filter_match_cg` (`cp_fail_cond_fallthrough`, `cp_src_id_class`, `cp_ar_aw_independent`, `cp_winner_class`), `sep_filter_range_granule_cg` (`cp_granule_shape_inst`, `cp_probe_point_verdict`, `cp_subgranule_off`), `sep_filter_default_policy_cg` (`cp_deny_basis`, `cp_filter_active_block`), `sep_fabric_inbound_aperture_cg.cp_dest_class_verdict`, `sep_fabric_port_silence_cp.sil_inbound_refused` (cause `filter_deny`), existing `sep_inbound_filter_allow_cg`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up with real PROD fuse sense of the pinned image, so the inbound filter is active. The LSU reads `FEAT_CTRL` (64 bits) and compares it with the golden decode that the model computes from the profile table of `lifecycle_controller.adoc` for that image. The golden holds the SEP_DBG state that keeps the filter active. A mismatch prints `CTRL-FILTER-ACTIVE FAIL` and stops the leaf.
. The LSU stages a non-zero marker at two consecutive SRAM words (16-byte aligned, so a 2-beat probe at the first word stays inside one 4 KB page and reads both) and at one cold scratch word, and reads all three back. The scratch register holds 32 data bits, so every compare of a scratch read uses bits [31:0], and bits [63:32] are reserved and logged only. These markers differ from every value that a probe writes. Each inbound write probe to a staged word drives a seeded value that differs from the value that the model holds for that word (the staged marker or the last write that landed), so a landed write changes the LSU read-back and a refused write leaves it unchanged.
. The LSU programs AP output remap region 0 with `valid` 1 and offset `0x20_0000_0000`, and STEE output remap region 0 with `valid` 1 and offset `0x28_0000_0000`. The model predicts the outbound address of an AP or STEE probe as the offset bits [55:19] joined with the region address bits [18:0] (`hw/ip/output_remap/regs/gen/adoc/output_remap.adoc`, `region_attrs`). The LSU reads the registers back. No filter entry is programmed at this step.
. Leg 3, reset state. SI reads and writes SRAM, scratch, a mailbox word and a crypto-region word, and SI reads the filter bank page and `SEP_CPU_CTRL`, each with `prot[1]` 0 and with `prot[1]` 1 (AxLEN 0, AxUSER 0). Error injection at `smn_inbound_axi_req_i`: every entry is disabled. The expected response is DECERR on RRESP or BRESP.
. The LSU reads back the staged SRAM and scratch words. Both hold their staged value.
. Error injection at the LSU outbound path: the LSU reads one SMU-window word (above `0x8000_1000`) and one AP region word (offset below `0x8_0000` in region 0), each with `prot[1]` 0 and with `prot[1]` 1. Every outbound entry is disabled. The expected response is DECERR.
. SI reads one word of the extension window (`0x2000_0000` to `0x3FFF_FFFF`, outside the eFuse shim window) and the word `0x4000_0100`, each with `prot[1]` 0 and with `prot[1]` 1. Each answers DECERR: no entry is enabled, so the filter blocks it (CHK-RESET-DENY-SI). PR-EXT shows no handshake for the extension-window read.
. Leg 3, armed but disabled. The LSU programs all 16 inbound and all 32 outbound entries with the range START_ADDR 0, END_ADDR 2^48-1, which covers every probed address (SRAM, scratch, mailbox, crypto word, filter bank page, `SEP_CPU_CTRL`, the SMU window and the translated AP and STEE words), and with read and write permission, `src_id` 0, `allow_burst` 1 and `entry_enabled` 0. `allow_ns` is 0 on half of the entries of each instance and 1 on the other half, so for each probe `prot[1]` at least one entry would match if it were enabled. The LSU reads every entry back.
. Repeat steps 4 to 7 with the armed entries.
. Leg 3, wide-range control. For each probe `prot[1]` value and each instance, the LSU enables one armed entry whose `allow_ns` equals the NS of the probes at the filter (the probe `prot[1]` inbound, and 1 outbound, where the SMU word and the AP word both leave non-secure), keeps its range START_ADDR 0, END_ADDR 2^48-1, and reads it back. SI reads the staged SRAM word, the staged scratch word and the mailbox, crypto, filter-page and `SEP_CPU_CTRL` words of the deny probes, and the LSU reads one SMU-window word and one translated AP word. Every read answers OKAY. The SRAM and scratch reads show their handshake on PR-XEXT or PR-CSR and return the staged data. The mailbox, crypto, filter-page and `SEP_CPU_CTRL` reads show their request on PR-INFLT, after the inbound filter. SI also writes the mailbox and crypto words; each write answers OKAY and shows its request on PR-INFLT. So each inbound class and direction that the armed run counts is shown to reach its unit when the filter admits it. Every refused inbound probe of the leaf shows no PR-INFLT request, so its DECERR comes from the filter. With the inbound entry enabled, SI also reads the extension-window word of step 7 with the same `prot[1]`. The filter admits it, so PR-EXT shows one request at that address (the PR-EXT control of CHK-RESET-DENY). Its response comes from the bench responder on `axi_extension_axi` and is logged as `INFO-WIDE-CTRL`, not graded. The LSU disables the entry again. This shows that the armed range matches a probe when the entry is enabled.
. Leg 3, control. The LSU selects the seeded control entry index `k` on each instance, programs it over the control probe range only (SRAM or scratch inbound, the SMU window or the AP region word outbound) with `allow_ns` equal to the NS of the probe at the filter, and enables it. The other entries stay armed and disabled. The LSU reads the entry back.
. Leg 3, control probes. SI reads the control-eligible inbound probe and the LSU reads the control-eligible outbound probe. Both answer OKAY. The inbound probe shows its handshake on PR-XEXT or PR-CSR.
. The LSU disables every entry of both instances. Legs 1, 2 and 4 follow. Before each cell of those legs the LSU programs every entry that the cell uses, disables all others and reads each programmed register back.
. Leg 1, cell list. Per instance, the test builds one cell for each (entry state, attribute) pair (the burst attribute applies to inbound SRAM only), the corner cells a, b, d and g on both instances and the corner cells c and e inbound only. Each cell is a read probe and a write probe, each with its own `prot[1]`. Each (entry state, attribute) cell runs on one destination class of its instance that the seed draws: SRAM or scratch inbound, the SMU window or the AP region outbound. The draw gives each destination class at least one cell per instance, so every class runs as a read and as a write on every seed. The burst attribute applies to the inbound SRAM class only.
. For each cell the LSU programs a seeded stack of 3 to 6 entries at seeded indices. The entries overlap on a seeded window set. For the AP class the entries cover the translated AP address. Every entry gets a random `group_id`, and `src_id` is 0 on every entry of leg 1.
. For each (state, attribute) cell, one attribute of the covering entry is changed so that the probe fails exactly one match term or permission bit (range, `prot[1]`, burst, read permission, write permission, or `entry_enabled`). Run the probe. Error injection: the expected response is DECERR. Then run the paired allow cell, which is the same cell with that one attribute restored. The expected response is OKAY. The burst-attribute cell is inbound SRAM only: its probe is a 2-beat INCR (AxLEN 1) whose 16 bytes lie inside the covering range, the covering entry has `allow_burst` 0 in the deny cell and 1 in the allow cell, and the probe of every other cell has AxLEN 0, because a single beat always satisfies the burst term. The scratch class and the outbound instance do not draw the burst attribute.
. Corner cell a. The lowest entry rejects the probe on `prot[1]`, and a higher entry allows it. The probe is admitted by the higher entry.
. Corner cell b. The lowest entry matches with the permission bit clear, and a higher entry allows it. Error injection: the expected response is DECERR, the higher entry never counts.
. Corner cell c, inbound only. The lowest entry has `allow_burst` 0, the probe is a 2-beat INCR burst (AxLEN 1) to SRAM, and a higher entry has `allow_burst` 1. The probe is admitted by the higher entry. Its paired allow cell is an AxLEN 0 probe to the same address.
. Corner cell d. The lowest entry is disabled and covers the probe, and a higher entry allows it. The probe is admitted.
. Corner cell e. The read and the write of one address carry different `prot[1]` and select different entries, so one is admitted and the other is refused. Inbound only: an outbound path sets NS from the address, so the read and the write of one address reach the outbound filter with the same NS.
. Corner cell g. No enabled entry covers the probe. Error injection: the expected response is DECERR.
. Each corner cell runs once as a read and once as a write on each instance it applies to. Inbound probes come from SI with AxLEN 0, and AxLEN 1 INCR to SRAM runs only for the burst-attribute cell, corner cell c and their allow cells. Outbound probes come from the LSU with AxLEN 0.
. The model computes the expected verdict of every cell from the programmed fields and the probe attributes. Each probe response is compared with it.
. Leg 2 entries have read and write permission, `allow_ns` equal to the NS of the probe at the filter and `allow_burst` 0, unless a cell names another value.
. Leg 2, inbound exact and mismatch. For each `src_id` value s of the set 1, 2, 4, 8, 15 and one non-zero value drawn from the other ten (seeded order), the LSU programs an entry over SRAM with `src_id` s. SI probes with AxUSER[3:0] = s (match) and with AxUSER[3:0] different from s, AxUSER[3:0] 0 included (mismatch), as read and as write.
. Leg 2, inbound wildcard. The LSU programs an entry over SRAM with `src_id` 0. SI probes with AxUSER[3:0] 0, 15 and two values drawn from 1 to 14, in a seeded order, as read and as write. Every probe is admitted.
. Leg 2, inbound fall-through. The lowest entry mismatches on the source ID, and a higher entry (wildcard or exact) admits. SI probes the covered address as a read and as a write, on every seed.
. Leg 2, inbound upper bits. When the inbound port carries more than 4 AxUSER bits, SI probes with an upper AxUSER bit set and AxUSER[3:0] equal to s against the entry of s. The probe matches. `CTRL-FILT-USER` shows the issued AxUSER, the upper bit included, on PR-INFLT at the filter output (the filter passes an admitted request unchanged, so that is the AxUSER it decoded).
. Leg 2, outbound mismatch. The LSU programs an outbound entry over the translated AP range with a non-zero `src_id` and enables it alone. The LSU reads and writes an AP word (source ID OTHERS). Error injection at the outbound filter input: the expected response is DECERR, because OTHERS does not equal the non-zero `src_id`. The LSU disables that entry, programs an entry over the same range with `src_id` 0 and enables it alone. The same probes are admitted: OKAY. The LSU repeats both runs for the STEE range.
. Leg 2, SMU source ID. The specification names SEP's source ID and gives no number for it, so the test reads it off PR-OUT. The LSU programs one outbound entry over an SMU-window word with `src_id` 0 and enables it alone. The LSU reads and writes the word once with each of the two drawn AxUSER values. Each probe answers OKAY with one PR-OUT handshake. The AxUSER[3:0] of each handshake is known, is not OTHERS (0) and is the same for both issued values, so the SMU path replaces the issued value. The read value is logged (`OBS-FILT-SRC-ID`) and is not compared with a number.
. Leg 2, outbound exact. The LSU programs an outbound entry over the SMU-window word with `src_id` equal to the source ID read in the previous step and enables it alone. The LSU probes the word with one of the two drawn AxUSER values; the SMU path replaces it with SEP's ID, so the probe matches (OKAY). The LSU then reprograms `src_id` to a non-zero value other than the read ID and probes again (mismatch). Error injection at the outbound filter input: the expected response of the mismatch run is DECERR. Reads and writes both run. These cells take the read ID as stimulus: they grade that the outbound filter matches `src_id` against the replaced AxUSER[3:0], not the number of the ID.
. Every deny cell of leg 2 runs next to a paired allow cell that differs in AxUSER[3:0] or in `src_id` only.
. Leg 4, granule. For each of the seven cells, draw a granule index `g` so that the granules `g` - 1 to `g` + 2 lie inside SRAM (inbound) or inside the SMU window above `0x8000_1000` (outbound). The first page of the SMU window is not used, because the bench responder decodes stores to `0x8000_0000` as console output.
. Program one entry over the cell range: `allow_burst` 1 for the 4 KB granule and 0 for the 8-byte granule, `src_id` 0, `allow_ns` equal to the NS of the probe at the filter (the probe `prot[1]` inbound; outbound, the NS that the SMU path sets, the inverse of address bit 55), read and write allowed. The shape sets START and END. One: both inside granule `g`, not aligned, START different from END (outbound 4 KB only). Equal: START equal to END, unaligned (4 KB granule, both instances). Straddle: START inside granule `g` and END inside granule `g` + 1 (both granules, both instances). Read START_ADDR and END_ADDR back. When START and END are in one granule they read the rounded values, otherwise the programmed values (CHK-GRANULE; `filter_ctrl.rdl`, `START_ADDR`).
. Probe each shape at the widened base minus 4, the widened base, the widened base plus 4, the last 4 bytes of the last admitted granule and the first 4 bytes after it. AxSIZE 2, AxLEN 0, read and write. Before each inbound cell the LSU stages a non-zero marker at every SRAM word that the cell probes and reads it back. Each write probe drives a seeded value that differs from that marker, and the LSU reads each probed word back after the probes. The model computes the admitted set from the widening rule. The probes outside the admitted set expect DECERR, and the probes inside expect OKAY.
. The LSU disables every entry and restores AP and STEE output remap region 0 to the reset value.
. After the graded window closes, two more legs run. In the group leg one inbound entry over a staged SRAM word has a non-zero `src_id` and a drawn `group_id`. SI reads once with AxUSER[11:4] equal to that `group_id` and once with a different, non-zero value (a transaction group of 0 matches any `group_id`), each with AxUSER[3:0] equal to `src_id` (CHK-FILT-GROUP). `CTRL-FILT-USER` shows the issued AxUSER on PR-INFLT for both reads. In the write-to-use leg one inbound entry and one outbound entry are programmed with no read-back, and the covered probe goes out right after the last write response (OBS-FILT-WRITE-TO-USE).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CTRL-FILTER-ACTIVE
|(Precondition of every check of this leaf. It is not a counted check.) The bring-up image is PROD with SEC_DIS 0 and no demotion, so `FEAT_CTRL` equals the golden decode of the profile table and the SEP_DBG state keeps the inbound filter active (`lifecycle_controller.adoc`, "Per-LC-state Feature Control Profile"). `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test` and `sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test` (CHK-PROD-FEAT) grade this value, so this leaf uses the compare only to stop on a wrong bring-up. A mismatch prints `CTRL-FILTER-ACTIVE FAIL: feat_ctrl=0x<v> golden=0x<g>` and stops the leaf, so the named checks of the leaf are missing and the leaf fails. Anchor: the LSU read of `FEAT_CTRL` on the control bus. Evidence: `CTRL-FILTER-ACTIVE LOG: feat_ctrl=0x<v> golden=0x<g> sep_dbg=<n>`.
|CHK-FILTER-CELL
|The response of every graded leg 1 cell equals the output of the filter reference model (`index.adoc`, "Match, Then Permit"). The graded cells are the new cells of the Test Coverage: every outbound cell, the corner cells on both instances, and the inbound `prot[1]` mismatch cell with its paired allow cell. The other inbound (entry state, attribute) cells (range, burst, read permission, write permission and `entry_enabled`) are graded by `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`; here they run as controls, log `CTRL-FILTER-CELL LOG` with the same fields and are not counted. An inbound allow cell also returns the staged data (read; both beats for a 2-beat probe; bits [31:0] only for a scratch word) or lands the written word on the LSU read-back (write; the written value differs from the value that the model holds for the word before the probe). A deny cell answers DECERR and never reaches the target: inbound, PR-XEXT or PR-CSR shows no handshake, and a refused write leaves the word at the value that the model holds on the LSU read-back; outbound, the DECERR is the verdict, and PR-OUT shows no handshake for the refused request. A wrong match term, a wrong winner, a permission bit read from the wrong entry, a fall-through that does not happen and a deny that leaks to the target each give a response or a handshake that differs from the model. Anchor: the SI and LSU responses, read data, the LSU read-back, PR-XEXT and PR-CSR (approved). Control: the paired allow cell drives the same probe. Evidence: `CHK-FILTER-CELL PASS: inst=in\|out dir=R\|W prot1=<0\|1> len=<n> winner=<idx\|none> perm=<0\|1> expect=<OKAY\|DECERR> got=<resp> data=0x<value> target_seen=<n> control_seen=<n>`.
|CHK-FILTER-SRC
|The verdict of every leg 2 cell equals the model output: a non-zero `src_id` matches only the equal AxUSER[3:0], `src_id` 0 matches every value, and a source-ID mismatch falls through to a higher entry (`smc-traffic-filters.svg`, `src_id` against AxUSER[3:0]). An upper AxUSER bit does not change the verdict. A filter that ignores the source ID, compares other bits, reads 0 as an exact value or lets an upper bit decide gives a verdict that differs from the model. On the SMU path the AxUSER[3:0] that PR-OUT shows for the two drawn LSU values is known, is not OTHERS (0) and is one value for both (`fabric.adoc`, `sep-outbound-attributes-table`: the SMU path sets SEP's ID). A path that keeps the LSU value shows two values, and a path that sets OTHERS shows 0. The number is logged, not graded: no specification or RDL states it. Anchor: the SI and LSU responses (approved) and PR-OUT for the SMU source ID. Control: the paired cell that differs in AxUSER[3:0] or in `src_id` only. Evidence: `CHK-FILTER-SRC PASS: inst=in\|out dir=R\|W class=exact\|mismatch\|wildcard\|fallthrough\|upper_bits src_cfg=<n> user=<n> expect=<OKAY\|DECERR> got=<resp> control_resp=<resp>` and `CHK-FILTER-SRC PASS: inst=out class=smu_src_id beats=4 constant=1 not_others=1 issued_src_ids={<a>,<b>}`. Logged: `OBS-FILT-SRC-ID: smu_path_src_id=0x<n> beats=4 user_11_4={...} (value logged)`.
|CTRL-FILTER-RAND
|Stimulus-completeness control. Per seed and instance, the test runs at least one cell of each class: allow, block, fall-through, permission block, no match, a read and write pair that diverge (inbound only), and a source-ID exact, mismatch and wildcard cell. Every count in the line is above zero, except `ar_aw_diverge` on the outbound instance. The DUT cannot fail this checker, and it makes no DUT claim. Evidence: `CTRL-FILTER-RAND LOG: seed=<n> inst=in\|out cells=<n> allow=<n> block=<n> fallthrough=<n> perm_block=<n> no_match=<n> ar_aw_diverge=<n> src_exact=<n> src_mismatch=<n> src_wildcard=<n>`.
|CHK-RESET-DENY
|After reset, with no filter programmed, every outbound probe answers DECERR (`index.adoc`, "Instances"; "Blocked Transactions"). The inbound reset-state block is graded by `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test` and `sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test`; here the inbound reset-state probes run as a control and log `CTRL-RESET-DENY LOG inst=in` with the same fields: PR-XEXT and PR-CSR show no handshake (the mailbox has no approved tap, so its probes show the response alone), and the staged SRAM and scratch words read back unchanged after the write probes. The responses of the extension-window and `0x4000_0100` probes are graded as CHK-RESET-DENY-SI. That check grades the stated response and does not tell a healthy filter from a faulty one, because the extension tie-off and the crossbar limit answer DECERR even when the filter admits. The check grades the PR-EXT silence of the extension-window probe: PR-EXT shows no handshake for it. An entry that resets enabled, or an unmatched policy that admits, gives an OKAY answer or a handshake. Anchor: the SI and LSU responses, the LSU read-back, PR-XEXT, PR-CSR (approved) and PR-EXT. Controls: CHK-RESET-DENY-CONTROL, and the extension-window read of the wide-range control step, which the enabled entry admits and which shows one request on PR-EXT, so PR-EXT is live in the same leaf. A leaf whose PR-EXT control shows no request fails the check. Evidence: `CHK-RESET-DENY PASS: inst=out probes=<n> prot1_0=<n> prot1_1=<n> decerr=<n> target_seen=0 readback_same=<n> ext_seen=0 ext_ctrl_seen=<n>`.
|CHK-RESET-DENY-ARMED
|With all 48 entries armed and disabled, every graded probe of each `prot[1]` value answers DECERR, with the same inbound silence and the same read-back as CHK-RESET-DENY. A filter that matches on range and permission without `entry_enabled` answers OKAY or shows a handshake. Every entry holds the range START_ADDR 0, END_ADDR 2^48-1, which covers every graded probe address and lies below 2^56-1, so the check rests on no unspecified top-of-space behaviour. For each probe `prot[1]` at least one entry holds that covering range, permission bits, `src_id` 0 and an equal `allow_ns`, so the entry would match if it were enabled. The wide-range control step proves it: with one armed entry per instance enabled and the range unchanged, every inbound probe class and the outbound control probes answer OKAY and the inbound probes show their handshake. A DUT that ignores `entry_enabled` therefore answers OKAY or shows a handshake in the disabled run, and the check fails. Anchor: the SI and LSU responses, the LSU read-back, PR-XEXT and PR-CSR (approved). Control: the wide-range control step and CHK-RESET-DENY-CONTROL. Evidence: `CHK-RESET-DENY-ARMED PASS: entries=48 enabled=0 range_end=0xffffffffffff armed_ns0=<n> armed_ns1=<n> probes=<n> prot1_0=<n> prot1_1=<n> decerr=<n> target_seen=0 wide_ctrl_okay=<n> wide_ctrl_seen=<n> wide_ctrl_in=sram,scratch,mbox,crypto,fpage,cpuctrl`.
|CHK-RESET-DENY-CONTROL
|With one entry per instance enabled over the probed unit only, the same control-eligible probe answers OKAY, and an inbound probe shows its handshake on PR-XEXT or PR-CSR. This proves that the probe path is alive, so the DECERR of the two deny checks comes from the filter. Anchor: the SI and LSU responses, PR-XEXT and PR-CSR (approved). Evidence: `CHK-RESET-DENY-CONTROL PASS: enabled_entry=<k> inst=in\|out probe=sram\|scratch\|smu\|ap prot1=<0\|1> resp=OKAY seen=<n>`.
|CHK-GRANULE
|The verdict at each leg 4 probe equals the admitted set that the model computes from the widening rule (`index.adoc`, "Address Range Granule"): START_ADDR widens down and END_ADDR widens up to the granule, a range shorter than the granule admits the whole granule, and a straddling range admits both granules. A refused inbound probe shows no PR-XEXT handshake, and a refused write leaves the probed SRAM word at the marker that the leg 4 cell staged there, on the LSU read-back. A DUT with no widening, a widening on the wrong side or a straddle that admits one granule answers a probe differently from the model. The START_ADDR and END_ADDR read-back after the entry write equals the rounded values when START and END land in one granule, otherwise the programmed values (`hw/ip/axi_filter/regs/filter_ctrl.rdl`, `START_ADDR`). Anchor: the SI and LSU responses, the LSU read-back and PR-XEXT (approved). Control: the admitted probes of the same shape answer OKAY and show their handshake. Evidence: `CHK-GRANULE PASS: inst=in\|out gran=8\|4096 shape=one\|equal\|straddle start=0x<hex> end=0x<hex> rb_start=0x<hex> rb_end=0x<hex> expect_rb=0x<hex>,0x<hex> admitted=[0x<lo>,0x<hi>] probes=<n> allow=<n> block=<n> refused_seen=0 control_seen=<n>`.
|OBS-DBG-FILTER-HIT
|Logged only, no pass or fail. For every probe of the graded legs and of the two logged legs: the instance, the class, the direction, the address, the response, and the value of `ext_debug_bus_o[191:160]` at the start of the probe and after each change during it (`lane_191_160`; an X or Z value prints `X`). The specifications name no lane layout of the debug bus.
|CHK-FILT-GROUP
|The group leg: both SI reads, with AxUSER[11:4] equal to (`equal`) and different from (`differ`) the entry `group_id`, are admitted: OKAY, at least one handshake at the target tap and the staged SRAM data. The entry matches on every enabled term (range, `prot[1]`, `src_id` against AxUSER[3:0], single beat), and group ID matching is disabled on every instance, so `group_id` has no effect (`hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc:41-43`). A DUT that compares AxUSER[11:4] with `group_id` refuses the `differ` read and fails. Control: `CTRL-FILT-USER` shows the issued AxUSER, bits 11:4 included, on PR-INFLT at the filter output for both reads (`CTRL-MISSING FILT-USER` fails the leaf). The check does not show which transaction field carries a group ID (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). Anchor: the SI response and read data, PR-XEXT and PR-CSR. Evidence: `CHK-FILT-GROUP PASS: entry=<k> group_id=<g> src_id=<s> axuser=0x<hex> kind=equal\|differ expect=OKAY got=OKAY target_ar=<n>`.
|CHK-RESET-DENY-SI
|In the reset state and in the armed-but-disabled state, the SI reads of one extension-window word and of `0x4000_0100`, each with `prot[1]` 0 and 1, answer DECERR within the bound: no entry is enabled, so the inbound filter blocks them, and a blocked transaction ends in DECERR (`hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc`, Blocked Transactions; `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc:145` gives DECERR at or above `0x4000_0000` too). A DUT that answers OKAY, SLVERR or no response fails. Anchor: the SI response. Evidence: `CHK-RESET-DENY-SI PASS: round=RESET\|ARMED si_read cls=ext\|xlim addr=0x<hex> prot1=<0\|1> resp=DECERR timed_out=False pr_ext=0 expect=DECERR`.
|OBS-FILT-WRITE-TO-USE
|Logged only, no pass or fail. The write-to-use leg: the instance (`in` or `out`), the entry index, the address, the response, and the AR count at the filter tap (`pr_inflt_ar`, or `pr_out_ar` for the outbound instance) and, for the inbound instance, at the target tap (`target_ar`). The specification does not state when a filter write takes effect. A read-back of the entry follows each probe.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The test grades `group_id` only against AxUSER[11:4] (CHK-FILT-GROUP); a grade on another group carrier needs a probe that carries a group, see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>. It does not grade the `locked` bit, the filter register storage, or the SEP debug bypass. The per-entry inbound cells, the single-granule probes and the reset values of the entries stay with the open tests named under Test Coverage. The test sends no outbound burst and no inbound burst to a register region, because the specification states no response for a burst to a register region. The test grades the lowest-match rule on the programmed fields only and does not claim a verdict for an inverted range or a range that touches 2^56-1.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_outbound_route_attr_test]]
===== 2.27 `sep_fabric_outbound_route_attr_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (Verilator on `lsu_stub_all_live`, VCS on `--target default` per the shared Phase 3 testlist-entry rule, group `cpu_stub`, `testlists/system.toml`), real PROD fuse sense (no `+skip_fuse_sense`) with a pinned image: PROD, SEC_DIS 0, and fixed `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` values whose DBG_1 bit 0 is clear in both, then one DEMOTE_1 write for the no-bypass leg, 4 seeds. Tool: Verilator and VCS. VCS is required for the X check on the PR-OUT capture.

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_outbound_route_attr_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (LSU master on `s_axi`, SI master on `m_axi`); `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (outbound responder: OKAY and zero read data for any address); the filter reference model of this bucket (written from `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc`, never from the RTL). Probes. Approved: the LSU and SI responses and read data, the LSU read-back of local words, the LSU and SI reads of `FEAT_CTRL`. PR-OUT (an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list), for CHK-OUT-CAPTURE, CHK-OUT-NOEXTRA and the disabled-pair silence of CHK-OUT-ROUTE. No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, RANDCFG. Dimensions: region index (0 to 15 per remap), offset bits 54:19 (non-identity; bit 55 is fixed at 1 for AP and 0 for STEE, and a second pass of each class flips it in the same region), direction order, AxPROT value, two LSU AxUSER values `u0` and `u1` (12 bits: bits [11:4] non-zero, bits [3:0] non-zero and different), which the LSU alternates from one access to the next, and the bits 54:32 of the alias offset of the `>= 4 GiB` page with address bit 55 clear (non-zero). The SMU aperture stays at the reset pair (`0x8000_0000`, size `0x4000_0000`). Legal range per `hw/ip/output_remap/regs/gen/adoc/output_remap.adoc` (`region_attrs`). Rejection list: an offset equal to the region identity; an offset sum at or above 2^56; a translated AP or STEE address (with either offset bit 55 value), or the untranslated word of the step 11 pair, that lies inside the SMU aperture, inside the local pair range, or inside the range of any other pair or stack entry that is enabled in the same step. `reseed = 4` samples different region indices, offsets, AxPROT values and AxUSER values. Alias region 0 maps the SMU page `0xA000_1000` to the same page with address bit 55 set (fixed), then to a drawn page at or above `0x1_0000_0000` with bit 55 clear. Every route class runs in both directions on every seed. The low-address leg draws its 8-byte word from the same stream after the draws of the other legs.

*Objective*: The route demux sends the SMU aperture (reset `SMU_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` and `SMU_REGION_SIZE`) and the AP and STEE regions through their output remaps to `smn_outbound_axi_req_o`. A scratch or system-CSR request does not leave on that port. The AP and STEE remaps set the source ID to OTHERS, AxUSER zero in every bit, and the SMU path (the SMU aperture and any address at or above `0x1_0000_0000`) sets it to SEP's ID. No specification or RDL gives the number of SEP's ID, so the test grades it as not OTHERS and as one value over two different issued values, and logs the number. A STEE region with `valid` 0 passes the address unchanged (`output_remap.adoc`, `region_attrs.valid`) and still leaves through the STEE remapper, so it carries OTHERS and NS as the inverse of its address bit 55. AxPROT[1] (NS) leaves as 1 on the AP path and as the inverse of bit 55 of the outgoing address on the SMU and STEE paths; the other AxPROT bits and the address, bit 55 included, pass unchanged. OTHERS is AxUSER zero (`fabric.adoc`, "Transaction Routing"), so an AP or STEE capture is graded on all 12 AxUSER bits. On the SMU path the specification states no value for AxUSER[11:4]: those bits and the W user are logged, not graded. The outbound filter checks address, NS and source ID after remapping and has no bypass. The test grades the forcing of OTHERS through the outbound filter: while the LSU drives a non-zero AxUSER[3:0] `u`, an AP or STEE request carries OTHERS, so it does not match an entry whose `src_id` is `u` and it matches an entry whose `src_id` is 0. The route of an address at or above `0x1_0000_0000` is graded by the hit cells of `sep_fabric_alias_remap_attr_rand_test`, and the NS mismatch response and the exact source-ID match of an SMU word by `sep_fabric_filter_match_priority_rand_test`. The test also moves the SMU aperture: the route of a programmed pair is stated (`fabric.adoc:176-178`), so the moved window must route like the reset window and the reset window must stop routing, and the first-match order puts the SMU aperture before the AP region (`fabric.adoc:174`). The specification states two conflicting rules for an address below `0x1_0000_0000` outside every window, so the test does not grade a route outside every window. Cites `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` ("Fabric Topology", output fabric; "Transaction Routing" and `sep-outbound-attributes-table`), `hw/sys/sep/doc/assets/sep-output-fabric.svg`, `hw/ip/axi_alias_remap/regs/gen/adoc/alias_remap.adoc` (`region_attrs`), `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc` (`SMU_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR`, `SMU_REGION_SIZE`), `hw/ip/output_remap/regs/gen/adoc/output_remap.adoc` (`region_attrs`), `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` ("Match, Then Permit"; "Blocked Transactions") `hw/sys/smc/doc/assets/smc-traffic-filters.svg` (`src_id` against AxUSER[3:0]) and `hw/sys/sep/doc/lifecycle_controller.adoc` ("Per-LC-state Feature Control Profile": PROD with SEC_DIS 0 and the demotion of DBG_1).

*Test Coverage*: The SMU reset aperture (first, middle and last word) and the AP and STEE regions with a non-identity offset, each on read and write and each with `prot[1]` 0 and 1; an address at or above `0x1_0000_0000` with bit 55 set and with bit 55 clear, the AP region with offset bit 55 clear and the STEE region with offset bit 55 set, on read and write with `prot[1]` 0 and 1; the STEE region with `valid` 0 at its pass-through address, on read and write with `prot[1]` 0 and 1; the source-ID stack over the AP and STEE words with the LSU AxUSER[3:0] non-zero; the outbound pair after DEMOTE_1; and local scratch and `SEP_SW_DEBUG` accesses behind an outbound pair that would admit them. Extends `sep_fabric_outbound_remap_filter_walk_test` and `sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`, which grade the offset translation and the low-bit pass-through of the AP and STEE regions through read requests and the valid-clear pass-through, `sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test` and `sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test`, which grade the `FEAT_CTRL` value and the inbound filter bypass after DEMOTE_1, and `sep_address_map_test`, which grades the local scratch and `SEP_SW_DEBUG` accesses with no outbound entry. Only the new cells are graded here: the SMU aperture on read and write, the AP and STEE writes, the forcing of OTHERS on AP and STEE traffic and the outbound pair with the inbound filter bypassed. The absence of scratch and `SEP_SW_DEBUG` requests on the port, the PR-OUT capture of AxPROT, AxUSER and write data, the NS and source ID that each path derives, and the attributes of the STEE `valid` 0 pass-through are new cells, and this test grades them. The AP and STEE read cells run as controls, and the open tests grade them. The open tests keep the rest. Functional coverage: `sep_fabric_outbound_route_cg` (`cp_route_class_dir` for the SMU, AP and STEE cells, `cp_smu_aperture_cfg`, `cp_out_attr`), `sep_remap_output_offset_cg.cp_instance_dir`, `sep_filter_match_cg.cp_fail_cond_fallthrough` (the outbound source-ID mismatch cells), `sep_filter_default_policy_cg.cp_dbg_bypass`, `sep_fabric_port_silence_cp` (`sil_out_local_target`, `sil_out_filter_deny`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up with real PROD fuse sense, so the inbound filter is active. Read `FEAT_CTRL` and log it (the no-bypass leg uses it as a logged precondition). Read `SMU_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` and `SMU_REGION_SIZE` and log them. The test writes them only in the moved-SMU and SMU-over-AP legs at the end.
. Draw the AP region index `i`, the STEE region index `j`, their offsets `O_ap` and `O_stee` (bits 55:19 differ from the region identity; `O_ap[55]` is 1 and `O_stee[55]` is 0), the AxPROT values, the direction order, `u0` and `u1`, and the alias offset `O_g` (bits 54:32, non-zero). The in-region word address is `a` = `0x100` (below `0x8_0000`). The model predicts the AP address as `{O_ap[55:19], a[18:0]}` and the STEE address as `{O_stee[55:19], a[18:0]}`. The model predicts NS 1 for AP and the inverse of address bit 55 for SMU and STEE, and AxUSER[3:0] 0 for AP and STEE. For SMU the model predicts no number: AxUSER[3:0] is not 0 and is the same on every SMU capture of the leaf.
. The LSU programs AP output remap region `i` with `valid` 1 and offset `O_ap`, and STEE output remap region `j` with `valid` 1 and offset `O_stee`. The LSU reads the registers back.
. Every outbound entry of this test comes as a pair over one range: one entry with `allow_ns` 0 and one with `allow_ns` 1, read and write allowed, `src_id` 0, so a probe with either `prot[1]` value matches one entry of the pair. The pairs cover the SMU aperture `0x8000_0000` to `0xBFFF_FFFF` (the SMU pair), the translated AP word (the AP pair), the translated STEE word (the STEE pair) and the local range `0x1080_2000` to `0x10A3_1FFF` (the local pair). The pairs use entry indices 2 and above. Entries 0 and 1 are for the source-ID stack. The LSU enables the SMU pair and reads it back.
. SMU reset aperture. The LSU reads and writes the first word `0x8000_0000`, the middle word `0xA000_0000` and the last word `0xBFFF_FFF8` with `prot[1]` 0 and with `prot[1]` 1 (the other AxPROT bits are seeded), alternating `u0` and `u1`. Each answers OKAY. The expected PR-OUT capture is one AW or AR at the issued address with the issued AxPROT bits 0 and 2, AxPROT[1] 1 (address bit 55 is clear) and AxUSER[3:0] SEP's ID (not 0, the same on every SMU capture), and the W data, strobe and a known W user.
