. The LSU disables the SMU pair. Error injection at the outbound filter input: the LSU reads and writes the first word again. The expected response is DECERR, and no PR-OUT capture shows. The LSU enables the SMU pair again.
. SMU path with bit 55. The LSU programs alias region 0 over the page `0xA000_1000` with offset 2^55 and `valid` 1 and reads it back. The LSU enables a pair over the granule of `0x80_0000_A000_1100` and reads and writes `0xA000_1100` with `prot[1]` 0 and with `prot[1]` 1. Each answers OKAY. The expected PR-OUT capture is at `0x80_0000_A000_1100` with AxPROT[1] 0 and AxUSER[3:0] SEP's ID. The LSU disables the pair.
. Address at or above `0x1_0000_0000` with bit 55 clear. The LSU reprograms alias region 0 with offset `O_g` and reads it back. The LSU enables a pair over the granule of `0xA000_1100` + `O_g` and reads and writes `0xA000_1100` with `prot[1]` 0 and with `prot[1]` 1. Each answers OKAY. The expected PR-OUT capture is at `0xA000_1100` + `O_g` with AxPROT[1] 1 and AxUSER[3:0] SEP's ID. The LSU disables the pair and clears the alias region.
. AP region. The LSU enables the AP pair. The LSU reads and writes `0x1100_0000` + `i` * `0x8_0000` + `a` with `prot[1]` 0 and with `prot[1]` 1. Each answers OKAY. The read cells are controls that the open tests grade; the write cells are graded here. The expected PR-OUT capture shows the translated address, AxUSER 0 although the LSU drove `u`, AxPROT[1] 1 whatever the issued value and although offset bit 55 is set, the other AxPROT bits as issued, and the W data and strobe as issued.
. The LSU disables the AP pair. Error injection at the outbound filter input: the LSU repeats the AP accesses. The expected response is DECERR.
. The LSU enables an outbound pair over the untranslated AP word only (the AP pair stays disabled). Error injection at the outbound filter input: the LSU repeats the AP accesses. The filter sees the translated address, so the expected response is DECERR. The LSU disables this pair.
. AP with bit 55 clear. The LSU reprograms AP region `i` with offset `O_ap` - 2^55, enables a pair over the granule of the translated word, and reads and writes the AP word with `prot[1]` 0 and with `prot[1]` 1. Each answers OKAY. The expected PR-OUT capture is at the translated address with bit 55 clear, AxPROT[1] 1 and AxUSER[3:0] 0. The LSU disables the pair and restores offset `O_ap`.
. STEE region. Repeat steps 9 to 11 for `0x1180_0000` + `j` * `0x8_0000` + `a` and the STEE pair. The expected AxPROT[1] is 1, the inverse of offset bit 55.
. STEE with bit 55. The LSU reprograms STEE region `j` with offset `O_stee` + 2^55, enables a pair over the granule of the translated word, and reads and writes the STEE word with `prot[1]` 0 and with `prot[1]` 1. Each answers OKAY. The expected PR-OUT capture is at the translated address with bit 55 set, AxPROT[1] 0 and AxUSER[3:0] 0. The LSU disables the pair.
. STEE with `valid` 0. The LSU reprograms STEE region `j` with offset `O_stee` and `valid` 0 and reads it back. The LSU enables a pair over the granule of the untranslated STEE word, and reads and writes the STEE word with `prot[1]` 0 and with `prot[1]` 1. Each answers OKAY. The expected PR-OUT capture is at the untranslated address (`output_remap.adoc`, `region_attrs.valid`: the address passes unchanged), with AxPROT[1] 1 (address bit 55 is clear) and AxUSER[3:0] 0 (the STEE remapper row of `sep-outbound-attributes-table`). The LSU disables the pair and restores `valid` 1.
. Source-ID stack. For each class (AP word, STEE word), the LSU programs outbound entry 0 over the translated range with `src_id` `u0`[3:0], read and write permission clear, and entry 1 over the same range with `src_id` 0 and both permissions set. Both entries are enabled and use the `allow_ns` of the NS that the class sets (1 for both words, because `O_stee[55]` is 0); the probe `prot[1]` is seeded. The SMU pair stays enabled. The LSU reads the entries back.
. The LSU reads and writes each class word with AxUSER `u0`. AP and STEE requests carry OTHERS, skip entry 0 and are admitted by entry 1: OKAY.
. Control for the forcing. The LSU sets the `src_id` of entry 0 to 0 and keeps its permissions clear. Error injection at the outbound filter input: the LSU repeats the AP and STEE requests with AxUSER `u0`. Entry 0 matches the requests and refuses them. The expected response is DECERR. The LSU disables entries 0 and 1.
. Local addresses. The LSU enables the local pair. The LSU stages a non-zero marker in cold scratch word `0x1080_2000` and in `SEP_SW_DEBUG`. The LSU reads both words (8 bytes); each read answers OKAY and returns the staged marker in the RDL field bits of its register (CHK-OUT-LOCAL-DATA), and the response and both data words are logged. The LSU reads the SMU word `0xA000_0000` as the capture control (the SMU pair is enabled). The expected PR-OUT result is no capture for the scratch and `SEP_SW_DEBUG` reads and a capture for the SMU read. The LSU restores `SEP_SW_DEBUG` to its reset value and disables the local pair.
. No-bypass leg. The LSU reads `FEAT_CTRL` (64 bits) on the control bus and logs it. With every inbound entry disabled, SI reads `FEAT_CTRL` and logs the response. Both reads are logged and not graded, because `sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test` and `sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test` grade the golden value and the inbound block.
. The LSU writes `DEMOTE_1` with the `demote` bit (bit 0) set and reads `DEMOTE_1` back. The LSU reads `FEAT_CTRL` and logs it. The SI read of the next step starts only after this read.
. SI reads `FEAT_CTRL` with no inbound entry. The read answers OKAY, which shows that the inbound filter is bypassed. This is the precondition of the check. When it does not answer OKAY, the leaf prints `CHK-OUT-NOBYPASS FAIL: bypass_not_established` and skips the next two steps.
. The LSU disables the SMU pair and reads the SMU word `0xA000_0000`. Error injection at the outbound filter input: the expected response is DECERR, with no capture on PR-OUT.
. The LSU enables the SMU pair and reads the SMU word `0xA000_0000` again. The read answers OKAY.

. Moved SMU aperture. Check that the moved pair (base `0x4100_0000`, size `0x0100_0000`) does not overlap the bench SMC aperture. The LSU programs the pair and reads it back, enables an outbound pair over the moved window and one over the reset window, and reads and writes the first, the last and a drawn word of the moved window with `prot[1]` 0 and 1: OKAY, one PR-OUT capture at the issued address with SEP's ID and NS 1 (CHK-OUT-SMU-MOVED). The reset base `0x8000_0000` answers DECERR with no capture. The LSU restores the reset pair (size register first) and the moved first and last words answer DECERR with no capture while their outbound pair stays enabled.
. SMU over AP. AP region 0 is valid with a drawn non-identity offset. The LSU programs the SMU pair to the AP region 0 window and enables outbound pairs over the untranslated and the translated granule. Reads and writes with `prot[1]` 0 and 1 answer OKAY and are captured untranslated with SEP's ID and NS 1 (CHK-OUT-SMU-FIRST). Control, after the reset pair is restored: the same accesses are captured translated with AxUSER 0 and NS 1. Every graded access of the leaf is issued first with the coverage window closed and graded, then repeated inside owner window 227.
. AP with offset 0. The LSU programs the AP region of the leaf with `valid` 1 and offset 0, enables a pair over the granule of the translated word and reads and writes the word with `prot[1]` 0 and 1. Each answers OKAY and the capture is at `{0, a[18:0]}` (CHK-OUT-CAPTURE). The LSU disables the pair and restores the region.
. Low external address. Outside the graded window, the LSU draws an 8-byte word below `0x1000_0000` outside every SMC aperture, enables a pair over its granule and reads and writes it with `prot[1]` 0 and 1. The response and the PR-OUT and PR-SMC captures are logged (OBS-OUT-LOWADDR). The LSU disables the pair.
*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-OUT-ROUTE
|The graded cells are the SMU aperture on read and on write, and the AP and STEE writes. Each answers OKAY with an enabled outbound entry over the class, and DECERR with no enabled entry (`fabric.adoc`, "Transaction Routing"; `index.adoc`, "Blocked Transactions"). For AP and STEE the entry covers the translated range, and an enabled entry over the untranslated range alone gives DECERR on the write, because the filter checks the address after remapping. A class that does not reach the outbound filter, or a remap that does not move the address, gives an answer that differs from the model. The AP and STEE read cells, with their disabled and untranslated-only variants, run as controls and are not graded here: the open tests grade them (`sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`, `sep_fabric_outbound_remap_filter_walk_test`), and the PASS line does not count them as graded classes. For the SMU first-word read and write with the SMU pair disabled, PR-OUT shows no capture from the request to the DECERR response. The same request with the pair enabled captures on PR-OUT, and a leaf whose enabled-pair request shows no PR-OUT capture fails the check. Anchor: the LSU responses, with the responder OKAY as the admitted verdict (approved), and PR-OUT (an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list) for the disabled-pair silence. Control: the same request answers OKAY with the entry enabled and DECERR with it disabled, and the read cells drive the same classes. Evidence: `CHK-OUT-ROUTE PASS: class=smu\|ap\|stee dir=<R and W for smu, W for ap and stee> entry_on_resp=OKAY entry_off_resp=DECERR untranslated_only_resp=DECERR\|na control_reads=<n> entry_off_out_seen=0\|na entry_on_out_seen=1\|na`.
|CHK-OUT-USER
|Behind the source-ID stack, AP and STEE requests with AxUSER `u0` from the LSU are admitted, on read and on write: OKAY (`fabric.adoc`, "Transaction Routing": the AP and STEE remappers replace the source ID with `OTHERS`; `smc-traffic-filters.svg`). A remapper that keeps the initiator AxUSER matches entry 0 (`src_id` `u0`[3:0], permissions clear) and answers DECERR. Anchor: the LSU responses (approved). Control: with the `src_id` of entry 0 set to 0, the same requests answer DECERR, so entry 0 is the entry that decides and the stack is alive. The SEP source ID of an SMU request is graded by CHK-OUT-CAPTURE. Evidence: `CHK-OUT-USER PASS: class=ap\|stee dir=R\|W user_issued=0x<u0> resp=OKAY control_resp=DECERR`.
|CHK-OUT-NOBYPASS
|With the inbound filter bypassed (the precondition step: the SI read of `FEAT_CTRL` answers OKAY with no inbound entry after DEMOTE_1), the LSU read of the SMU word answers DECERR with the SMU pair disabled and OKAY with it enabled (`fabric.adoc`, "Filtering and Protection": the outbound filter has no bypass). A debug bypass that reaches the outbound filter admits the request with the pair disabled. The `FEAT_CTRL` value and the inbound bypass are graded by `sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test` and `sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test`, and this check grades only the outbound pair. Anchor: the LSU responses (approved). Control: the enabled pair, and the OKAY of the SI read that shows the bypass. Evidence: `CHK-OUT-NOBYPASS PASS: bypass_pre=OKAY feat_after=0x<v1> disabled_resp=DECERR enabled_resp=OKAY`.
|CHK-OUT-NOEXTRA
|PR-OUT shows no capture for the LSU reads of the cold scratch word and `SEP_SW_DEBUG` behind an enabled local pair that covers them (`fabric.adoc`, "Fabric Topology": a local address stays local). The response and the data of these reads are graded by CHK-OUT-LOCAL-DATA. Anchor: PR-OUT (an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list). Control: the SMU read of the same leaf captures on PR-OUT. Evidence: `CHK-OUT-NOEXTRA PASS: local_reads=<n> out_seen=0 control_out=<n>`.
|CHK-OUT-LOCAL-DATA
|Each LSU read of the cold scratch word and of `SEP_SW_DEBUG` behind the enabled local pair answers OKAY and returns the seeded non-zero marker that the LSU staged, compared on the RDL field bits of the register (`SCRATCH_0_.data` bits [31:0], `SEP_SW_DEBUG.sep_sw_debug`); the other bits are logged. A local read that the fabric sends out, answers with an error or answers with other data fails. Anchor: the LSU response and read data. Control: CHK-OUT-NOEXTRA in the same step. Evidence: `CHK-OUT-LOCAL-DATA PASS: cold_scratch\|sep_sw_debug addr=0x<addr> resp=OKAY got=0x<m> want=0x<m>`.
|CHK-OUT-CAPTURE
|Each PR-OUT capture matches its issued request: the predicted address, AxPROT bits 0 and 2 as issued, AxPROT[1] as its path sets it (1 for AP, the inverse of address bit 55 for SMU and STEE, the STEE `valid` 0 pass-through included), AxUSER 0 in all 12 bits (OTHERS) for an AP or STEE request, and the W data and strobe as issued (`fabric.adoc`, "Transaction Routing", `sep-outbound-attributes-table`). The remap replaces the address above the 512 KiB region granule with the programmed region offset and keeps the issued address below it (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc:178-179`; `hw/ip/output_remap/regs/output_remap.rdl:22-25`), so an AP pass with offset 0 predicts `{0, a[18:0]}`. For an SMU-path request (the SMU aperture and the `>= 4 GiB` class) AxUSER[3:0] is not OTHERS (0); over the leaf it is the same on every SMU capture although the LSU alternates `u0` and `u1`, whose bits [3:0] differ, so a path that keeps the LSU value shows two values and fails. At most one of the two can equal SEP's ID, so no draw depends on the number, which no specification or RDL states; the number is logged. X or Z in a captured field, W user included, fails on VCS. AxUSER[11:4] of every capture (graded as zero on an AP or STEE capture) and the W user of an SMU-path write are logged; on the SMU path they are not graded, because the specification states no value for them. Anchor: PR-OUT (an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list). Control: the capture cells themselves drive PR-OUT. Evidence: `CHK-OUT-CAPTURE PASS: class=smu\|ge4g\|ap\|stee\|stee_inv dir=R\|W issued=0x<addr> seen=0x<addr> prot=<n> user_issued=0x<u> user_seen=<n> wdata=0x<d>\|na wstrb=0x<s>\|na prot_issued=0x<n>`. At the end of the run the leaf requires the number of PR-OUT captures to equal the number of admitted requests and logs `INFO-OUT-CAPTURE-SUMMARY LOG: captures=<n> admitted=<n>`, then grades the SMU source ID over the leaf: `CHK-OUT-CAPTURE PASS: path=smu src_id beats=<n> constant=1 not_others=1 issued_src_ids={<a>,<b>}`. Logged: `OBS-OUT-SRC-ID: smu_path_src_id=0x<n> beats=<n> user_11_4={...} (value logged)`, `OBS-OUT-USER-HI: class=smu\|smu_moved\|smu_over_ap\|ge4g dir=R\|W user_issued_11_4=0x<h> user_seen_11_4=0x<h> (value logged)`, `OBS-OUT-WUSER: class=smu\|ge4g dir=W wuser_issued=0x<u> wuser_seen=0x<w> awuser_seen=0x<a> (value logged)`.
|CHK-OUT-HIBIT
|Every route class is graded at each address bit 55 value it reaches (`fabric.adoc`, "Transaction Routing", `sep-outbound-attributes-table`): AP with bit 55 set and clear (NS 1 for both), the SMU aperture with bit 55 clear (NS 1; the reset aperture lies below 4 GiB), the `>= 4 GiB` class (`ge4g`) and STEE with bit 55 clear (NS 1) and set (NS 0), and the STEE `valid` 0 pass-through (`stee_inv`) with bit 55 clear (NS 1). Over the captures that pass CHK-OUT-CAPTURE, each of these cells shows a read and a write at issued AxPROT[1] 0 and 1, so the outgoing NS and source ID are compared on the PR-OUT capture for both issued NS values, and the outgoing NS follows the path and not the initiator. A path that passes AxPROT[1] through fails CHK-OUT-CAPTURE in the cells where the issued value differs. Control: the cells with bit 55 clear. Evidence: `CHK-OUT-HIBIT PASS: ap: bit55=0 ns=1 cells=p<0 or 1><R or W>,...; ap: bit55=1 ns=1 cells=...; smu: bit55=0 ns=1 cells=...; ge4g: bit55=0 ns=1 cells=...; ge4g: bit55=1 ns=0 cells=...; stee: bit55=0 ns=1 cells=...; stee: bit55=1 ns=0 cells=...; stee_inv: bit55=0 ns=1 cells=...`.
|CHK-OUT-SMU-MOVED
|With the SMU pair moved to base `0x4100_0000`, size `0x0100_0000`, its first, last and a drawn word answer OKAY on read and write, each with one PR-OUT capture at the issued address, SEP's ID (AxUSER[3:0] not 0) and NS the inverse of address bit 55; the reset base answers DECERR with no capture; after the reset pair is restored, the moved first and last words answer DECERR with no capture (`fabric.adoc:176-178`, `:181-182`, `:145-146`). Catches a fixed SMU decode, an old window that still decodes, and a window that is not restored. Legal moved values are an open specification question; the leaf grades the route of the one programmed pair.
|CHK-OUT-SMU-FIRST
|With the SMU window over AP region 0 (valid, non-identity offset), the accesses are captured untranslated with SEP's ID and NS 1, so the SMU match wins (`fabric.adoc:174`, the demux takes the first match in the order shown). Control: with the SMU pair at reset, the same accesses are captured translated with AxUSER 0 (OTHERS) and NS 1. Catches an AP check before the SMU check and an AP region that does not translate.
|OBS-OUT-LOWADDR
|Logged only, no pass or fail. For an LSU read and write of a drawn word below `0x1000_0000` outside every SMC aperture, behind an enabled pair over its granule: the direction, the address, the response, and the addresses seen at PR-OUT (`pr_out`) and PR-SMC (`pr_smc`). The specification states two conflicting rules for the route of such an address.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The test grades the route of one moved SMU pair, not the set of legal moved values. It does not grade the route of an address below `0x4000_0000` outside every window (the specification states two conflicting rules: the routing prose sends it out to the SMN and the output fabric text returns it to the peripheral crossbar, which forwards it to the `ext` initiator). At or above `0x4000_0000` and below `0x1_0000_0000`, an address outside every window returns to the peripheral crossbar, which answers DECERR (`fabric.adoc:145-146`, `:181-182`); CHK-OUT-SMU-MOVED grades that for the reset base while the pair is moved and for the moved words after the restore, as `sep_fabric_smc_route_test` does above the SMC aperture. The test does not grade the offset translation of the AP and STEE read cells. That stays with `sep_fabric_outbound_remap_filter_walk_test` and `sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`, which also keep the grading of the AP and STEE read cells that run here as controls and the filter verdict of the `valid` 0 pass-through. This test grades the address, NS and source ID of the STEE `valid` 0 pass-through on PR-OUT. The route of an address at or above `0x1_0000_0000` is graded with the alias hit cells of `sep_fabric_alias_remap_attr_rand_test`; this test grades its NS and source ID on PR-OUT. The NS mismatch response of the outbound filter and the exact source-ID match of an SMU word stay with `sep_fabric_filter_match_priority_rand_test`. The `FEAT_CTRL` value and the inbound bypass stay with the open lifecycle tests. The test does not claim the AxUSER of a real CPU master, only that the AP and STEE remappers force OTHERS and the SMU path forces one value that is not OTHERS while the LSU bench master drives two other values. It does not claim the number of SEP's ID, AxUSER[11:4] or the W user, which no specification states.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_alias_remap_attr_rand_test]]
===== 2.28 `sep_fabric_alias_remap_attr_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (Verilator on `lsu_stub_all_live`, VCS on `--target default` per the shared Phase 3 testlist-entry rule, group `cpu_stub`, `testlists/system.toml`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 4 seeds. Tool: Verilator and VCS. VCS is required for the X check on AxCACHE at the alias remap output.

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_alias_remap_attr_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (LSU master on `s_axi`); `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (outbound responder: OKAY and zero read data for any address); the filter reference model of this bucket. Probes. Approved: the LSU response and the outbound-filter verdict (an outbound entry over the predicted address). PR-ALIAS (address and AxCACHE at the input and at the output of the local-master alias remap; an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list), for the AxCACHE checks: CHK-ALIAS-CACHE, CTRL-ALIAS-CACHE-IN and the AxCACHE part of the valid-clear control. PR-OUT is used for OBS-ALIAS-OUT-CACHE only; no check reads it. No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, RANDCFG. Dimensions: region index, start page `S`, page count (2 to 5, inside the SMU window above `0x8000_1000`), offset `O` (bits 55:12, bit 32 set, sum below 2^56), `cacheable` `c`, incoming AxCACHE `a` (from the AXI4-defined encodings `0x0`, `0x2`, `0x3` and `0xF`), and the direction order. The overlap leg also draws the region `k2` (not `k`); its offset is `O` with bit 33 inverted. Legal range per `hw/ip/axi_alias_remap/regs/gen/adoc/alias_remap.adoc` (`region_start`, `region_end`, `region_attrs`). Rejection list: `S` equal to or above `E`; overlapping valid regions (the specification states no priority); an offset carry out of bit 55 (the specification states no result); `c` equal to `a`. `reseed = 4` samples different regions, pages, offsets, `c` and `a`. The six boundary classes and the two AxCACHE corners run on every seed.

*Objective*: A transaction hits a remap region when `start_addr <= address < end_addr`, with `end_addr` non-inclusive. A hit adds the offset to address bits [55:12] and replaces all four AxCACHE bits with the `cacheable` field. A miss keeps the address and AxCACHE. The specification states pass-through after the alias remap only for AxPROT bits 0 and 2, not for AxCACHE, so AxCACHE is graded at the alias remap output (PR-ALIAS) and not at PR-OUT. The specification states no AxCACHE pass-through from the LSU port to the alias remap input, so a miss cell compares the output AxCACHE with the input AxCACHE of the same request, both on PR-ALIAS, and a hit cell compares the output AxCACHE with `c`. The address is graded through outbound-filter verdicts. Cites `hw/ip/axi_alias_remap/regs/gen/adoc/alias_remap.adoc` (`region_start`, `region_end`, `region_attrs`), `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` ("Address Remapping") and `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` ("Address Range Granule"; "Blocked Transactions").

*Test Coverage*: Six boundary classes on read and write (byte `S` - 1, byte `S`, a middle page, byte `E` - 1, byte `E`, a page above `E`), the AxCACHE override on all four bits (`c` = `0xF` over `a` = `0x0`, and `c` = `0x0` over `a` = `0xF`) on read and write, and a valid-clear run of the same region. Extends `sep_fabric_local_alias_offset_walk_test` and `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test`, which grade the hit and miss address rule, the start-hit and end-miss cells and the valid gate. Only the new cells are graded here: the byte classes at `S` - 1 and `E` - 1, a middle page and a page above `E`, the AxCACHE override on all four bits, and the six classes with `valid` 0 for an offset that carries into bit 32. The `S` and `E` classes stay as controls. The open tests keep the rest. Every hit translates to an address at or above `0x1_0000_0000`, because the offset has bit 32 set, so the hit cells are also the read and write cells of the route class at or above 4 GiB. Functional coverage: `sep_remap_alias_cg` (`cp_boundary_dir`, `cp_cacheable_override_dir`, `cp_valid_gate`, `cp_offset_expansion`) and `sep_fabric_outbound_route_cg.cp_route_class_dir` (the `ge_4g` cells).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up with `+skip_fuse_sense`. Read `FEAT_CTRL` and log it.
. Draw the region `k`, the start page `S`, the page count and so `E`, the offset `O`, `c` and `a` with `a` different from `c`. The other 15 alias regions stay invalid.
. The LSU programs alias region `k`: `region_start` = `S`, `region_end` = `E`, `region_attrs` with `valid` 1, `cacheable` `c` and `offset` `O`. The LSU reads the three registers back.
. The model computes, for each class source address `src`, the hit flag from `S` <= `src` < `E` and the translated address `((src>>12)+(O>>12))<<12 \| src[11:0]`. The classes are byte `S` - 1, byte `S`, the first byte of page `S` + 1 (the middle page), byte `E` - 1, byte `E` and the first byte of the page above `E`.
. For each class and each direction, the LSU programs one outbound entry over the 8-byte granule of the translated address (`allow_burst` 0, `src_id` 0, `allow_ns` equal to the NS that the SMU path sets for that address, the inverse of its bit 55, read and write allowed) and disables all other outbound entries. The LSU reads the entry back.
. The LSU issues a 1-byte probe (AxSIZE 0, AxLEN 0) at `src` with AxCACHE `a`. For a hit the expected response is OKAY. For a miss the expected response is DECERR. The LSU logs the response. For each OKAY response it also logs the AxCACHE at the alias remap output and at PR-OUT (OBS-ALIAS-OUT-CACHE).
. The LSU programs the entry over the 8-byte granule of `src` instead and disables the entry of step 5. The LSU reads it back and repeats the probe. For a hit the expected response is DECERR. For a miss the expected response is OKAY.
. Run steps 5 to 7 for the six classes, read and write.
. Overlap leg. Region `k` stays valid. The LSU programs a second region `k2` (drawn, not `k`) with the same `region_start` and `region_end`, `valid` 1 and the offset `O` with bit 33 inverted, and programs one outbound entry over the translated address of the middle page under `O`. The LSU reads one byte of the middle page. The alias remap output carries one AR at the middle page translated by the offset of the lower-numbered of `k` and `k2` (CHK-ALIAS-OVERLAP); the response is logged (OBS-ALIAS-OVERLAP). The LSU clears region `k2`.
. AxCACHE corners. For the middle-page hit probe, run `c` = `0xF` over `a` = `0x0` and then `c` = `0x0` over `a` = `0xF`, each as a read and as a write. The LSU reprograms `cacheable`, programs the outbound entry over the translated 8-byte granule as in step 5 and reads both back before each run. The expected PR-ALIAS value is AxCACHE equal to `c` for the hit.
. For the byte `E` miss probe, with the outbound entry over the source granule, run AxCACHE `a` as a read and as a write. The expected AxCACHE at the PR-ALIAS output equals the AxCACHE at the PR-ALIAS input of the same request.
. Valid-clear run. The LSU reprograms region `k` with the same bounds and offset, `cacheable` `c` and `valid` 0. It reads the registers back. It repeats steps 5 to 7 for the six classes, read and write. Every class is a miss.
. Valid-clear run with `cacheable` `0xF`. The LSU reprograms `cacheable` = `0xF` with `valid` 0 and repeats the middle-page probe with AxCACHE `0x0` and an outbound entry over the source granule, read and write. The model expects a miss. The expected AxCACHE at the PR-ALIAS output equals the AxCACHE at the PR-ALIAS input of the same request.
. The LSU restores alias region `k` to the reset values and disables every outbound entry.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ALIAS-CLASS
|For every class the model computes hit or miss from `start_addr <= address < end_addr` (`alias_remap.adoc`, `region_start` and `region_end`). A hit is admitted at the address `((src>>12)+(O>>12))<<12 \| src[11:0]` and refused at the source address. A miss is admitted at the source address and refused at the translated address. An inclusive end, an exclusive start or a wrong offset each flips one verdict of the pair. Anchor: the LSU responses with the outbound-filter verdict as the address anchor (approved). The valid-clear run is graded: with `valid` 0 every class is admitted at the source address and refused at the translated address, so a region with `valid` 0 does not apply the offset that carries into bit 32. Control: the `S` and `E` classes. Evidence: `CHK-ALIAS-CLASS PASS: region=<k> valid=<0\|1> class=below\|start\|mid\|end_m1\|end\|above dir=R\|W src=0x<addr> predicted=0x<addr> hit=<0\|1> hit_addr_resp=<code> src_addr_resp=<code>`.
|CHK-ALIAS-CACHE
|The AxCACHE at the alias remap output equals `c` for a hit, on all four bits (`alias_remap.adoc`, `region_attrs`: the field overrides all existing AxCACHE bits). For a miss and for the valid-clear run, the output AxCACHE equals the input AxCACHE of the same request (`fabric.adoc`, "Address Remapping": an invalid entry is a transparent pass-through). A partial AxCACHE override shows a different value on at least one bit when the input differs from `c` on all four bits. A corner cell or a valid-clear `cacheable` `0xF` cell fills its bin only when the input AxCACHE differs from `c` on all four bits; otherwise the cell is graded, logged with the input value, and fills no bin. X or Z in AxCACHE fails on VCS. Anchor: the input and output requests on PR-ALIAS (an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list). Control: the miss cells for the hit cells. Evidence: `CHK-ALIAS-CACHE PASS: region=<k> dir=R\|W cache_issued=0x<a> cache_remap_in=0x<i> cache_remap_out=0x<o> expect=0x<c\|i> hit=<0\|1>`.
|CTRL-ALIAS-CACHE-IN
|Stimulus control for CHK-ALIAS-CACHE. On every AxCACHE probe the AxCACHE at the PR-ALIAS input equals the AxCACHE the LSU issued, so each hit cell runs with the drawn input that differs from `c` and each miss cell with the drawn input. No specification states the pass-through from the LSU port to the alias remap input, so this is a control and not a check; a probe whose input differs fails the leaf as `CTRL-ALIAS-CACHE-IN FAIL` with the values of the CHK-ALIAS-CACHE line. Evidence: `CTRL-ALIAS-CACHE-IN PASS: probes=<n>; the remap input AxCACHE equals the issued AxCACHE on every probe`.
|CTRL-ALIAS-RAND
|Stimulus-completeness control. Per seed, the test runs at least one hit, one miss and one AxCACHE override. The DUT cannot fail this checker, and it makes no DUT claim. Evidence: `CTRL-ALIAS-RAND LOG: seed=<n> hits=<n> misses=<n> cache_override=<n>`.
|OBS-ALIAS-OUT-CACHE
|Logged only, no pass or fail. For each class probe that answers OKAY: the direction, the source address, the address and AxCACHE at the alias remap output (`alias_out_addr`, `alias_out_cache`) and the address and AxCACHE of the matching PR-OUT beat (`pr_out_addr`, `pr_out_cache`, `none` when no beat shows). The specification states pass-through after the alias remap for AxPROT bits 0 and 2 only.
|CHK-ALIAS-OVERLAP
|One read through two valid regions over the same pages with offsets that differ in bit 33 leaves the alias remap as one AR at the source translated by the offset of the lower-numbered region (`hw/ip/axi_alias_remap/regs/alias_remap.rdl:42`: "When more than one valid region contains the address, the lowest-numbered region applies."). The seed draws `k2` above or below `k`, so a remap that takes the higher-numbered or the last-programmed region shows the other offset and fails. The check is required evidence of the leaf; a map with fewer than two alias regions fails it. Anchor: the AR at the PR-ALIAS output. Evidence: `CHK-ALIAS-OVERLAP PASS: region_a=<k> offset_a=0x<O> region_b=<k2> offset_b=0x<O^bit33> src=0x<addr> alias_out_addr=0x<addr> n_out=1 expect=0x<addr> (region <min>)`.
|OBS-ALIAS-OVERLAP
|Logged only, no pass or fail. The response of the overlap read. The outbound entry covers the translation of region `k` only, so the response follows the outbound filter.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The class cells do not draw empty or inverted regions, or an offset with bit 32 clear, because the specification states no result for them. The overlap leg grades two overlapping regions (CHK-ALIAS-OVERLAP), and `sep_fabric_local_alias_offset_walk_test` grades an offset carry out of bit 55 (CHK-ALIAS-CARRY). The test does not grade AxCACHE at the outbound port, because the specification states pass-through after the alias remap only for AxPROT bits 0 and 2, not for AxCACHE. The AxCACHE at the outbound port is logged (OBS-ALIAS-OUT-CACHE). The hit and miss address rule at `S` and `E`, the start-hit and end-miss cells and the valid gate stay with `sep_fabric_local_alias_offset_walk_test` and `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test`.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_smc_route_test]]
===== 2.29 `sep_fabric_smc_route_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (Verilator on `lsu_stub_all_live`, VCS on `--target default` per the shared Phase 3 testlist-entry rule, group `cpu_stub`, `testlists/system.toml`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds, one testlist entry. The seed draws configuration A (base `0x4000_0000`, size `0x0100_0000`) or configuration B (base `0x9000_0000`, size `0x0100_0000`), and the leaf drives the aperture inputs through BH-SMC before it releases reset. Tool: Verilator and VCS. VCS is required for the X check on the SMC read data of CHK-SMC-ROUTE.

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_smc_route_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (LSU master on `s_axi`); `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (outbound responder: OKAY and zero read data for any address). Probes and hook. Approved: the LSU response and read data, the read of `SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS`. Added hook: BH-SMC, a top-port drive that the test adds to the port table of the HDL Top section of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` (the drive of `smc_global_base_addr_i` and `smc_region_size_i`, from plusargs or from the leaf before reset release, and an SMC responder that stores and returns written data on `sep_ext_to_smc_axi` in the `lsu_stub_all_live` target; the default bench ties both inputs to 0 and has no SMC responder in this target). PR-SMC (`sep_ext_to_smc_axi_req_o`) and PR-OUT are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, RANDCFG. Dimensions: the configuration, A or B, with equal weight; two different non-zero source IDs, AxUSER[11:4] (non-zero) and AxPROT bits 0 and 2 of each aperture access. AxPROT[1] and the source ID of each access follow a fixed pattern over each four accesses (write, read, write, read): the writes carry AxPROT[1] 0 then 1, the reads 1 then 0, and each direction carries both source IDs. The two apertures, the markers and the probe words are fixed, because the specification states no legal aperture values. The SMU aperture stays at its reset values. A seed runs the cells of the drawn configuration only, because the specification states no behaviour for an aperture change after reset and a second configuration would need a second reset. The configuration bins are seed-dependent and close on a merge of regressions (`SEP_FCOV.adoc`, fabric and remap groups); one regression of 3 seeds misses one configuration with a probability of 25 %, so no single regression claims both configurations.

*Objective*: The route demux matches the SMC aperture that the SMC supplies on `smc_global_base_addr_i` and `smc_region_size_i` first in the order. It sends that traffic to `sep_ext_to_smc_axi_req_o` without the outbound filter. The route demux order decides the route: only the aperture that the SMC supplies goes to the SMC port, so the static SMC row of the memory map (`0x4000_0000` to `0x7FFF_FFFF`) names the SMC address space and is not the selector. Requests to the SMC are issued only after `smc_fuse_sense_done` reads 1, because the register description states that they hang until then. The SMC path keeps the initiator's source ID and NS (`sep-outbound-attributes-table`, SMC aperture row), and no path changes AxPROT bits 0 and 2, so each request reaches the SMC port with the AxPROT and the whole AxUSER (all 12 bits) that the LSU issued. Cites `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` ("Fabric Topology", output fabric; "Transaction Routing" and `sep-outbound-attributes-table`), `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` (`smc_global_base_addr_i`, `smc_region_size_i`, `sep_ext_to_smc_axi_req_o`), `hw/sys/sep/doc/peripheral_subsystem.adoc`, `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc` (SMC row), `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc` (`SMU_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` and `SMU_REGION_SIZE` reset values, `SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS`), `hw/sys/sep/doc/assets/sep-output-fabric.svg` and `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` ("Blocked Transactions").

*Test Coverage*: The first and last aperture word of configuration A on read and write; the word above the aperture top, inside the memory-map row of the SMC, on read and write; and the first-match order against the overlapping reset SMU window (configuration B). A seed runs the cells of the configuration that it draws. The `rom_fw` boot tests (for example `sep_rom_non_secure_boot_test`) grade the basic configuration A route and the unfiltered path: the Boot ROM writes and reads back four patterns at an SMC scratch word through the aperture while every outbound entry is disabled, and a mismatch fails the boot. This entry extends them. Only the new cells are graded here: the aperture edge words, the word above the aperture top, the configuration B first-match order and the AxPROT and source ID of the aperture accesses on PR-SMC. The unfiltered accesses of this entry and the DECERR answer to an SMU-window read with every outbound entry disabled are graded in CHK-SMC-ROUTE: they show that the aperture bypasses the outbound filter while the filter refuses other traffic. `sep_address_map_test` reads the SMU registers only. Functional coverage: `sep_fabric_outbound_route_cg` (`cp_route_class_dir` bin `smc_aperture`, `cp_smc_cfg_class_dir`, `cp_smc_readiness`), `sep_fabric_port_silence_cp` (`sil_smc_outside_aperture`, `sil_out_on_smc_route`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw the configuration from the seed, log `INFO-CFG-SMC cfg=A|B base=0x<b> size=0x<s>`, and drive its aperture inputs through BH-SMC before reset release. Bring up. Read `FEAT_CTRL` and log it. Read `SMU_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` and `SMU_REGION_SIZE` and require their RDL reset values, so the configuration B aperture lies inside the SMU window (control `CTRL-SMC-SMU-RESET LOG: smu_base=0x<b> smu_size=0x<s> rdl_base=0x<b> rdl_size=0x<s> cfg_b_inside=1`; a mismatch fails the leaf). The test never writes them.
. Read `SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` until `smc_fuse_sense_done` is 1, in a bounded wait. A timeout fails the test. Log the number of reads. No SMC request is issued before this step ends.
. Configuration A (steps 3 to 8 run when the seed draws A). The LSU programs outbound pair 0 over `0x4000_0000` to `0xBFFF_FFFF` (one entry with `allow_ns` 0 and one with `allow_ns` 1, read and write allowed, `src_id` 0) and enables it. The LSU reads the pair back. A request that took the outbound path would pass the filter and show on PR-OUT.
. The LSU writes and reads the first aperture word `0x4000_0000` and the last aperture word `0x40FF_FFFC` with two distinct fixed markers, each access with its drawn AxPROT and AxUSER. Each read answers OKAY with its marker. PR-SMC counts one AW and one AR for each word, each with the AxPROT and AxUSER[3:0] of its access. PR-OUT shows no handshake.
. PR-OUT control. The LSU reads `0x8000_0100` (SMU window, outside the aperture). The read answers OKAY and PR-OUT captures it.
. Static-row leg, with pair 0 still enabled. Error injection at the route demux input: the LSU reads and writes `0x4100_0000`, one word above the aperture top and inside the memory-map row of the SMC. Pair 0 admits the word, so a route that sends it to the SMN passes the filter and shows a PR-OUT handshake. The route demux sends only the supplied aperture to the SMC port (`fabric.adoc`, "Fabric Topology"), so PR-SMC shows no handshake. The word lies outside the SMU window, below `0x1_0000_0000` and outside the AP and STEE regions, so the route demux returns it to the peripheral crossbar, which answers DECERR at or above `0x4000_0000` (`fabric.adoc`, output fabric and "Fabric Topology"). The expected response is DECERR on RRESP and BRESP with PR-SMC and PR-OUT silent. The leaf waits for each response in a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-SMC-STATIC-ROW.
. Unfiltered leg. The LSU disables pair 0, so all 32 outbound entries are disabled, and reads the pair back. The LSU repeats the accesses of step 4. Each answers OKAY, and PR-SMC counts it.
. Error injection at the route demux input: the LSU reads `0x8000_0100` (SMU window) with all outbound entries disabled. The expected response is DECERR on RRESP (`index.adoc`, "Blocked Transactions"). PR-OUT shows no handshake.
. Configuration B (steps 9 to 12 run when the seed draws B). The SMC aperture `0x9000_0000` to `0x90FF_FFFF` lies inside the reset SMU window `0x8000_0000` to `0xBFFF_FFFF`. After step 2, the LSU programs outbound pair 0 over `0x8000_0000` to `0xBFFF_FFFF` (same attributes as in step 3), enables it and reads it back.
. The LSU writes and reads the first aperture word `0x9000_0000` and the last aperture word `0x90FF_FFFC` with distinct fixed markers, each access with its drawn AxPROT and AxUSER. Each read answers OKAY with its marker. PR-SMC counts one AW and one AR for each word, each with the AxPROT and AxUSER[3:0] of its access. PR-OUT shows no handshake.
. PR-OUT control. The LSU reads `0x8000_0100`, which lies inside the SMU window and outside the aperture. The read answers OKAY and PR-OUT captures it.
. The LSU disables pair 0.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SMC-ROUTE
|The SMC aperture that the SMC supplies is matched first and sent to `sep_ext_to_smc_axi_req_o` without the outbound filter (`fabric.adoc`, "Fabric Topology"). In configuration A, over the accesses of step 4, the PR-SMC count equals the number of those accesses (one AW and one AR for each of the two edge words), and the PR-OUT count of these accesses is zero. Each edge-word read through the SMC responder returns the written value (a real readback), and X or Z in the read data fails on VCS. A route that misses the aperture edges, or sends aperture traffic outbound, gives a lower PR-SMC count or a non-zero PR-OUT count. Unfiltered leg: with all 32 outbound entries disabled, each access of step 7 answers OKAY, each read returns its marker and PR-SMC counts each access, and the SMU-window read of step 8 answers DECERR with no PR-OUT handshake (`index.adoc`, "Blocked Transactions"). A filter on the SMC path refuses the step 7 accesses, and a bypassed outbound filter answers OKAY to the step 8 read, so either fails the check. The `smc_fuse_sense_done` read is 1 before the first request. Anchor: PR-SMC, PR-OUT and the LSU response and read data (BH-SMC drives the aperture). Control: the SMU-window read of step 5, outside the aperture, answers OKAY, is captured on PR-OUT and shows no PR-SMC handshake; the aperture accesses of step 4 drive PR-SMC. Evidence: `CHK-SMC-ROUTE PASS: addr=0x40000000\|0x40FFFFFC smc_aw=<n> smc_ar=<n> out_seen=0 control_out=<n> control_smc=0 rdata=0x<M> wr=0x<M> unfilt_smc=<n> deny_resp=DECERR deny_out=0 fuse_done=1`.
|CHK-SMC-STATIC-ROW
|PR-SMC shows no handshake for the word above the top, on read and on write (`fabric.adoc`, "Fabric Topology": the route demux sends only the supplied aperture to the SMC port). A route that decodes the static SMC row to the SMC port shows a handshake. The word is outside the SMU window and the leg runs while outbound pair 0 admits it (step 6), so a route that sends it to the SMN passes the filter. The route demux returns it to the peripheral crossbar, which answers DECERR at or above `0x4000_0000` (`fabric.adoc`, output fabric: "every other address returns to the peripheral crossbar of the input fabric"; "Fabric Topology"): the read and the write answer DECERR and PR-OUT shows no handshake. A route that sends the word to the SMN shows a PR-OUT handshake. Anchor: PR-SMC, PR-OUT and the LSU response. Control: the aperture accesses of the same leaf drive PR-SMC and the SMU-window control read drives PR-OUT. Evidence: `CHK-SMC-STATIC-ROW PASS: above=0x41000000 dir=R\|W smc_seen=0 out_seen=0 resp=DECERR control_smc=<n> control_out=<n>`.
|CHK-SMC-FIRST-MATCH
|In configuration B the first and last aperture word reach PR-SMC and PR-OUT shows no handshake for them, and the SMU-window read outside the aperture is captured on PR-OUT and shows no PR-SMC handshake (`fabric.adoc`, "Fabric Topology": the SMC aperture is first in the order). A DUT with the SMU match ahead of the SMC match sends the aperture words outbound, so PR-SMC stays silent. The `smc_fuse_sense_done` read is 1 before the first request. Anchor: PR-SMC and PR-OUT. Control: the `0x8000_0100` read, which drives PR-OUT; the aperture words, which drive PR-SMC. Evidence: `CHK-SMC-FIRST-MATCH PASS: smc_base=0x90000000 smu_base=0x80000000 smc_seen=<n> out_seen=0 control_out=<n> control_smc=0 fuse_done=1`.
|CHK-SMC-ATTR
|In both configurations, the AW and AR beat of each aperture access on PR-SMC carries the AxPROT and the whole AxUSER (all 12 bits) that the LSU issued (`fabric.adoc`, `sep-outbound-attributes-table`: the SMC aperture keeps the initiator's source ID and NS; "No path changes ... the other AxPROT bits"). X or Z in the captured AxPROT or AxUSER fails on VCS. A path that sets NS or the source ID, changes any AxUSER bit, or changes AxPROT bit 0 or 2, gives a beat whose value differs from the issued one; AxUSER[11:4] is drawn non-zero, so a path that clears or forces the upper bits fails. Anchor: PR-SMC. Control: over the leaf each direction carries both AxPROT[1] values and two different non-zero source IDs (`CTRL-SMC-ATTR LOG`; a leaf without them fails), so a path that forces any constant NS or source ID fails one beat. Evidence: `CHK-SMC-ATTR PASS: addr=0x<a> ch=aw\|ar prot_issued=0b<p> prot_seen=0b<p> user_issued=0x<u> user_seen=0x<u>`, `CTRL-SMC-ATTR LOG: aw: prot1=[0, 1] src_ids=[<a>, <b>]; ar: prot1=[0, 1] src_ids=[<a>, <b>]`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker of the drawn configuration PASSes: CHK-SMC-ROUTE, CHK-SMC-STATIC-ROW and CHK-SMC-ATTR for configuration A, CHK-SMC-FIRST-MATCH and CHK-SMC-ATTR for configuration B. A missing check line of the drawn configuration fails the leaf. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The test grades the word above the supplied aperture in configuration A only. It does not grade the hang of a request to the SMC before `smc_fuse_sense_done` (the testbench SMC responder is the only SMC, and a hang that the responder models cannot fail the SEP RTL), or other aperture values. The SMU aperture registers are not written. Nothing here claims that the SMC fabric itself decodes the aperture.

'''''
[[sep_fabric_extension_port_window_test]]
===== 2.30 `sep_fabric_extension_port_window_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (Verilator on `lsu_stub_all_live`, VCS on `--target default` per the shared Phase 3 testlist-entry rule), real PROD fuse sense, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_extension_port_window_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (LSU splice master on `s_axi`; SI master on `m_axi`). The extension port responder of `tb_top` (`+sep_ext_port_responder`, set by the testlist entry) answers `axi_extension_axi_resp_i` with the seeded reply of each access: a B code, or an RRESP and an RDATA word for each R beat, OKAY or SLVERR, never DECERR. Approved probes: PR-XEXT (the SI request that reaches the local crossbar), the LSU and SI responses, and the register read-back. PR-EXT (`axi_extension_axi_req_o`: address, AxSIZE, AxLEN, strobe and data) and PR-EXT-RSP (`axi_extension_axi_resp_i`: B and R code, R data, RLAST) are observation taps on the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. The responder is a SEP top-port drive on the SEP_TB_ARCH HDL Top list. No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the middle word offset, the AxSIZE of each single-beat probe, the write data, the page offset of the SI burst and the port replies. Legal range: every single-beat address is aligned to its AxSIZE (the drawn offset is rounded down) and starts at or above `0x2000_0000` + `EFUSE_SHIM_SIZE`. The middle word offset is drawn at byte grain from `0x2000_0008` to `0x3FFF_FFF7`, so after the round-down it stays above the shim window and the first word and below the last word. The first word above the shim takes AxSIZE 0 to 2. The middle word and the last word `0x3FFF_FFF8` take AxSIZE 0 to 3. The burst starts 8-byte aligned inside the 4 KB page at `0x2000_1000`, and the whole burst stays in that page. `EFUSE_SHIM_SIZE` stays at its default, because the specification states no range for it. Port replies: for each initiator and direction the three single-beat probes carry OKAY, SLVERR and one code drawn from {OKAY, SLVERR}, in a seeded order, so every seed gives both codes for each initiator and direction. Each read probe takes a seeded 64-bit word. The write burst takes a seeded B code, and each of the eight read-burst beats a seeded code and word. The initiators, the directions and the address classes are fixed loops, not draws. `reseed = 3` samples different middle offsets, sizes, data, page offsets and reply orders.

*Objective*: The extension port serves `0x2000_0000` to `0x3FFF_FFFF` except the first `EFUSE_SHIM_SIZE` bytes, for the LSU and the System Interface. The eFuse shim window does not leave on the port. The System Interface may issue bursts. The SEP External row answers "Adopter-defined", which means that the adopter's implementation answers: a request that leaves on the port gets the response code and read data that the port returns, beat for beat. The port replies with codes and data that the fabric does not produce (OKAY and SLVERR with seeded data), so the leaf grades that the initiator response is the port's reply. A burst that the inbound filter refuses answers DECERR and never reaches the local crossbar or the port: the port never answers DECERR, so this DECERR is the fabric's own, and the pair of the two outcomes tells the two sources apart. Cites `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` ("Top Level AXI4"; "Fabric Topology", local fabric), `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` (`axi_extension_axi_req_o`, `axi_extension_axi_resp_i`), `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` ("External Window Reference Model"), `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc` (SEP External row, Adopter-defined), `doc/trm/src/memory_map.adoc` ("Decode Response Codes", Adopter-defined), `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` ("Blocked Transactions") and `hw/sys/sep/doc/assets/sep_axi_connectivity.svg`.

*Test Coverage*: LSU and SI, read and write, at the first word above the shim, a middle word and the last word of the window, with port replies OKAY and SLVERR. The first and last byte of the shim window. An SI INCR write burst with AxLEN 3 and an SI INCR read burst with AxLEN 7 through an entry with `allow_burst` 1. The same two bursts with the covering entry disabled. This entry extends `sep_address_map_test`, which grades the LSU decode bounds of the extension demux. Only the new cells are graded here: the System Interface initiator with reads, writes and bursts, the PR-EXT capture of the issued attributes and data, the shim window silence on PR-EXT, and the port reply at each initiator. `sep_address_map_test` keeps the LSU decode bounds. The reserved word `0x1FFF_FFFC` below the window is a cell of `sep_fabric_row_response_matrix_test`. The word `0x4000_0000` above the window is a crossbar-limit cell of `sep_fabric_inbound_rebase_test`. Functional coverage: `sep_fabric_dedicated_port_cg` (`cp_ext_init_dir_ls`, `cp_ext_addr_class`, `cp_ext_burst`, `cp_ext_port_reply`) and `sep_fabric_port_silence_cp` (`sil_ext_shim_window`, `sil_ext_filter_deny`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up with real PROD fuse sense. Read `FEAT_CTRL` and require it to equal the golden decode of the PROD image with SEP_DBG 0, so the inbound filter is active (control `CTRL-EXT-FILTER-ACTIVE LOG: feat_ctrl=0x<v> golden=0x<v> sep_dbg=0`; a mismatch fails the leaf). Require the extension port responder to be on (control `CTRL-EXT-RESPONDER LOG: on=1`; `on=0` fails the leaf).
. From the LSU, program inbound entry 0: `START_ADDR` `0x2000_0000`, `END_ADDR` `0x3FFF_FFFF`, and `FILTER_CONFIG` with `entry_enabled` 1, `read_allowed` 1, `write_allowed` 1, `allow_burst` 1, `src_id` 0 and `allow_ns` equal to AxPROT[1] of the SI requests. Read back the three registers before the first SI request.
. Draw the middle word offset, the AxSIZE of each single-beat probe, the page offset of the burst and the port replies from the seed.
. From SI, issue one single-beat read and one single-beat write to each address class: the first word above the shim, the middle word and the last word `0x3FFF_FFF8`. Before each request, set the responder to the seeded reply of that request. Keep one request outstanding. A write drives seeded data with the strobes of its AxSIZE. For each request, record the PR-XEXT handshake and address, the PR-EXT capture, the PR-EXT-RSP reply and the SI response and read data.
. From the LSU, issue the same reads and writes to the same address classes, each with its own seeded reply. For each request, record the PR-EXT capture, the PR-EXT-RSP reply and the LSU response and read data.
. Shim window. From the LSU and from SI, read and write the first byte `0x2000_0000` and the last byte `0x2000_0000` + `EFUSE_SHIM_SIZE` - 1 with AxSIZE 0. A write drives the reset value of the byte (`EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME` `0x20` at byte 0, `0x00` at byte 3), so the shim register keeps its value. Record PR-EXT for each request. Log each response and do not grade it. A response that does not arrive within a bounded wait fails the leaf.
. Shim byte write. After fuse sense, the LSU reads the shim word at `0x2000_0000`, then the LSU writes one byte of it with the old byte XOR a drawn non-zero delta, and the LSU reads the word again (CHK-EXT-SHIM-BYTE). Then the same with an SI byte write at another drawn lane.
. Shim burst. From SI, through entry 0, issue an INCR read burst and an INCR write burst with AxSIZE 3 and a drawn AxLEN 1 to 3 at `0x2000_0000`. Every write beat carries the reset value of `EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME` in its low word; the LSU then reads the shim word (CHK-EXT-SHIM-BURST).
. From SI, issue an INCR write burst with AxLEN 3 and AxSIZE 3 at the drawn start address, with all strobes set and seeded data on the four beats, after setting the seeded B code. Record the PR-XEXT handshake and address, the PR-EXT AxLEN, AxSIZE and write beats, the PR-EXT-RSP B and the SI response.
. From SI, issue an INCR read burst with AxLEN 7 and AxSIZE 3 at a drawn start address in the same page, after setting the seeded code and word of each beat. Record the PR-XEXT handshake and address, the PR-EXT AxLEN and AxSIZE, the eight PR-EXT-RSP R beats, and the RRESP and RDATA of each SI beat.
. From the LSU, disable inbound entry 0 (write `FILTER_CONFIG` with `entry_enabled` 0 and the other fields unchanged). Read `FILTER_CONFIG` back.
. Error injection at `smn_inbound_axi_req_i`: repeat the write burst of step 7 with the covering entry disabled. Expected response: DECERR on B (`hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc`, "Blocked Transactions"). PR-XEXT and PR-EXT stay silent from the request to the response.
. Error injection at `smn_inbound_axi_req_i`: repeat the read burst of step 8 with the covering entry disabled. Expected response: DECERR on every R beat until RLAST, eight beats (AxLEN + 1; IHI 0022 A3.4.1, no early burst termination). PR-XEXT and PR-EXT stay silent from the request to the last beat.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-EXT-REPLY
|The initiator receives the port's reply. For each LSU and SI single-beat request of steps 4 and 5, the response code equals the seeded reply, and a read returns the seeded 64-bit word on the byte lanes of its address and AxSIZE. For the admitted SI write burst the B code equals the seeded code. For the admitted SI read burst the RRESP of each of the eight beats (from the `m_axi` bus monitor) and the RDATA of each beat equal the seeded code and word of that beat, in order. The golden is the seeded draw, which the leaf gives the responder before the request. The responder never answers DECERR, and the seeded data never comes from the fabric error responders. Catches a fabric that forwards the request and answers the initiator with a response it makes itself (for example DECERR with `0xBADCAB1E`), a fabric that changes the code or the data of a port beat, and a beat delivered out of order. Evidence: one line per single-beat request, `CHK-EXT-REPLY PASS: init=LSU\|SI dir=R\|W class=first\|mid\|last issued=0x<addr> size=<n> port_reply=OKAY\|SLVERR init_resp=OKAY\|SLVERR want_rdata=0x<v>\|na init_rdata=0x<v>\|na`, and one line per admitted burst, `CHK-EXT-REPLY PASS: init=SI dir=W burst len=3 port_reply=<c> init_resp=<c>` and `CHK-EXT-REPLY PASS: init=SI dir=R burst len=7 beats=8 port_reply=<c,...> init_beats=<c,...> data_ok=1`.
|CHK-EXT-REACH
|Each SI single-beat request of step 4 shows one AW or AR handshake on PR-XEXT with the issued low 32 address bits. Catches a filter or crossbar that does not forward the request and a wrong address. A response that does not arrive within a bounded wait fails the leaf. Control: PR-XEXT shows handshakes in the admitted legs, and CHK-EXT-BURST shows it silent for the refused burst. Evidence: one line per request, `CHK-EXT-REACH PASS: dir=R\|W class=first\|mid\|last issued=0x<addr> xbar_seen=0x<addr>`.
|CHK-EXT-PORT
|PR-EXT shows for each LSU and SI request of steps 4 and 5 one request with the issued address, the issued AxSIZE, AxLEN 0, the issued strobe and, for a write, the issued data on the strobed lanes. The port returns exactly one known reply beat (PR-EXT-RSP B or R) before the initiator response. Catches a wrong window decode, a request that leaves with other attributes or data, a request that leaves twice, and an initiator response that comes before the port reply. The code and word of that beat are the responder's own output and are checked by CTRL-EXT-RSP-DRIVE. Evidence: `CHK-EXT-PORT PASS: init=LSU\|SI dir=R\|W issued=0x<addr> seen=0x<addr> size=<n> len=0 strb=0x<s> data_ok=<0\|1\|na> port_resp=OKAY\|SLVERR port_rdata=0x<v>\|na t_port=<t>ps t_init=<t>ps`.
|CHK-EXT-SHIM
|The first and last byte of the shim window give no PR-EXT capture, for either initiator and either direction. Catches a shim window that leaks onto the port. Control: the first word above the shim of steps 4 and 5 gives a capture on PR-EXT in the same leaf. A leaf whose control gives no capture fails. Evidence: `CHK-EXT-SHIM PASS: first=0x20000000 last=0x<addr> seen=0 next_word_seen=<n>`.
|CHK-EXT-BURST
|The admitted write burst and the admitted read burst each show a PR-XEXT handshake at the start address. PR-EXT shows one B for the write and eight R beats, RLAST on the eighth only, for the read, each before the SI response (their codes and words are checked by CTRL-EXT-RSP-DRIVE). A fabric that answers the burst itself before the port shows no port reply before the SI response. The two bursts with the entry disabled answer DECERR at SI, on B and on all eight R beats, and PR-XEXT and PR-EXT stay silent for each. Catches a filter that passes a burst with no enabled entry. Control: the admitted burst of the same shape in the same leaf, which shows PR-XEXT alive. Evidence: `CHK-EXT-BURST PASS: dir=W len=3 xbar_seen=1 init_resp=<c> denied_resp=DECERR denied_xbar_seen=0 beats_seen=4 data_ok=1 denied_ext_seen=0 port_reply=<c> port_reply_beats=1` and `CHK-EXT-BURST PASS: dir=R len=7 xbar_seen=1 init_beats=<c,...> denied_resp=DECERR denied_xbar_seen=0 admitted_beats=8 denied_beats=8 denied_ext_seen=0 port_reply=<c,...> port_reply_beats=8`. The check also grades the PR-EXT part: PR-EXT shows the issued AxLEN and AxSIZE and, for the write, every beat with the issued data.
|CHK-EXT-SHIM-BYTE
|For an LSU and an SI one-byte write to the shim word, the LSU read after equals the read before with only the written byte replaced by the written value (whole 32-bit word; `memory_map.adoc:154-157`, the shim window is forwarded unchanged on `efuse_bank_ctrl` and not out the external port), the read after differs from the read before, and PR-EXT and PR-EXT-RSP show no capture over the three accesses. Catches a dropped or widened strobe, a shifted lane, a lost write and a shim access sent out the port.
|CHK-EXT-SHIM-BURST
|An SI INCR burst that starts at the shim window shows exactly one PR-XEXT handshake at the issued address and no PR-EXT or PR-EXT-RSP capture (`doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc:142-144`: SEP routes a burst on its first address, so its later beats go to the shim). The read burst returns AxLEN + 1 beats and the low word of beat 0 equals the last LSU read of the shim word. After the write burst the shim word reads the reset value, and it read another value before. Catches per-beat decode that sends later beats to the port, a burst answered inside SEP, a dropped burst and an extra crossbar request.
|OBS-EXT-SHIM-BYTE-RESP
|Logged only, no pass or fail. The response code of the shim reads and byte writes. The shim response is adopter-owned, and sub-word access to shim registers is an open specification question.
|OBS-EXT-SHIM-BURST-RESP
|Logged only, no pass or fail. The response codes of the shim bursts and the data of the read beats after beat 0, which the specification leaves open.
|CTRL-EXT-RSP-DRIVE
|Bench control, not a DUT check. Each PR-EXT-RSP beat of the single-beat requests and of the admitted bursts carries the code and, for a read, the word that the leaf set on the responder before the request. A responder that does not give the seeded reply fails the leaf, so CHK-EXT-REPLY never grades an unseeded golden. The DUT cannot fail this control: the net is driven by the bench.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Functional coverage*: owner window 230. The leaf runs every leg twice: pass 1 with the window closed, graded; pass 2, after pass 1 passed, inside the window and graded again. A FAIL in pass 1 ends the leaf before pass 2. `cp_shim_byte_write` and `cp_shim_burst_route` depend on no response code.

*Scope limit*: The leaf does not grade the DECERR code of the extension port, because the responder answers OKAY and SLVERR only; DECERR at the initiator is graded only as the fabric refusal of CHK-EXT-BURST. It does not grade the response of the eFuse shim window, which it logs. It does not grade the LSU decode bounds of the extension demux (`sep_address_map_test`), the reserved word `0x1FFF_FFFC` (`sep_fabric_row_response_matrix_test`) or the word `0x4000_0000` (`sep_fabric_inbound_rebase_test`). DMA access to the port is graded by `sep_fabric_dma_endpoint_matrix_test`. The CPU debug master has no stimulus path in this run mode.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_row_response_matrix_test]]
===== 2.31 `sep_fabric_row_response_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (Verilator on `lsu_stub_all_live`, VCS on `--target default` per the shared Phase 3 testlist-entry rule), real PROD fuse sense, 4 seeds. The Key Manager leg holds KM in reset with no KM image. VCS is required for the X check.

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_row_response_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (LSU splice master on `s_axi`; SI master on `m_axi`). The expected cells come from the generated memory-map table, the `ocah_*` annotations of `sep_memory_map.rdl` and the register allocation of the generated IP-XACT (`sep_reg_meta`). Approved probes: the SI and LSU responses and read data, and the LSU read-back of live and neighbour registers. The test adds no observation tap. The attribution of a DECERR to the decode and not to the filter rests on the control that the decoded registers answer OKAY through the same entry. No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed. Every seed walks every cell of the inventory, so row, cell kind, direction, initiator and the four OTBN AxCACHE values are fixed loops. The dimensions are the offset inside each cell, the row order and AxID (non-zero), and the start and AxLEN of the crypto-window INCR bursts. The SI live-word read also draws AxPROT[2], AxUSER, AxCACHE, AxQOS, AxREGION and AxLOCK, and the logged legs draw their own words, staged values and source IDs. Every graded single-beat cell uses AxSIZE 2 with address bit 2 clear and with address bit 2 set, except a live-word cell, which uses the address of its word. AxSIZE 3 is logged and not graded. AxSIZE 0 and 1 serve only the no-alias compare in the reset-control gaps. The burst leg walks every crypto window, start kind and direction, and draws the start address and AxLEN (INCR, AxSIZE 3, AxLEN 1 to 255, the whole burst inside one 4 KB page). Legal range per `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`. Rejection list: Forwarded rows (AP, STEE); adopter-defined cells (TRNG, SEP External); the two reserved CPU rows (no stated response); bursts outside the burst leg and bursts to any register region other than the crypto region (no stated response); burst starts on a past-extent word or a Reserved row (their single-beat answer is DECERR as well, so a burst there cannot show the burst rule); any SEP_DBG-bit draw; the cells that open tests grade (listed under Test Coverage). The read burst must return all of its AxLEN + 1 beats, because AXI does not support early burst termination (IHI 0022 A3.4.1). `reseed = 4` samples different offsets, orders and IDs over the same cell set. Every seed walks the whole inventory, so more seeds only repeat the cells.

*Objective*: Each memory-map row answers as its cells state. The table states the cells as seen at the inbound AXI port, so every Reserved, Past Extent and Hole in Extent cell of a row that the inbound port reaches is graded through SI behind an enabled entry over the row. The reset-control rows state what the SEP CPU sees, so the reset-control gaps, tails and past-extent cells are graded from the LSU only. The inbound port answers reset control with DECERR and states no read data. The table states the cells for a 32-bit access, so the code and data of a cell are graded for AxSIZE 2 in either half of the 64-bit data bus. An AxSIZE 3 access is logged and not graded. A Reserved row answers DECERR with read data `0xBADCAB1E` and DECERR on a write. Past the decoded extent a row answers its Past Extent cell. An unbacked offset inside the extent answers its Hole in Extent cell: SLVERR with `0xFFFFFFFF` for OTBN, HMAC and KMAC; OKAY with 0 for the entropy source and ABR (read 0, write discarded). A live word of the extent answers OKAY with the register value. An OTBN register access is served with AxCACHE[1] = 0 (non-modifiable) and with AxCACHE[1] = 1 (modifiable), because the crypto interconnect applies a modifiable-bit fix before the data-width converter (`hw/sys/sep/doc/otbn.adoc`, "OCAH Modifications"). The values `0x0`, `0x2`, `0x3` and `0xF` are chosen test values. The reset controller gaps and tails answer DECERR with `0xBADCAB1E` and do not alias onto a live register. In the Key Manager mailbox host window only the seven registers at offsets `0x00` to `0x18` decode; every other offset and the reserved span answer DECERR with `0xBADCAB1E`. A non-zero AxLEN to the crypto region `0x1090_0000` to `0x1094_FFFF`, whatever the address, is answered with DECERR on every beat, and no beat reaches an accelerator. An inbound entry that covers a register region with `allow_burst` 1 admits bursts that the target rejects, so the DECERR comes from the crypto demux and not from the filter. Cites `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc` (Hole in Extent and Past Extent columns), `hw/sys/sep/regs/include/sep_memory_map.rdl` (`ocah_*` annotations), `doc/trm/src/memory_map.adoc` ("Decode Response Codes"), `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` ("Memory Map"; "Single-Beat Register Access"), `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` ("Fabric Topology"; "Filtering and Protection"), `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` ("Single-Beat Access Only"), `hw/sys/sep/doc/otbn.adoc` ("OCAH Modifications"), `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_mailbox_sep.adoc`, `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc` (`SW_RESET_N`) and `hw/sys/sep/doc/assets/sep_axi_connectivity.svg`.

*Test Coverage*: The 8 Reserved rows with bases `0x1005_0000`, `0x1080_4000`, `0x1091_4000`, `0x1092_1000`, `0x1093_0600`, `0x1096_0000`, `0x10C0_0000` and `0x1200_0000` (DECERR, `0xBADCAB1E`), each with its first word, last word and a drawn word. The last word of the row at `0x1200_0000`, `0x1FFF_FFFC`, is the word below the extension window. The hole-in-extent cells of OTBN, HMAC and KMAC from SI (SLVERR, `0xFFFFFFFF`, both halves, reads and writes; `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` keeps the LSU single read), of the entropy source (OKAY, 0) and of a drawn ABR hole word with a 32-bit access from SI (OKAY, 0, write discarded). The past-extent cells of the DMA, watchdog, OTBN, AES, CSRNG, EDN, entropy source and lifecycle controller (DECERR) from SI. The live-word cell of each of these rows (DMA, watchdog, reset control, OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, CSRNG, EDN, entropy source, lifecycle controller, KM mailbox and ABR): the highest-offset register of the extent that is stable, has no read side effect and has an RDL reset value on every field; for the entropy source and ABR, whose holes answer OKAY with 0, it is the highest-offset such register with a non-zero reset value. RMOD, FIFO, window and hardware-updated words are excluded. The leaf logs the chosen word per row. The reset-control gaps, tails, past-extent cells and the adjacent Reserved row from the LSU, with AxSIZE 0 and 1 for the no-alias compare. The no-alias references HMAC `CFG`, one KMAC `PREFIX` word and `SW_RESET_N`. The OTBN host access with AxCACHE `0x0`, `0x2`, `0x3` and `0xF` on both channels. The Key Manager mailbox window from SI with KM held in reset. The SI write and read bursts to the crypto windows through an entry with `allow_burst` 1, starting on an in-extent register and on a hole, each with a single-beat control. The burst leg shares the rows, the initiator, the entry and the run mode of the single-beat legs, so it is a leg of this test. Other tests grade the cells below, and this entry does not repeat them. `sep_unmapped_access_policy_test` grades the exact code and data of the Reserved rows at `0x10A1_0280`, `0x10A1_0380`, `0x10A2_0400`, `0x10A2_1200` and `0x10A3_2000`, the two scratch gap Reserved rows, the alias remap bank, filter bank and SEP CPU control hole and past-extent cells, the eFuse control and token past-extent cells and the SPI gaps and tails. `sep_address_map_test` reads one word in every LSU-reachable block. `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` grades the past-extent refusal and the no-alias compare of the DMA, watchdog, AES, OTBN, ABR, CSRNG, EDN and entropy source windows, and the crypto-region burst refusal from the LSU with a single-beat control. `sep_abr_access_size_test` grades the ABR past-extent cells and, with a 64-bit access from the LSU, the hole word above the ABR error counter. `sep_axi_map_refuse_test` grades refusal only for every reserved row. `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` grades the DMA hole cells and the LSU single read that completes SLVERR at the HMAC CSR and FIFO gap, the KMAC gap and the OTBN CSR and IMEM gap. `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test` grades the entropy pool cells. `sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test` grades SI access to the boot ROM, the reset controller and the ICCM. The Phase 3 tests `sep_pic_reg_rand_test` and `sep_mailbox_access_error_inject_test` own the PIC rows and the mailbox-wrapper cells. This entry adds only: the exact code and data of the other 8 Reserved rows, the exact code and data of the past-extent cells from SI (from the LSU for reset control), the live-word cell of each row, the hole-in-extent cells of OTBN, HMAC and KMAC from SI (the System Interface initiator, the `0xFFFF_FFFF` data in both halves of the word, and the writes), the hole-in-extent cell of the entropy source, a drawn ABR hole word with a 32-bit access from SI, the Key Manager mailbox window, the no-alias compare of the reset controller, HMAC and KMAC, and the System Interface burst to the crypto region through an entry that admits bursts. The open owner keeps every other cell. Functional coverage: `sep_aperture_reserved_access_cg` (`cp_row_cell`, `cp_row_init_dir`, `cp_aperture_offset_dir`, `cp_non_aliasing`), `sep_crypto_bus_access_cg` (`cp_otbn_cache_reg`) and `sep_fabric_inbound_aperture_cg` (`cp_burst_region`). The existing `sep_axi_decode_cg` (`cp_resp_wr`, `cp_resp_rd`) and `sep_inbound_filter_allow_cg` fill from the OKAY cells.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up with real PROD fuse sense. Read `FEAT_CTRL` and log it. Leave `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 at 0, so KM stays in reset, and load no KM image.
. Build the cell inventory from the generated memory-map table, the `ocah_*` annotations of `sep_memory_map.rdl` and the register allocation of the generated IP-XACT. Every cell is one (row, cell kind, direction, initiator) tuple. The burst cells are (crypto window, start kind, direction) tuples. The inventory excludes the Forwarded and adopter-defined cells and every cell that the Test Coverage section assigns to another test.
. From the LSU, program inbound entry 0: `START_ADDR` `0x1000_0000`, `END_ADDR` `0x1FFF_FFFF`, and `FILTER_CONFIG` with `entry_enabled` 1, `read_allowed` 1, `write_allowed` 1, `allow_burst` 1, `src_id` 0 and `allow_ns` equal to AxPROT[1] of the SI requests. Read the three registers back before the first SI request: `FILTER_CONFIG` equals the written fields, and `START_ADDR` and `END_ADDR` equal `0x1000_0000` and `0x1FFF_FFFF`, or the leaf fails as a failed control (`CTRL-ROW-FILTER-RANGE: rb_start=0x10000000 rb_end=0x1fffffff want_start=0x10000000 want_end=0x1fffffff`). So the entry covers every Reserved row up to `0x1FFF_FFFF`, and a DECERR there is the decode and not the filter. Every SI access of this test goes through this entry.
. Reset-control leg, from the LSU. Read `SW_RESET_N` (`0x1080_3000`) at AxSIZE 2. Its field bits [6:0] read the RDL reset value `0x7E`, and this value is the reference. Bits [31:7] are Reserved with no stated read value, and the leaf logs them. Then, per direction, access the pairs `0x1080_3008` and `0x1080_300C`, a drawn 8-byte-aligned pair in `0x1080_300C` to `0x1080_3FFC`, `0x1080_3FF8` and `0x1080_3FFC`, and `0x1080_4000` and `0x1080_4004` at AxSIZE 2. Error injection at the decode of the row: each of these is a past-extent or reserved address. Expected response: DECERR, with read data `0xBADCAB1E` on a read. Log the AxSIZE 3 access of each cell and do not grade it.
. Reset-control control, from the LSU, before the no-alias draws. Write `SW_RESET_N` = `0x76` (bit 3, `hmac_sw_rst_n`, cleared) and read it back. Write `SW_RESET_N` = `0x7E` and read it back. This pair restores the reset value, so KM stays in reset and HMAC runs again.
. No-alias draws on the reset controller, from the LSU. Write the gap `0x1080_3008` to `0x1080_3FFF` at drawn addresses with AxSIZE 0, 1, 2 and 3. The AxSIZE 0 and 1 draws use 4-byte-aligned addresses, so their data lies on the byte lanes of bits [6:0]. Error injection at the decode of the row. Expected response: DECERR on every write. Each write drives data whose bits [6:0] are the bitwise complement of the reference (`0x01`) and seeded data above bit 6. After each write, read `SW_RESET_N`. Grade the DECERR response of the AxSIZE 2 draws under CHK-ROW. Log the response of the AxSIZE 0, 1 and 3 draws and do not grade it; those draws serve only the no-alias compare.
. Read HMAC `CFG` from the LSU until it answers OKAY, in a bounded wait. A timeout fails the leaf. The wait ends the isolation and reset of the HMAC domain before the next write.
. From the LSU, program the no-alias references: HMAC `CFG` and one KMAC `PREFIX` word, each at a seeded value that differs from its reset value. Read both back.
. OTBN host access, from the LSU. For each AxCACHE value in the fixed loop `0x0`, `0x2`, `0x3`, `0xF`, drive that value on AR and on AW. Read `INTR_ENABLE`, write it with the inverse of bit 0 of the value read, read it back and read `STATUS`. AxCACHE[1] is 0 for `0x0` and 1 for the other three, so each channel carries both values of AxCACHE[1].
. Reserved rows, from SI. For each of the 8 rows and each direction, access three 8-byte words: the first, the last and a drawn word. Access each word at AxSIZE 2 at its low half (address bit 2 clear) and at its high half (bit 2 set). A write drives seeded data. Error injection at the decode of the row. Expected response: DECERR on every access, with read data `0xBADCAB1E` on a read. Log the AxSIZE 3 access of each word and do not grade it.
. Hole-write control, from the LSU, before the hole accesses of each unit of step 12. Pick the neighbour register of the unit (a stable register of the same unit with no read side effect; HMAC `CFG` and the KMAC `PREFIX` word are the references) and read it. Write the bitwise complement of the value read to it as an in-extent write, read it back (the read must change) and restore the value read. A neighbour whose read does not change fails the leaf before any hole access of the unit. The control logs `CTRL-ROW-HOLE LOG: unit=<u> neighbour=0x<a> wrote=0x<w> read=0x<r> before=0x<b>`.
. Hole-in-extent cells, from SI. For each of OTBN, HMAC, KMAC, the entropy source and ABR, draw an 8-byte word whose two halves are both holes, and use the neighbour register and its value read in step 11. Read both halves of the hole at AxSIZE 2. Write both halves at AxSIZE 2 with the bitwise complement of the neighbour value. Read the neighbour from the LSU again. Error injection at the decode of the row. Expected response: SLVERR with `0xFFFFFFFF` on a read and SLVERR on a write for OTBN, HMAC and KMAC. OKAY with read data 0 on a read and OKAY on a write for the entropy source and ABR. Log the AxSIZE 3 access of each word and do not grade it. For ABR, after the hole accesses, write the complement to the neighbour again with the window closed, read its live word as step 14 does and restore the value.
. Past-extent cells, from SI. For each of the DMA, watchdog, OTBN, AES, CSRNG, EDN, entropy source and lifecycle controller, and each direction, access the pair E and E + 4, where E is the first byte past the decoded extent, and a drawn 8-byte-aligned pair above E + 4 inside the aperture, at AxSIZE 2. Error injection at the decode of the row. Expected response: DECERR on every access, with read data `0xBADCAB1E` on a read. Log the AxSIZE 3 access of each cell and do not grade it.
. Live-word cells. For each row of the Test Coverage list except reset control, read the chosen live word from the LSU, read it from SI at AxSIZE 2, then read it from the LSU again. The SI read of each live word draws AxPROT[2], AxUSER (12 bits), AxCACHE, AxQOS, AxREGION and AxLOCK; the CHK-ROW live-word line carries them. The model gives the expected value of the RDL field bits of the word: their RDL reset value, or the value that an earlier step of the leaf left in them. The Reserved bits of the word are logged. For reset control, the live word is the `SW_RESET_N` read of step 4.
. Key Manager mailbox, from SI, with KM held in reset. Read `SEP_STATUS`, `SEP_IRQ_STATUS`, `SEP_CTRL` and `SEP_IRQ_ENABLE` as the baseline. Read `SEP_WRITE_SEPARATOR`. Do not access `SEP_WRITE_DATA` or `SEP_READ_DATA`. Then, per direction, access the pairs at offsets `0x1C` and `0x20`, `0xFF8` and `0xFFC`, drawn pairs in `0x1C` to `0xFFC`, and drawn pairs in `0x1092_1000` to `0x1092_FFFC`. Read the baseline again after each access. Error injection at the decode of the row. Expected response: DECERR on every access, with read data `0xBADCAB1E` on a read.
