Baseline control: after the last access, SI writes `SEP_IRQ_ENABLE` = `0x1C` (the three sticky-event enables; reset value 0), reads it back, writes 0 and reads it back.
. Burst leg, from SI, for each cell: each of the OTBN, HMAC, KMAC, entropy source and ABR windows, each start kind (a stable RW register at an 8-byte-aligned address, and a hole) and each direction. Draw the start address and AxLEN. From the LSU, read the snapshot of every stable register of the touched window that has no read side effect.
. Error injection at `smn_inbound_axi_req_i`, for the same cell: issue the INCR burst with AxSIZE 3 and the drawn AxLEN. Each write beat drives the bitwise complement of the snapshot words at its address, and seeded data where no snapshot word exists. Expected response: DECERR on the single B or on every R beat.
. For a write burst, read every snapshot register from the LSU again.
. Control, for the same cell: issue a single-beat SI access with AxSIZE 2 at the start address. For a register, write the complement of its snapshot value, read it back, and restore the snapshot value. For a hole, read it and write it.
. Entropy pool, from SI through entry 0. Read `STATUS` with one 64-bit access (OKAY). Write a register word and an unmapped word of the pool (CHK-ROW-POOL-W). Read both halves of a drawn unmapped 8-byte word with 32-bit reads (CHK-ROW-POOL-UNMAPPED).
. SPI host burst across the extent end, from SI, every seed. Stage `CSID`, `ERROR_ENABLE` and `EVENT_ENABLE`. Issue INCR read and write bursts of 32-bit beats that start one and two beats below the extent end (`0x10B0_0038`) and run a drawn 1 to 8 beats past it inside one 4 KB page (CHK-ROW-SPI-BURST). Control: the same burst wholly inside the extent (CHK-ROW-SPI-BURST-CONTROL).
. After the graded window closes, three more legs run. Burst past the register extent: from SI, one 2-beat INCR read and one 2-beat INCR write at the last register word of the DMA, watchdog and CPU-control rows. Each beat of the read is logged with its response, the write data is the LSU value of the word with a seeded upper half, and the word is read from the LSU before and after; the read after uses an AXI timeout of `WDT_CDC_CLKS` watchdog clock periods, because a watchdog register read waits for the earlier write to land in the `clk_wdt_i` domain (OBS-ROW-BURST). Fabric CSR leg: for one inbound-filter entry, one alias remap region, one AP remap region and the STEE remap, the LSU stages a seeded value and reads it back, SI reads the same word and the LSU restores the old value; the SI reads of the inbound-filter and alias remap words are graded (CHK-ROW-FABRIC-CSR) and the AP and STEE remap words are logged (OBS-ROW-FABRIC-CSR); after the STEE remap word, SI reads the inbound-filter entry 1 `START_ADDR` word through the wildcard entry 0, then with entry 0 set to a drawn non-zero `src_id` reads it with the AxUSER equal to and different from that `src_id` (CHK-ROW-FILTER-SRC); and for the lifecycle `FEAT_CTRL` SI reads both 32-bit halves (CHK-ROW-FABRIC-CSR). For the source-ID cell the LSU first stages a drawn non-zero value in the inbound-filter entry 1 `START_ADDR` word and restores it after the three SI reads (CHK-ROW-FILTER-SRC). The two SI reads of `FEAT_CTRL` are watched at the local crossbar's `ext` initiator. Sub-word leg: the LSU reads and writes one byte at byte offset 1 and one halfword at byte offset 2 of a scratch word, a watchdog threshold word, `SEP_NMI_VEC`, the SPI `CSID` and `CONFIGOPTS` words, a Key Manager mailbox interrupt-enable word and the OTBN IMEM word, with the complement of the old value, and of the eFuse shadow word, `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL` and `SW_RESET_N`, with the old value; the word is read back and restored (OBS-REG-SUBWORD). For the watchdog threshold word the read responses and the write responses are graded (CHK-REG-SUBWORD-WDT).
. At the end of each seed, compare the number of cells walked with the inventory count for each row, cell kind, direction and initiator, the burst cells included.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ROW
|The response code and the addressed data lane equal the cell of the generated table, for every SI cell and for the LSU reset-control cells, at AxSIZE 2. Each non-live cell is accessed with address bit 2 clear and set. A live-word cell is accessed at its own address. X or Z in an error payload fails on VCS. After a hole write, the neighbour register of step 12 reads unchanged, and the write data is the bitwise complement of the neighbour value. The live-word cell answers OKAY, and its RDL field bits equal the expected value of the model (the RDL reset value of the fields, or the value that an earlier step left in them). The Reserved bits of the word have no stated read value, so they are masked out of the compare and logged. The two LSU reads of the word are preconditions: the field bits of each equal the expected value, or the check fails. For the entropy source and ABR the expected field value is non-zero, so an SI read that decodes to a hole (OKAY, 0) fails. The reset-control live word answers OKAY with `0x7E` on bits [6:0], its RDL reset value. Every register compare of this check and of CHK-ROW-NOALIAS, CHK-ROW-CACHE, CHK-ROW-KM, CHK-ROW-BURST and CHK-ROW-BURST-CONTROL (neighbour, reference, `INTR_ENABLE` bit 0, baseline, snapshot and read-back) uses the RDL field bits only. Catches a wrong code, a wrong payload, a hole write that lands and a live word that does not return its value. Anchor: the SI and LSU responses and read data, and the LSU read of the neighbour. Control: the in-extent write of step 11 to the same neighbour changes its read before the hole accesses of the unit, and the live-word cell of the same row answers OKAY. Evidence: `CHK-ROW PASS: row=<unit> kind=<cell> init=LSU\|SI dir=R\|W size=2 addr=0x<addr> wdata=0x<w>\|na resp=<code> rdata=0x<value> expect=<code>/0x<value>`; a live-word line adds `field_mask=0x<m> lsu_before=0x<v> lsu_after=0x<v> rsvd=0x<r>`. Each AxSIZE 3 access logs `row=<unit> kind=<cell> size=3 resp=<code> rdata=0x<value> graded=0` at info level.
|CTRL-ROW-RAND
|Stimulus-completeness control. Per seed, the number of cells walked equals the inventory count for each row, cell kind, direction and initiator, the burst cells included. A missing cell fails. The DUT cannot fail this checker, and it makes no DUT claim. Evidence: `CTRL-ROW-RAND LOG: seed=<n> walked=<n> inventory=<n> rows=<n> reserved=<n> past=<n> hole=<n> live=<n> si=<n> burst=<n>`.
|CHK-ROW-NOALIAS
|Each live reference register (HMAC `CFG`, the KMAC `PREFIX` word and `SW_RESET_N`) keeps its value after every write draw. Every draw writes data that differs from the reference value on every watched bit: bits [6:0] for `SW_RESET_N`, and the writable bits of the RDL for HMAC `CFG` and the KMAC `PREFIX` word. The draws are the gap and tail writes of step 6 and the hole writes of step 12. Catches a gap or hole write that aliases onto a live register. Anchor: the LSU read of the reference after each draw. Control: an in-extent write to the same register changes its read (steps 5 and 11, before the draws of that register; `CTRL-ROW-NOALIAS LOG: unit=<u> wrote=0x<w> read=0x<r>`). A leaf whose control does not change the read fails before any draw of that register. Evidence: `CHK-ROW-NOALIAS PASS: unit=<name> off=0x<o> live=0x<a> before=0x<b> after=0x<b> wdata=0x<w>`.
|CHK-ROW-CACHE
|An OTBN register access from the LSU answers OKAY on both channels with AxCACHE[1] = 0 and with AxCACHE[1] = 1 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/otbn.adoc`, "OCAH Modifications"). The chosen values `0x0`, `0x2`, `0x3` and `0xF` carry both values of AxCACHE[1] on each channel, and the `INTR_ENABLE` read-back equals the written value. The written value is the inverse of the value read before it, so a write that does not land fails. The AxCACHE of each AR and AW handshake of the instance is captured on the LSU port (`s_axi_arcache`, `s_axi_awcache`) and must equal the value of the instance, so a request that does not carry the value fails. Catches a host port that refuses a modifiable or non-modifiable access, and a stimulus that never presents one of the two AxCACHE[1] values. Anchor: the LSU responses, the captured AxCACHE and the read-back. Evidence: `CHK-ROW-CACHE PASS: init=LSU cache=0x<c> bus arcache=['0x<c>', '0x<c>', '0x<c>'] awcache=0x<c> rresp=OKAY bresp=OKAY readback=0x<v>`.
|CHK-ROW-KM
|Every Key Manager past-extent and reserved access answers DECERR with `0xBADCAB1E` on a read and DECERR on a write, and the baseline registers read the same after each access. Catches a mailbox window that decodes offsets past its seven registers and an access that disturbs a baseline register. Anchor: the SI responses and the baseline reads. Control: the five registers without a FIFO side effect answer OKAY through the same entry, and the baseline control write of `SEP_IRQ_ENABLE` reads back `0x1C`, so a baseline register can change while KM is held in reset (`reset_controller.adoc`, section Resetting the Key Manager: the SEP-facing side of the mailbox is in the Key Manager cold domain, so the SEP host can still reach the mailbox during the reset). A leaf whose baseline control does not read back `0x1C` fails. Evidence: `CHK-ROW-KM PASS: off=0x<o> dir=R\|W resp=DECERR rdata=0xbadcab1e baseline_same=1 baseline_ctl wrote=0x1c/read=0x1c wrote=0x0/read=0x0`.
|CHK-ROW-BURST
|Every SI write burst of step 17 answers DECERR on B, and every SI read burst returns AxLEN + 1 R beats, each DECERR (IHI 0022 A3.4.1: no early burst termination). After a write burst, every snapshot register equals its snapshot from before the burst. The burst write data is the bitwise complement of the snapshot, so a landed beat shows as a changed register. Catches a demux that forwards a burst or its first beat. Anchor: the SI response of each beat and the LSU read of the touched window. Control: CHK-ROW-BURST-CONTROL. The count of DECERR beats is logged. Evidence: `CHK-ROW-BURST PASS: init=SI addr=0x<addr> kind=reg\|hole len=<n> dir=W\|R resp=DECERR beats_decerr=<n> wdata=0x<w>\|na regs_compared=<n> changed=0`.
|CHK-ROW-BURST-CONTROL
|The single-beat SI access of step 19 with AxSIZE 2 at the same start address, through the same entry, answers its 32-bit cell: OKAY for a register (the write lands and reads back, then the snapshot value is restored), and SLVERR with `0xFFFFFFFF` or OKAY with 0 for a hole. The single-beat answer differs from DECERR, so the DECERR of CHK-ROW-BURST comes from the crypto demux and not from the filter, and a dead path cannot pass. Evidence: `CHK-ROW-BURST-CONTROL PASS: init=SI addr=0x<addr> size=2 kind=reg\|hole single_resp=<OKAY\|SLVERR> single_rdata=0x<value>`.
|CHK-ROW-POOL-W
|An SI write to the entropy pool, at a register word and at an unmapped word, answers SLVERR (`entropy_pool.adoc:38-39`). A 64-bit `STATUS` read answers OKAY first, so the row is live.
|CHK-ROW-POOL-UNMAPPED
|An SI 32-bit read of each half of an unmapped pool word answers SLVERR with zero data in the addressed lane (`entropy_pool.adoc:47-48`). Limit: the pool is empty in this leaf and an empty `DATA` read also answers SLVERR, so the check cannot tell an alias onto `DATA` from the unmapped cell.
|CHK-ROW-SPI-BURST
|For an SI read burst that crosses the SPI host extent end, every in-extent beat answers OKAY with the staged or RDL reset value, and every beat past the extent answers not OKAY and returns no staged live word; for the write burst the in-extent beat lands, no other register changes and the B is not OKAY (`peripheral_subsystem.adoc:43-46`: the error responder of `sep_io` answers only the beats past the extent; extent `memory_map.adoc:81`). The AR or AW is one burst of the issued shape. Catches a refused burst, past beats answered OKAY or aliased onto a live register, early termination and a split burst.
|CHK-ROW-SPI-BURST-CONTROL
|The same burst wholly inside the SPI host extent answers OKAY on every beat with the staged values, and the write lands. A DUT that refuses every SPI burst fails here.
|OBS-ROW-SPI-BURST-CODE
|Logged only, no pass or fail. The exact response and read data of each past-extent beat and the B code of the crossing write. The code of a past-extent burst beat is an open specification question.
|CHK-SI-ID-ECHO
|Every SI access of the leaf, OKAY and error rows alike, answers with the drawn non-zero AxID on RID (read) or BID (write), sampled from the `m_axi` response handshake. A response carries the ID of its request (AMBA AXI IHI 0022), and the fabric retains the AXI IDs needed for responses across the AXI4-Lite bridge (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc:22-23`). The check also fails on an SI access, the logged burst leg included, that returns no response, and when no SI access answered. Evidence: `CHK-SI-ID-ECHO PASS: <n> SI accesses answered BID/RID equal to the drawn AxID`.
|OBS-ROW-BURST
|Logged only, no pass or fail. For a 2-beat INCR SI read and write that start at the last register word of the DMA, watchdog and CPU-control rows: the start address, the RRESP of each beat, the overall read response, the B response, the read data, and the LSU read of the word before and after the write.
|OBS-REG-SUBWORD
|Logged only, no pass or fail. For each target of the sub-word leg and each size: the byte address, the read response and read data, the write response and write data, and the LSU read of the word before and after. The specification states no response and no effect for an 8-bit or 16-bit access to these registers, except the responses of the watchdog threshold word (CHK-REG-SUBWORD-WDT).
|CHK-REG-SUBWORD-WDT
|The LSU 8-bit read at byte offset 1 and 16-bit read at byte offset 2 of the watchdog `WKUP_THOLD_LO` word answer OKAY, and the writes of the same shapes answer SLVERR. A narrow read inside a register returns OKAY, and a partial write (a byte strobe that misses a byte the register owns) gets a TL-UL error and SLVERR synchronously (`hw/sys/sep/regs/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl/sep_cpu_ctrl.rdl:388-393`; the watchdog is `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS.wdt`, `:442`). A DUT that stores a partial write with OKAY, or refuses the narrow read, fails. The word after the write is not stated and stays logged. Evidence: `CHK-REG-SUBWORD-WDT PASS: target=wdt_wkup_thold_lo addr=0x<hex> size=<0\|1> rd_resp=OKAY wr_resp=SLVERR expect=OKAY/SLVERR`.
|CHK-ROW-FABRIC-CSR
|The SI read of the inbound-filter entry 1 `START_ADDR` word and of the alias remap region 1 `START` word returns the value the LSU staged, and the SI reads of both halves of `FEAT_CTRL` return the bring-up golden that `feat_ctrl_expected` computes for the pinned image. The inbound crossbar reaches the system CSRs, which program the inbound filter entries and the remaps (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc:69-71,140-147`), and forwards the other addresses below `0x4000_0000` to the local crossbar, where the lifecycle controller is not among the units that refuse inbound access (`hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc:99-104`). A DUT whose inbound path refuses these words or returns other data fails. No TT text states the response code of these reads, so it is logged on the line (`si_resp`). The lifecycle controller sits behind the local crossbar, so both `FEAT_CTRL` reads must also show their AR at the crossbar's `ext` initiator (`xbar_ext_in_*`, past the inbound filter; `ext_ar=2`): a refused read that returns zero data fails. Anchor: the SI read data and, for `FEAT_CTRL`, the AR count. Evidence: `CHK-ROW-FABRIC-CSR PASS: target=infilt_start\|alias_start addr=0x<hex> staged=0x<hex> lsu_rb=0x<hex> si_resp=<code> si_rdata=0x<hex> expect=staged` and `CHK-ROW-FABRIC-CSR PASS: target=lc_feat_ctrl ... si_rdata=0x<hex64> ext_ar=2 expect=0x<golden>,ext_ar=2`.
|OBS-ROW-FABRIC-CSR
|Logged only, no pass or fail. For the AP region 1 and STEE region 0 remap `ATTRS` words: the staged value, the LSU read-back, and the response and read data of the SI read of the same word. `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc:99-101` lists the AP/STEE remap regions among the units that answer DECERR to any external access, while `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc:146` places the remap registers among the system CSRs that the inbound path reaches, so the response of the SI read is an open specification question.
|CHK-ROW-FILTER-SRC
|The LSU stages a drawn non-zero value in the inbound-filter entry 1 `START_ADDR` word, reads it back (the read-back must equal the staged value) and restores the old value after the reads. With inbound entry 0 set to a drawn non-zero `src_id`, the SI read of that word with AxUSER equal to `src_id` returns the LSU read-back of that word, as the read through the wildcard entry does, and the read with a different AxUSER answers DECERR. The response codes of the admitted reads are logged, not graded (no TT text states them). The SI reaches that word (CHK-ROW-FABRIC-CSR) and the staged value is not zero, so a dead path or a refused read that returns zero data fails the data terms. A foreign source ID does not select the entry, entries 1 to 15 keep their reset `entry_enabled` 0, and unmatched SEP inbound traffic is blocked with DECERR (`hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc:41-42,61-62,73-77,144-146`; `hw/ip/axi_filter/regs/filter_ctrl.rdl:35`); the bring-up line records SEP-scope debug off (`sep_debug=0`), so the filter bypass is not in effect. A DUT that ignores `src_id` admits the mismatch read and fails. Evidence: `CHK-ROW-FILTER-SRC PASS: target=infilt_start entry_src_id=<n> axuser_match=<u> axuser_mismatch=<u> wildcard=<code>/<data> match=<code>/<data> mismatch=DECERR/<data> staged=0x<v> expect=<data>,<data>,DECERR`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Functional coverage*: owner window 231. Each owned sample is held and samples only on the `row_pass` release, which the leaf writes after every check of the instance passed; the start of each instance and a FAIL drop the held samples. `cp_otbn_cache_reg` samples only on the release value 3, which the leaf writes only after CHK-ROW-CACHE of an OTBN cache instance passed. `cp_si_pool` and `cp_burst_region[spi_cross_extent_r, spi_cross_extent_w]` are filled by the pool and SPI legs.

*Scope limit*: The leaf grades the cells that Test Coverage lists, and the open tests named there keep every other cell. It does not grade the Forwarded rows (AP, STEE) or the adopter-defined cells (TRNG, SEP External). It does not grade the response of a burst to a register region outside the crypto region and the SPI host extent, because the specification states none; of the SPI crossing burst it grades the class of each past beat and logs the code. It does not grade an AxSIZE 3 access. The LSU response of a cell that the SEP CPU does not see as an error is not graded. The live-word cell is a read. An in-extent write is made only at the reference registers, the hole neighbours, the burst control registers and the Key Manager mailbox `SEP_IRQ_ENABLE` of the baseline control. The LSU single read that completes SLVERR at the HMAC CSR and FIFO gap, the KMAC gap and the OTBN CSR and IMEM gap stays with `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`; this leaf grades only the new hole cells.

'''''

[[sep_cpu_lsu_alias_window_twin_test]]
===== 2.32 `sep_cpu_lsu_alias_window_twin_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `cpu`, firmware image `alias_twin_test`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds. The splice stub cannot run this test, because the alias remap sits in the core path. VCS is required for the X check on the reads.

*Source Module*: `sep_cpu_lsu_alias_window_twin_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU FW (CPU LSU reads and writes; the firmware prints each value). Approved probe: the CPU read-back of each static word through the alias and the direct address. PR-SRAM (`sep_sram_req_o`), for the address compare of the SRAM words only, and PR-CPU (the raw CPU LSU and IFU requests) are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. CHK-CPU-AXPROT grades the LSU AxPROT against `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` (table `sep-cpu-axprot-table`): every LSU AR and AW handshake on PR-CPU-LSU carries `0b001` (privileged, secure, data), each channel has at least one, and an X or Z on the tap fails (`CHK-CPU-AXPROT PASS: master=lsu ch=ar|aw prot={0b001} count=<n> expect=0b001`). CHK-CPU-SINGLE-BEAT grades AxLEN 0 on every LSU AR and AW handshake on PR-LSU-XBAR and PR-LSU-ROM (observation taps on the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list). The IFU AxPROT of this leaf is logged only (`OBS-AXPROT: master=ifu ...`); `sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test` grades it and the IFU single beat, because this firmware runs from the ICCM and issues no IFU read on the fabric. No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the seeded value of each writable static word (the cold scratch word and the SPI host `CSID`) and of each SRAM word, the marker value, the moved window base B and the second marker. B is 16 MiB aligned in [`0xD100_0000`, `0xEF00_0000`]: above the reset window base and the DMA TCM backdoor at `0xC000_0000` to `0xCFFF_FFFF`, and low enough that every moved alias the leaf uses stays below 2^32. The specification states no legal range for `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR`, so the legal range is an open specification question (<<sep-vplan-phase3-oos,SEP_VPLAN>>, `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR` legal values). The second marker is non-zero and differs from the other drawn values. The host patches the values into the firmware parameter block. Every seed covers all five unit classes and both SRAM words. Rejection list: twins that have a read side effect (entropy pool drain, DMA start). `reseed = 3` samples different static values, markers and moved bases over the same word set.

*Objective*: The 768 MiB window at `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR` (reset `0xD000_0000`) rewrites to `0x1000_0000`, so an alias address answers as its direct twin on every target the leaf reads. The window compare and the rewrite follow the programmed `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR`: with the base at B, the alias `B + (direct - 0x1000_0000)` answers as its direct twin (`fabric.adoc`, "Address Remapping": the remap rewrites the 768 MiB window at `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR`). The leaf drives no LSU access to the extension window, and no access to the reset-base alias while the window is at B: the extension tie-off answers DECERR, the reset-base alias then matches no local route, and the specification states no CPU consequence of an error response on a load. Cites `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` ("Address Remapping"; "Fabric Topology", local fabric), `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` ("Memory Map"), `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` (introduction, system-bus masters) and `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc` (`SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR`).

*Test Coverage*: Direct and alias reads of one static word per unit class: scratch (a cold scratch word), AES (`CTRL_SHADOWED`, read only), system CSR (`SEP_VERSION_ID`), SPI host (`CSID`) and entropy source (`COMPONENT_ID`). A marker write through the alias address to the RW scratch word, read at the direct address. Direct and alias reads of the SRAM start word `0x1000_0000` and last word `0x1003_FFFC` (alias `0xD000_0000` and `0xD003_FFFC`). This entry extends `sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test`, which grades the LSU alias read and write of the SRAM window. Only the new cells are graded here: the five unit classes and the window start and last SRAM word. `sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test` keeps the LSU alias read and write of the SRAM window. With the window moved to B: direct, moved-alias and direct reads of the scratch word and the SPI host `CSID`, and a second marker write through the moved alias of the scratch word, read at the direct address. The DMA alias forms are legs of `sep_fabric_dma_endpoint_matrix_test`. Functional coverage: `sep_remap_alias_cg` (`cp_local_alias_twin`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. The host patches the seeded values into the firmware parameter block, stages the image and releases the CPU.
. Firmware writes the seeded value of each writable static word at its direct address: the cold scratch word and the SPI host `CSID`. It writes the seeded values of the SRAM words at `0x1000_0000` and `0x1003_FFFC`.
. For each unit class, firmware reads the static word at its direct address, then at its alias address (the direct address + `0xC000_0000`), then at its direct address again. It prints the three values. The words of `SEP_VERSION_ID` and `COMPONENT_ID` are read-only, and firmware does not write them. Firmware does not write AES `CTRL_SHADOWED` either: an AES register write that the unit ignores while it is not idle (`aes.hjson`) has no stated result, and a read has no idle gate.
. For each of the two SRAM words, firmware reads the direct address and then the alias address. It prints both values. PR-SRAM records the address of each request.
. Firmware writes the marker through the alias address of the cold scratch word, reads the direct address and prints the value.
. Firmware writes B to `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR` (the low 32 bits) and prints B and the CSR read-back. It reads the cold scratch word and the SPI host `CSID` at the direct address, at the moved alias `B + (direct - 0x1000_0000)` and at the direct address again, and prints the three values of each word.
. Firmware writes the second marker through the moved alias of the cold scratch word, reads the direct address and prints the value. It writes the reset value `0xD000_0000` to `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR` and prints the CSR read-back.
. The host compares the printed values with the seeded values and the marker, and logs the checkers.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-TWIN
|For each unit class and for the two SRAM words, the alias read equals the direct read. The two direct reads of a unit class are equal, so the word is stable. For a writable word, both reads equal the seeded value that the host patched. For AES `CTRL_SHADOWED`, bits [15:0] of both reads equal `0x11FD`, the RDL reset value of its fields (`OPERATION` 1, `MODE` `0x3F`, `KEY_LEN` 1, `SIDELOAD` 0, `PRNG_RESEED_RATE` 1, `MANUAL_OPERATION` 0; `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aes/regs/gen/adoc/aes.adoc`). `SEP_VERSION_ID` reads its RDL reset `0xDEADBEEF` (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`), and the entropy source `COMPONENT_ID` reads its RDL reset (`NAME` 1, `MINOR_VERSION` 1, `MAJOR_VERSION` 0). Each compare uses the RDL field bits only: the Reserved bits of `CTRL_SHADOWED` ([31:16]) and `COMPONENT_ID` ([23:16]) have no stated read value and are logged. X or Z in the read data fails on VCS. Catches a wrong rewrite base and a window that misses a target. Anchor: the CPU read-back of a static word that holds a seeded value where writable. Control: the direct read. The check also grades the PR-SRAM address compare: the direct read and the alias read of each SRAM word each show one PR-SRAM request, and the two addresses are equal. A read that shows no PR-SRAM request fails the check. Evidence: `CHK-TWIN PASS: unit=<name>\|sram_start\|sram_end direct=0x<addr> alias=0x<addr> value=0x<value> field_mask=0x<m> rsvd=0x<r> match=1`. The PR-SRAM part of the SRAM lines is `sram_direct_seen=0x<addr> sram_alias_seen=0x<addr>`.
|CHK-TWIN-WR
|A marker written through the alias address reads back at the direct address. The marker differs from the seeded value of the word, so a write that lands elsewhere or not at all fails. Anchor: the CPU read of the direct address. Evidence: `CHK-TWIN-WR PASS: alias=0x<addr> marker=0x<value> direct_read=0x<value>`.
|CHK-TWIN-MOVED
|`SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR` reads back B. With the window at B, for the cold scratch word and the SPI host `CSID`, the moved-alias read equals the direct read, the two direct reads are equal, and the direct read equals the expected value (the marker of CHK-TWIN-WR for the scratch word, the seeded value for `CSID`), compared on the RDL field bits. The second marker written through the moved alias reads back at the direct address, and `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR` reads back `0xD000_0000` after the restore. The second marker differs from the first, so a moved-alias write that lands elsewhere or not at all fails. Catches a window compare or rewrite that does not follow the programmed base (for example a base fixed at the reset value): the moved-alias access then misses the window. Anchor: the CPU read of the direct address and the CSR read-back. Control: the direct reads. The reset-base alias is not read while the window is at B (see Objective). Evidence: `CHK-TWIN-MOVED PASS: unit=<scratch\|spi_word> base=0x<B> direct=0x<addr> alias=0x<addr> value=0x<v> expect=0x<v> alias_value=0x<v> direct2_value=0x<v> field_mask=0x<m> alias_rsvd=0x<r> match=1` and `CHK-TWIN-MOVED PASS: wr base=0x<B> alias=0x<addr> marker=0x<v> direct_read=0x<v> prior=0x<v> field_mask=0x<m> rsvd=0x<r> restored=0xd0000000`.
|CHK-CPU-AXPROT
|Every LSU AR and AW handshake on PR-CPU-LSU over the run carries AxPROT `0b001` (privileged, secure, data; `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, `sep-cpu-axprot-table`), and each channel has at least one. An X or Z on the tap fails. Catches a core that drives another AxPROT on its data accesses. Anchor: PR-CPU-LSU. Evidence: `CHK-CPU-AXPROT PASS: master=lsu ch=ar\|aw prot={0b001} count=<n> expect=0b001`.
|CHK-CPU-SINGLE-BEAT
|Every LSU AR and every LSU AW handshake on PR-LSU-XBAR and PR-LSU-ROM over the window has AxLEN 0, one beat (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc:38-39`: the CPU masters issue single-beat transactions only), and each channel has at least one. An X or Z AxLEN fails. Catches LSU store merging or a burst read on the demux ports. Anchor: PR-LSU-XBAR and PR-LSU-ROM. Evidence: `CHK-CPU-SINGLE-BEAT PASS: master=lsu ch=ar\|aw count=<n> PR-LSU-XBAR=<n> PR-LSU-ROM=<n> len={0} mismatches=0 expect=0`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Functional coverage*: owner window 232 opens when the CPU is released and stays open through every check. The LSU cells of `cp_local_alias_twin` and `cp_local_alias_moved` are held at the LSU request and sample when the leaf writes `twin_pass` after every check of the run passed (CHK-CPU-AXPROT, CHK-CPU-SINGLE-BEAT, CHK-TWIN, CHK-TWIN-WR, CHK-TWIN-MOVED and the firmware PASS); a FAIL closes the window first, which drops them. `cp_cpu_single_beat` (`lsu_ar`, `lsu_aw`), `cp_master_ch` (`lsu_ar_001`, `lsu_aw_001`) and `cp_component_id[reset_value_read]` are held at the event in the same way and sample on the same `twin_pass` rise; an LSU AxPROT other than 0b001 samples an illegal bin of `cp_master_ch` at once.

*Scope limit*: The leaf does not claim the LSU alias read and write of the SRAM window (`sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test`), the DMA alias forms (`sep_fabric_dma_endpoint_matrix_test`), the DMA alias at a moved base, the legal range of `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR`, bits [55:32] of `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR`, the response at the reset-base alias while the window is moved, the alias remap regions or the extension window.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_dma_endpoint_matrix_test]]
===== 2.33 `sep_fabric_dma_endpoint_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `cpu`, firmware image `dma_endpoint_matrix_test`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`, 4 seeds. The firmware resets and brings up the entropy stack, with the internal DRBG on streams 0 and 1 and the external source on stream 2 (the pool fill); the test drives the raw ring-oscillator noise (`start_esrc_noise_driver`, the ESRC noise force of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`), which needs `+esrc_noise_force`, and the pool words on the external stream 2 (`env/sep_ext_trng_stream.py`, the external TRNG stream top-port drive of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`). SMC aperture plusargs with configuration A of `sep_fabric_smc_route_test` (base `0x4000_0000`, size `0x0100_0000`). VCS is required for the X check on the copied register words.

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_dma_endpoint_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU FW (DMA set-up, copies, compares and console lines), ocah_axi_vip, `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (outbound responder: OKAY and zero read data for any address), and the SMC memory model on `sep_ext_to_smc_axi`, and the external TRNG stream driver on stream 2 (`env/sep_ext_trng_stream.py`; the pool words of the pool leg). The bench drives DECERR on `axi_extension_axi_resp_i`. Approved probes: the DMA `STATUS` and `ERROR_CODE` registers, the LSU read-back of SRAM and register words, and the outbound-filter verdict (the DMA status after a write behind a source-ID stack). PR-OUT (with its replies PR-OUT-RSP), PR-EXT (with its reply channels), PR-SMC, PR-ROM, PR-DMACSR, PR-DMA-RAW (the raw DMA master request) and the DMA master tap `dma_axi_req_probe_o` (the request after the DMA local-alias remap, with its B response) are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. CHK-DMA-AXPROT grades the DMA AxPROT against `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` (table `sep-cpu-axprot-table`): every AR and AW handshake of the raw DMA master request on PR-DMA-RAW carries `0b001` (privileged, secure, data), each channel has at least one, and an X or Z on the tap fails (`CHK-DMA-AXPROT PASS: master=dma ch=ar|aw prot={0b001} count=<n> expect=0b001`). Added hook: BH-SMC, a top-port drive that the test adds to the port table of the HDL Top section of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` (SMC aperture plusargs and the SMC memory model for this target). No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the order of the four random copies (every seed runs each pair of the list once: SRAM to scratch, scratch to SRAM, a WDT status word to SRAM and an AES `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` word to SRAM), the length of each copy, the source and destination offsets, the alias or direct form, the order of the register endpoints, the SMC window offset and length, and the number of word pairs of the pool leg (1 to 31, below the 32-entry pool depth of `hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc:36-37`). Legal range: the reachable targets; length is a 4-byte multiple up to 256 for the random copies, and the length plus the offset stays inside the extent of each endpoint: at most 64 B at a scratch bank base (the next 64 B are Reserved and answer DECERR), and a WDT or AES register source is read at a fixed address (increment 0), 4 to 4096 for the register legs (every run is one chunk, so a fixed-address side never starts a second chunk), and 16 to 1024 for the SMC leg; the whole SMC transfer stays inside the aperture. Rejection list of the random pairs (the directed legs below are outside it): SRAM to SRAM as a graded pair (`sep_dma_transfer_matrix_rand_test` grades it), any copy with an ICCM or DCCM endpoint (`sep_dma_basic_test`, `sep_dma_hash_test` and `sep_tcm_dma_aperture_ecc_test` grade them), writes to the reset controller, DMA writes to a filter entry (CHK-DMA-FILT-WR does one as a directed leg), reads with side effects, a scratch copy that holds no word at a scratch word address (bits [31:0]). The directed legs run on every seed. `reseed = 4` samples different pair orders, lengths, offsets, forms and orders. The host patches the seeded values into the firmware parameter block.

*Objective*: The DMA reaches SRAM, WDT, reset controller, crypto, system peripherals, IO and the extension port, and does not reach its own CSR or the boot ROM. The copies between SRAM and the CPU TCM backdoor are graded by `sep_tcm_dma_aperture_ecc_test`, and the SRAM to SRAM copy images by `sep_dma_transfer_matrix_rand_test`. The DMA master passes the local alias remap. The DMA leaves AxUSER at zero (OTHERS), which the SMC path keeps and the AP path sets anyway; the SMU path replaces it with SEP's ID and sets NS to the inverse of address bit 55 (`sep-outbound-attributes-table`). Every DMA beat on the SMC port carries AxUSER 0 and AxLEN 0. The CPU masters drive SEP's ID, so the leaf grades that the AxUSER of the CPU beat on the SMC port is not `OTHERS` (0). No specification or RDL gives the number of SEP's ID, so on the SMU path the leaf grades that the AxUSER[3:0] of every DMA beat and of the CPU store is not `OTHERS` and is one value, and logs it. The leaf grades the DMA attributes on the SMU path through the outbound filter: the source-ID stack refuses the DMA write only at a non-zero source ID with NS 1. Cites `hw/sys/sep/doc/assets/sep_axi_connectivity.svg`, `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` ("Top Level AXI4"; "Transaction Routing"), `hw/sys/sep/doc/dma.adoc` ("OCAH Modifications"; "Completion Reporting"), `hw/sys/sep/doc/peripheral_subsystem.adoc`, `hw/sys/sep/doc/assets/sep-output-fabric.svg` and `hw/sys/smc/doc/assets/smc-traffic-filters.svg` (`src_id` against AxUSER[3:0]).

*Test Coverage*: Reachable DMA pairs among SRAM, scratch, a WDT status word, an AES `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` word, the outbound port (SMU window and AP region) and the extension port, plus one directed SRAM to SRAM copy at direct addresses per seed, graded as the direct form of the alias leg. The alias forms. The DMA AxUSER through the source-ID stack over the SMU window. The register endpoints SCRATCH[0], SPI `CSID` and `SEP_SW_DEBUG`, the `SW_RESET_N` read and the inbound entry 0 `FILTER_CONFIG` read. The SMC-port beats. The not-connected cells: the boot ROM base and a DMA CSR word. This entry extends `sep_dma_basic_test`, `sep_dma_hash_test`, `sep_dma_cpu_contention_test`, `sep_dma_transfer_matrix_rand_test` and `sep_tcm_dma_aperture_ecc_test`. Only the new cells are graded here: the remaining reachable endpoint pairs, the register endpoints, the alias forms, the DMA AxUSER, the SMC-port beats and the not-connected cells. `sep_dma_basic_test` keeps SRAM to ICCM, ICCM to SRAM and the fabric error of a transfer past the DMA CSR window. `sep_dma_hash_test` keeps SRAM to DCCM. `sep_dma_transfer_matrix_rand_test` (CHK-DMA-IMAGE) keeps the SRAM to SRAM copy images. `sep_tcm_dma_aperture_ecc_test` (CHK-TCM-APERT) keeps the copies between SRAM and every ICCM and DCCM word, and the DMA read at the DCCM direct address, which lies below the alias window. `sep_dma_cpu_contention_test` keeps the SRAM data of a DMA and a CPU stream. The DMA legs share one firmware image, one run mode and one DMA bench, so they are legs of one test. Functional coverage: `sep_dma_endpoint_cg` (`cp_dma_pair`, `cp_dma_addr_form`, `cp_dma_smc_beat`), `sep_fabric_dedicated_port_cg` (`cp_ext_init_dir_dma`), `sep_fabric_outbound_route_cg` (the DMA cells of `cp_out_attr`), `sep_remap_output_offset_cg` (`cp_instance_dir`), `sep_remap_alias_cg` (the DMA alias cells of `cp_local_alias_twin`) and `sep_fabric_port_silence_cp` (`sil_dma_not_connected`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. The host patches the seeded values and the leg enables into the firmware parameter block, stages the image and releases the CPU. The SMC leg runs on every seed.
. Firmware programs the DMA once: `ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_BASE` 0, `ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_LIMIT` `0xFFFF_FFFF`, `RANGE_VALID` 1, `TRANSFER_WIDTH` 4 bytes and the done and error interrupt enables. A DMA run means: program the source and destination address, `TOTAL_DATA_SIZE`, `CHUNK_DATA_SIZE` equal to `TOTAL_DATA_SIZE` (one chunk; `secure_dma.adoc`, CHUNK_DATA_SIZE: for a single memory transfer the two registers hold the same value), `SRC_CONFIG` and `DST_CONFIG`; clear `STATUS` DONE, ERROR and CHUNK_DONE (write one to clear) and the DMA bus-error latch (`DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR`); read `STATUS` and check that all three bits read 0; set `CONTROL.GO`; wait for `STATUS.DONE` or `STATUS.ERROR`. A wait that ends in neither within a bounded wait fails the leg. A recovery copy means: clear the DMA error state, then run an SRAM to SRAM copy of seeded words and compare the destination with the source.
. Random copies. For each pair of the list SRAM to scratch, scratch to SRAM, WDT status word to SRAM and AES `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` word to SRAM, in the seeded order (every pair runs on every seed), stage the source with seeded words and the destination with a different pattern, run the DMA and compare. For a register source, set the increment of the register side to 0 (fixed address) and compare each destination word with the LSU read of the register before and after the run. A scratch word holds its `data` field in bits [31:0] at its 8-byte-aligned address, and the 4-byte beat at that address + 4 lands on bits [63:32], which are Reserved with no stated read value. The scratch pairs therefore compare only the 4-byte words at a scratch word address (address bit 2 clear) with their twins on the SRAM side, and log the other words. The WDT word is `WDOG_REGWEN` (`regwen` reads 1 from reset until a write of 0, `aon_timer.adoc`; the leaf never writes it) and the AES word is `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` (RW0C, reset value 1, reads 1 until a write of 0, `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aes/regs/gen/adoc/aes.adoc`; the leaf never writes it), so bit 0 of both reads 1 before and after the run. Bits [31:1] of both words are Reserved with no stated read value, so the compare uses bit 0 and logs bits [31:1]. The staged destination pattern has bit 0 = 0. A bit 0 that reads 0 or changes fails the pair, because a DMA read that lands on another zero word gives the same destination. Then run the directed SRAM to SRAM copy at direct addresses. It is the direct-form cell of CHK-DMA-ALIAS and is not graded as a pair.
. Extension port. Run one DMA read from `0x2000_0100` into SRAM and one DMA write from SRAM to `0x2000_0100`, each one 4-byte beat (`TOTAL_DATA_SIZE` 4). The port answers DECERR (`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`, `axi_extension_axi_resp_i`: tie to DECERR if unused), so each run ends with `STATUS.ERROR` and `ERROR_CODE.BUS_ERROR`. PR-EXT records the AR and the AW address and the number of beats, and its reply channel the response. Run a recovery copy after each.
. Alias legs. Run an SRAM to SRAM copy with the source at `0xD000_0000` + `o_s` and the destination at `0xD000_0000` + `o_d`, with seeded offsets whose ranges do not overlap. Stage the source at the direct address `0x1000_0000` + `o_s` with seeded words and the destination with a pattern that differs from them, and read the destination at the direct address `0x1000_0000` + `o_d`. Run an 8-byte DMA write (`TOTAL_DATA_SIZE` 8) from SRAM to the destination `0xFFFF_FFF8`, so the write ends at `0xFFFF_FFFF`, and run a recovery copy afterwards. PR-EXT records the address of that write.
. Outbound legs. Program AP region 0 with a non-identity offset and `valid` 1. Program an outbound entry pair (`allow_ns` 0 and 1, read and write allowed, `src_id` 0) over the remapped AP word and a second pair over the SMU window word. Read the entries back. Run a DMA write from SRAM to an AP region word and a DMA write from SRAM to the SMU window word. Store one word from the CPU to the SMU window as the control. PR-OUT records every beat.
. Source-ID stack over the SMU window. Program all 32 outbound entries over the SMU window word, all enabled: entries 0 to 14 with `src_id` 1 to 15, `allow_ns` 0 and write permission set; entries 15 to 29 with `src_id` 1 to 15, `allow_ns` 1 and write permission clear; entries 30 and 31 with `src_id` 0 (wildcard), one for each `allow_ns` value, with write permission set. Read the entries back. The stack refuses a write only at a non-zero source ID with NS 1, so it needs no number for SEP's ID. Error injection at the outbound filter input: run a DMA write to the SMU window word, which leaves with SEP's ID (not `OTHERS`) and NS 1. Expected response: DECERR, shown as `STATUS.ERROR` with `ERROR_CODE.BUS_ERROR`. Check `STATUS` and `ERROR_CODE`, then clear the DMA error state and run a recovery copy. Then set write permission on entries 15 to 29, read them back and run the same write as the control write. It completes with `STATUS.DONE` and no error.
. Register endpoints. Read each register once from the LSU. For each of SCRATCH[0], SPI `CSID` and `SEP_SW_DEBUG`, run SRAM to register first and register to SRAM next, with the register side at a fixed address (increment 0). The last source word of the SRAM to register run is seeded and differs from the register value read before it. Firmware writes the inbound entry 0 `FILTER_CONFIG` with seeded non-zero `src_id` (bits [19:16]) and `group_id` (bits [23:20]) and every other field at its reset value, so the entry stays disabled, and reads it back. Run a DMA read of `SW_RESET_N` to SRAM and a DMA read of the inbound entry 0 `FILTER_CONFIG` to SRAM. After each destination register, read it from the LSU. Restore each written register, `FILTER_CONFIG` included.
. Output remap legs. Program STEE regions w and r (drawn, distinct) with a drawn offset at or above 4 GiB and `valid` 1; the read offset is the write offset with bit 55 flipped, so both NS values leave on every run. Program an outbound entry pair over each remapped word. Run a DMA read from AP region 0 into SRAM, a DMA write from SRAM to STEE region w and a DMA read from STEE region r into SRAM. PR-OUT records every beat and PR-OUT-RSP the reply data. Read the STEE region attributes and the outbound entries 5 to 8 back (CHK-DMA-USER).
. Inbound filter writes. The last inbound filter entry is kept disabled. Run a DMA write of a drawn word to the low word of its `START_ADDR`, then to the high word (its `END_ADDR` stays above `START_ADDR` in another granule), and read each back from the LSU. Lock the entry (`FILTER_CONFIG.locked`) and run a DMA write of a third word to the low word; read it back from the LSU (CHK-DMA-FILT-WR).
. SMC port. Poll `SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` until `smc_fuse_sense_done` reads 1, within a bounded wait, and log the poll count. Run a DMA copy from SRAM to an SMC window address inside configuration A (16 to 1024 bytes), then a DMA copy from that address back to a different SRAM region. Read one word from the CPU at the same SMC address as the control. Then, in its own graded leg, the CPU reads one SMC word, stores a drawn word that differs from the word the DMA left there, and reads it back (CHK-DMA-SMC-CPU).
. Not-connected legs. Read the boot ROM base word and a DMA CSR word (`ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_LIMIT`, non-zero) from the LSU. PR-ROM records the handshake of the boot ROM read. Stage a destination SRAM value that differs from both. Error injection at the DMA master request, one run each for these three targets: source at the boot ROM base, destination at the boot ROM base, source at the DMA CSR word. From the `CONTROL.GO` write until the done or error interrupt the CPU waits in a bounded loop that reads only a DTCM flag, so no CPU access reaches the DMA CSR in that window. A wait that ends with no interrupt fails the firmware with a line that names the leg. The host bounds the wait; a timeout fails CHK-DMA-NOTCONN. No DMA path reaches the boot ROM or the DMA CSR port (`hw/sys/sep/doc/assets/sep_axi_connectivity.svg`; `fabric.adoc`, "Fabric Topology"), so the request must not reach the target. No spec sentence states what the fabric answers for such a request, so the DMA end state (`STATUS`, `ERROR_CODE`) is logged as `OBS-DMA-NOTCONN-RESP`. A bus error response would end the run with `STATUS.ERROR` and `ERROR_CODE.BUS_ERROR` (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/secure_dma/regs/gen/adoc/secure_dma.adoc`, `STATUS.ERROR`: error occurred; `ERROR_CODE.BUS_ERROR`: the bus transfer returned an error). After each interrupt, read the destination, the DMA `STATUS` and `ERROR_CODE`, clear the DMA error state and run a recovery copy. Two further runs follow the same shape with the Key Manager mailbox `STATUS` word (leg `nc_km_src`) and the PIC base word (leg `nc_pic_src`) as the source, after the destination SRAM word is staged with the complement of the source word as the LSU read it. The `km_src` run is graded by CHK-DMA-KM-SRC. The `pic_src` run logs the end state, the destination and the source word as `OBS-DMA-NOTCONN-RESP` with `tgt=pic_src` and is not graded. Read the boot ROM word and the DMA CSR word from the LSU again as the control. Entropy-pool source leg: before the not-connected legs the firmware resets the entropy domain and programs it (`sep_entropy_configure`, then the generators, the bounded boot-phase wait and the EDN enable of `fw/drivers/sep_entropy.h`), every time: the entropy source and its ring oscillators are enabled out of reset (`hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:46`, `:565`, `:571`) and the bench drives their noise from the start, so the boot phase can end before that step, and only the programming selects the internal DRBG for streams 0 and 1 (`EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL` resets to all external, `hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc:98-100`). Right after the programming, before the EDN enable, the firmware writes `EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL` with bit 2 alone set, so stream 2, the pool fill, selects the external source (`integration/rdl/sep/sep_cpu_ctrl.rdl:308-310`) and the internal endpoint is backpressured and keeps its words (`hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc:104-105`). The record logs the bring-up code and the select read back after it. In the control leg `pool_pre` the test sends 2 x P drawn words (P word pairs, 1 to 31) on the external stream 2; the pool packs two 32-bit words into one 64-bit entry and holds 32 entries (`trng.adoc:36-37`), so the level stays below the depth. The firmware stages the destination SRAM word, polls the pool `STATUS` from the LSU until `fifo_level` is 1 or more (a bounded poll; a refused read fails the leg), writes `EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL` with bit 2 alone set again, reads it back and reads the level until it reads the same value 64 times in a row (a bounded poll). It then runs one DMA read from the pool `DATA` word into SRAM, reads the level again and writes `EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL` back to 0.
. Each graded leg runs between `M G` and `M C` markers. Staging and the register and `FILTER_CONFIG` restores run in control legs (`stage`, `reg_restore`, `filt_restore`); the restored values are logged.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DMA-PAIR
|The destination data equals the source for every legal copy of step 3: SRAM to scratch, scratch to SRAM, a WDT status word to SRAM and an AES `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` word to SRAM. Anchor: a real LSU readback of an SRAM or scratch destination, or a register source read by the LSU. For a scratch pair only the words at a scratch word address (bits [31:0], the `data` field) are graded; the words on bits [63:32] are logged. A scratch pair that grades no word fails. Bit 0 of a register source reads 1 before and after the run, and bit 0 of each destination word equals 1, so a DMA read that reaches another zero word fails; bits [31:1] are Reserved and logged. X or Z in a copied register word fails on VCS. A DMA that does not reach its target leaves the destination at its staged pattern, so a missing path and a corrupted copy fail. Each outbound leg of step 6 completes with `STATUS.DONE` and no `STATUS.ERROR`. The SRAM to SRAM copy and the copies between SRAM and the CPU TCM are graded by `sep_dma_transfer_matrix_rand_test` (CHK-DMA-IMAGE) and `sep_tcm_dma_aperture_ecc_test` (CHK-TCM-APERT), and this check does not grade them. The check also grades the beats of the outbound legs on PR-OUT: the probe shows the beats of that leg at the expected address and range. For each extension-port leg, PR-EXT shows the AR or AW at `0x2000_0100`, every PR-EXT reply of the leg is DECERR (`port_table.adoc`, `axi_extension_axi_resp_i`: tie to DECERR if unused; `fabric.adoc`, local fabric: other addresses of the `0x2000_0000` aperture leave on the AXI extension port), and the run ends with `STATUS.ERROR` and `ERROR_CODE.BUS_ERROR` and no other defined `ERROR_CODE` bit (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/secure_dma/regs/gen/adoc/secure_dma.adoc`, `STATUS.ERROR`: error occurred; `ERROR_CODE.BUS_ERROR`: the bus transfer returned an error). The specification states no DMA rule for the beats after an error response, so the leg is one beat, only that request is graded and the beat count is logged. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-PAIR PASS: src=<name> dst=<name> len=<n> data_ok=1\|na done=1`. The probe part of the line is `seen=<port>`. An extension-port line adds `port_resp=DECERR replies=<n> dma_status=0x<value> error_code=0x<value>`.
|CHK-DMA-ALIAS
|A source or destination alias address copies as its direct twin. The data read from an alias source equals the seeded words that firmware staged at the direct address. The LSU read of the direct address is a control and equals the same words, or the check fails. The data written to an alias destination reads back at the direct address. The directed SRAM to SRAM copy at direct addresses of step 3 is the direct-form cell: its destination, read by the LSU, equals the staged source words, or the check fails. Catches a DMA path that skips the alias remap. Anchor: the LSU read of the direct twin. For the destination at `0xFFFF_FFF8`, PR-EXT shows the first write beat at `0x3FFF_FFF8`, every PR-EXT reply of that run is DECERR, and the run ends with `STATUS.ERROR` and `ERROR_CODE.BUS_ERROR` and no other defined `ERROR_CODE` bit. The specification states no DMA rule for the beats after an error response, so the later beat is logged and not graded. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-ALIAS PASS: form=direct\|alias alias=0x<addr>\|na direct=0x<addr> data_ok=1`.
|CHK-DMA-USER
|Behind the source-ID stack over the SMU window word, the DMA write is refused (`STATUS.ERROR`, `ERROR_CODE.BUS_ERROR`). With the write permission of entries 15 to 29 set, the same write completes (`STATUS.DONE`, no error). A write that leaves the SMU path with source ID `OTHERS` (0), the value the DMA drives, or with NS 0, matches an allowing entry of the stack and completes, so the refusal fails (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, "Transaction Routing", `sep-outbound-attributes-table`). The AP route replaces the source ID with OTHERS, so a stack over the AP range would show only that, and this leaf runs none. Anchor: the DMA `STATUS` and `ERROR_CODE`. Control: the permission flip of entries 15 to 29. A leaf whose control write does not complete fails. The OUTSET read-back also covers the outbound entries 5 to 8 and the two STEE `REGION_ATTRS` (offset and `valid` through the RDL field masks; the other bits are logged as `OBS-STEE-ATTRS-RSVD`). Evidence: `CHK-DMA-USER PASS: dst=smu stack_bus_error=1 dma_status=0x<value> error_code=0x<value> control_complete=1`. The check also grades the PR-OUT part: every DMA beat on PR-OUT carries the address that the route produces (`{offset[55:19], issued[18:0]}` for the AP region, and the issued address zero-extended for the SMU window) and the source ID of its path: AxUSER 0 in all 12 bits (OTHERS) on the AP path; on the SMU path an AxUSER[3:0] that is not `OTHERS` (0) and is the same on every DMA beat of the `out_smu` and `stack` legs and on the CPU store to the SMU window. No specification or RDL gives the number of SEP's ID, so the number is logged and not graded. Evidence of the PR-OUT part: `CHK-DMA-USER PASS: probe=PR-OUT leg=out_ap\|out_smu\|stack path=ap\|smu beats=<n> user=<list> addr=<list>` and `CHK-DMA-USER PASS: probe=PR-OUT path=smu src_id beats=<n> constant=1 not_others=1 issued_src_ids={0x0}`. Logged: `OBS-CPU-SRC-ID: smu_path_src_id=0x<n> beats=<n> user_11_4={...} (value logged)`.
|CHK-DMA-EP-REG
|For an SRAM to register copy with a fixed register address, the LSU read of the register equals the last source word, at size 4, and the last source word differs from the register value read before the run, so a write that does not land fails. For the register to SRAM copy that follows, every destination word equals that last source word, a value that the stimulus sets. The LSU reads of the register before and after that run are controls and log `CTRL-DMA-EP-REG LOG`; a read that differs from the last source word fails the check. Nothing writes `SW_RESET_N` before its leg, so bits [6:0] of the DMA read of `SW_RESET_N` and of the LSU read both equal the RDL reset value `0x7E` (`hw/sys/sep/regs/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl/sep_reset_ctrl.rdl:14-24`); the destination is staged at the inverse of that value, so a DMA read that does not land differs in every graded bit. Bits [31:7] are Reserved with no stated read value and are logged. X or Z in a copied word fails on VCS. Catches a register endpoint that the DMA does not reach and a write beat that carries other data. The write beats before the last one at a fixed register address have no approved anchor, so only the last beat is graded. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-EP-REG PASS: reg=<name> dir=R\|W words=<n> conserve=1 readback=0x<v> expect=0x<e>`; the `SW_RESET_N` line adds `lsu=0x<v> staged=0x<s>`.
|CHK-DMA-EP-FILT
|The field bits of the word (bits [31:0]) that the DMA reads from the inbound entry 0 `FILTER_CONFIG` equal the model value: the seeded `src_id` and `group_id` that firmware wrote, 3 in `data_bus_width` (bits [14:12]) and the reset value in every other field (`hw/ip/axi_filter/regs/gen/adoc/filter_ctrl.adoc`). The compare uses the mask `0x01FF_7113`. Bits 31:25, 15, 11:9, 7:5 and 3:2 are Reserved with no stated read value, so they are masked out and logged. The LSU read of the same word is a control and its field bits equal the same value, or the check fails. The destination is staged at the inverse of the written word, so it differs from the model value in every graded bit. Catches a DMA read that reaches another register or another filter entry, which holds the reset `src_id` and `group_id`. Anchor: the SRAM word against the model value. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-EP-FILT PASS: data_bus_width=3 word=0x<v> mask=0x01ff7113 expect=0x<e> lsu=0x<v> staged=0x<s> rsvd=0x<r>`.
|CHK-DMA-SMC
|`smc_fuse_sense_done` reads 1 before the first SMC request. Every DMA beat on PR-SMC has AxUSER 0 and AxLEN 0 (`fabric.adoc`, "Transaction Routing": the DMA leaves AxUSER at zero; "Top Level AXI4": the DMA issues single-beat transactions only). PR-SMC shows at least one DMA beat in each direction, so a DMA leg that does not reach the SMC port fails. The SMC memory model holds the source words after the write leg, and SRAM holds them after the read leg. Control: the CPU beat shows on PR-SMC, and a missing control beat fails the leg. The CPU beat carries an AxUSER other than 0: the CPU masters drive SEP's ID, and `OTHERS` is AxUSER zero (`fabric.adoc`, "Transaction Routing"); its value is logged. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-SMC PASS: fuse_done=1 dir=w\|r beats=<n> user_nonzero=0 len_nonzero=0 data_ok=1 control_beats=<n>`.
|CHK-DMA-SMC-CPU
|The CPU read, store and read-back of the SMC word give two AR beats and one AW beat on PR-SMC. Every beat carries AxUSER[3:0] other than 0 and one value on every beat, equal to the SMU-path source ID of CHK-DMA-USER (both are SEP's ID: `fabric.adoc:313`, `:316`, `:319-321`; the SMC path keeps the initiator's ID and NS), and AxPROT 0b001 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, `sep-cpu-axprot-table`). The W lane on PR-SMC carries the stored word and the read-back returns it. Catches an ID from another source, `OTHERS`, an ID that differs between read and write, NS set, or a store that does not land. The number of SEP's ID is logged (`OBS-CPU-SRC-ID`). Anchor: PR-SMC and the CPU read-back. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-SMC-CPU PASS: ar=2 aw=1 src_id=<list> smu_path_src_id=0x<n> prot=0b001 store=0x<w> w_lane=0x<w> read=0x<v> readback=0x<w>`.
|CHK-DMA-NOTCONN
|In the two source legs (boot ROM source and DMA CSR source), the destination never holds the value of the boot ROM word or the DMA CSR word that the LSU read first. No DMA path reaches the boot ROM or the DMA CSR port (`sep_axi_connectivity.svg`; `fabric.adoc`, "Fabric Topology"). Each run ends with a DMA interrupt; a run with no interrupt fails. No spec sentence states the response of the fabric to such a request, so the end state (`end`, `STATUS`, `ERROR_CODE`) is logged as `OBS-DMA-NOTCONN-RESP` and is not graded. The `pic_src` run logs the same line, with `dst=` and `src_v=`, and is not graded. A fabric that routes the DMA to the boot ROM or to its own CSR copies the word to the destination, so the check fails. In the boot ROM source, boot ROM destination and DMA CSR source legs, the DMA master tap `dma_axi_req_probe_o` shows the DMA request at the target in the leg (an AR at the boot ROM base or at `ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_LIMIT` for a source, an AW at the boot ROM base for the destination); a leg with no such request fails, so a DMA that refuses the address internally cannot pass. The recovery copy after each run that ended in done or error completes with equal data, which shows that the DMA is alive. Control: the LSU reads of the boot ROM and of the DMA CSR return their values in the same leaf. In the boot ROM destination leg the ROM cannot change, so PR-ROM is the anchor: PR-ROM shows no handshake from the response of the `CONTROL.GO` write to the done or error interrupt, and the run ends in done or error. PR-ROM control: the LSU read of the boot ROM base word at the start of the not-connected step shows one AR handshake on PR-ROM at that address. A leaf whose PR-ROM control shows no handshake fails the check. In the DMA CSR source leg, PR-DMACSR shows no handshake from the response of the `CONTROL.GO` write to the done or error interrupt, while the CPU waits on the DTCM flag. PR-DMACSR control: the firmware writes that program the DMA for that run show on PR-DMACSR. A leaf whose PR-DMACSR control shows no handshake fails the check. PR-ROM silence supplements the boot ROM source leg. In the entropy-pool source leg the pool `STATUS.fifo_level` reads 1 or more and below the 32-entry depth before the GO (an empty pool also refuses a `DATA` read, `hw/sys/sep/doc/entropy_pool.adoc:31-33`; at the depth a pop that the fill replaces leaves the level unchanged; depth `hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc:36-37`), the DMA read shows on the DMA master tap at the pool `DATA` word (positive probe), the run ends with a DMA interrupt, the staged non-zero SRAM word is unchanged (no DMA path to the entropy pool, `sep_axi_connectivity.svg:146-150`), and the level after the run equals the level before the GO (a read pops one word, `entropy_pool.adoc:31-33`, and with the fill stopped nothing refills the pool). The precondition reads are part of the line: `EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL` reads back 0x4 and the settle poll ended on 64 equal level reads. Precondition line `CTRL-DMA-POOL-PRE`, which fails the leaf when it is false: the level read before the GO is 1 or more and below 32, and every word the test sent on stream 2 transferred before the GO (`CTRL-DMA-POOL-PRE PASS: target=pool_src level_before_go=<n> depth=32 words_sent=<n> words_transferred=<n> feed_done_ps=<t> go_ps=<t> feed_error=None`). Control: a run that selects the internal source for stream 2 again after the GO and before the level read fails this check on the exact compare (an unselected internal endpoint keeps its queued words, `trng.adoc:104-105`, so the pool level rises). Evidence: `CHK-DMA-NOTCONN PASS: target=rom_src\|dma_csr_src src_value=0x<v> dst_value=0x<d> end=<end> dma_status=0x<value> control_value=0x<v> recovery_ok=1`. The rom_src line adds `rom_seen=<n> dma_ar_at_tgt=<n>` and the dma_csr_src line adds `csr_seen=0 csr_ctrl_seen=<n> dma_ar_at_tgt=<n>`, with `dma_ar_at_tgt` 1 or more. The rom_dst line is `CHK-DMA-NOTCONN PASS: target=rom_dst rom_seen=0 rom_ctrl_seen=1 end=<end> dma_status=0x<value> recovery_ok=1 dma_aw_at_tgt=<n>`, with `dma_aw_at_tgt` 1 or more. The pool line is `CHK-DMA-NOTCONN PASS: target=pool_src level_before_go=<n> depth=32 polls=<n> src_sel=0x4 settle_reads=<n> stable_reads=64 dma_ar_at_pool=1 end=<end> dma_status=0x<value> error_code=0x<value> dst_value=0x<d> staged=0x<d> level_after=<n> entropy_rc=0x<v>(logged) entropy_sel=0x<v>(logged) recovery_ok=1`.
|CHK-DMA-REMAP
|A DMA write and read through AP region 0 and a DMA write and read through STEE regions w and r: each PR-OUT beat is at `{offset[55:19], address[18:0]}` and `+4` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc:178-181`, `:313-315`), carries AxUSER 0 in all 12 bits (OTHERS) and AxPROT with NS (AxPROT[1], `:304-305`) at 1 for AP and at the inverse of bit 55 of the remapped address for STEE, the other AxPROT bits as the DMA drives them (0b001; `:321-322`: no path changes them). On a read leg the SRAM destination holds the PR-OUT-RSP R data. Each run ends DONE. The two STEE offsets differ in bit 55, so both NS values leave on every run. Catches a remap that applies only to LSU traffic, wrong offset bits, an AxUSER that is not OTHERS, an NS not set or inverted, a changed AxPROT bit, or read data that does not land. The reply codes of the bench responder are logged (`INFO-REMAP-REPLY`). Anchor: PR-OUT, PR-OUT-RSP and the LSU read of the SRAM destination. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-REMAP PASS: leg=out_ap\|ap_r\|stee_w\|stee_r beats=<n> addr=<list> expect=<list> user=<list> prot=<list> expect_prot=<list> ns=<0\|1> dma_status=0x<value> error_code=0x0 [sram=<list> r_data=<list>]`.
|CHK-DMA-FILT-RANGE
|Every outbound filter entry of the `out_setup` leg (entries 1 to 8) and of the `stack` leg (entries 0 to 31, then 15 to 29 after the permission flip) is programmed as [a, a + 7] with a 8-byte aligned and `allow_burst` 0. START and END therefore share one 8-byte granule (`data_bus_width` reset 3, `hw/ip/axi_filter/regs/filter_ctrl.rdl:48`), and the rounded values that hardware writes back equal the programmed values (`filter_ctrl.rdl:94-102`). The firmware reads START and END of each entry back on their RDL field masks and counts the entries where either differs from the programmed value; every count is 0. Catches a write-back that rounds wrongly, a lost START or END write and a write that reaches another entry. Anchor: the CPU read-back. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-FILT-RANGE PASS: leg=out_setup entries=8 differences=0 expect=0` and `CHK-DMA-FILT-RANGE PASS: leg=stack entries=32 differences=0 flip_entries=15 flip_differences=0 expect=0`.
|CHK-DMA-KM-SRC
|The DMA copy of one 32-bit word from the Key Manager mailbox `STATUS` word to the SRAM word staged at the complement of the CPU read of `STATUS`: the run raises the DMA interrupt and ends `STATUS.DONE` with no error and `ERROR_CODE` 0, and the destination holds the CPU read of `STATUS`. The DMA reaches `sep_crypto` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/assets/sep_axi_connectivity.svg:130`), which holds the Key Manager mailbox (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc:91`). Catches a DMA that cannot reach the mailbox and a copy that loses or alters the word. Anchor: the destination SRAM word read by the CPU. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-KM-SRC PASS: target=km_src end=done dma_status=0x<value> error_code=0x00000000 src_value=0x<v> dst_value=0x<v> irq=1`.
|CHK-DMA-FILT-WR
|The DMA writes of the last inbound filter entry `START_ADDR`: the low word and then the high word of the unlocked entry read back from the LSU as written, on the RDL field mask of `START_ADDR` (`fabric.adoc:334-336`: the entries reside in the system CSRs and are reached directly by local masters; `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc:124-129`: the bounds sit in different granules, so no write-back). With the entry locked, the DMA write answers DECERR on the DMA master tap B, the run reports a DMA bus error, and the word reads back unchanged (`index.adoc:151-156`). Each write shows its AW on the DMA master tap. Catches a fabric that blocks a DMA system-CSR write, a write that does not land, or a lock that ignores DMA writes. The bits outside the field are logged (`OBS-FILT-WR-RSVD`). Anchor: the LSU read-back and the DMA master tap. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-FILT-WR PASS: leg=filt_wr_low\|filt_wr_high\|filt_wr_lock entry=<k> addr=0x<a> written=0x<w> before=0x<b> readback=0x<r> expect=0x<e> field_mask=0x<m> aw_seen=1 bresp=<OKAY\|DECERR> dma_status=0x<value> error_code=0x<value>`.
|CHK-DMA-AXPROT
|Every AR and AW handshake of the raw DMA master request on PR-DMA-RAW over the run carries AxPROT `0b001` (privileged, secure, data; `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, `sep-cpu-axprot-table`), and each channel has at least one. An X or Z on the tap fails. Catches a DMA wrapper that drives another AxPROT. Anchor: PR-DMA-RAW. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-AXPROT PASS: master=dma ch=ar\|aw prot={0b001} count=<n> expect=0b001`.
|CHK-DMA-IRQ
|While `rst_ni` is 1, the DMA done and error interrupt bits of `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` resolve to 0 or 1 on every `clk_i` cycle of the run. An X or Z fails the leaf (VCS; Verilator is two-state). Anchor: the interrupt probe. Evidence: no PASS line; a failure prints `CHK-DMA-IRQ FAIL: X/Z on the DMA done or error interrupt out of reset`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Functional coverage*: owner window 233 is open over each `M G` leg. Every check grades after the firmware ends, so the sampler holds each cell of this owner (`dma_ep_pass`: `cp_dma_pair`, `cp_dma_addr_form`, `cp_dma_smc_beat` `user0_len0_r` and `user0_len0_w`, `cp_ext_init_dir_dma`, `cp_out_attr[dma_smu_user_sep]`, the DMA cells of `cp_instance_dir`, `cp_local_alias_twin[dma_ext_top_alias]`, `sil_dma_not_connected`, `cp_filter_csr_write`; `dma_smc_cpu_pass`: the CPU cells of `cp_dma_smc_beat`) and samples it once when the leaf writes the labels, after every check of the run passed. A window close does not drop a held cell; reset does. A `cp_dma_pair` cell counts only a run whose graded outcome is a completed reach: the run ends DONE with no error (a remapped pair also needs its PR-OUT capture), and `sram_to_ext` ends with the adopter error response after a DMA write handshake on PR-EXT. A run that reads or writes a not-connected target, and a run the leaf grades as refused (the source-ID stack write and the locked `START_ADDR` write), count no pair. `sil_dma_not_connected[entropy_pool]` needs the pool level at the DMA read to be 1 or more and below 32. The legal DMA values of `cp_master_ch` (`dma_ar_001`, `dma_aw_001`) are held at each raw DMA beat in the window and sample on the same `dma_ep_pass` write, after CHK-DMA-AXPROT passed; an illegal DMA AxPROT samples at the beat.

*Scope limit*: The leaf does not grade the bus response code at a not-connected target, because the specification states none. It does not grade the number of SEP's ID in the CPU or SMU-path AxUSER, only that it is not `OTHERS` and is one value. It does not grade the beat-by-beat data of a fixed-address destination before the last beat. It does not grade the SRAM to SRAM copy or the copies between SRAM and the CPU TCM (see Test Coverage). The DMA hole cells are graded by `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`. The SMC route and aperture match are graded by `sep_fabric_smc_route_test`, not by this leaf. No alias remap region is programmed in this leaf. The PR-OUT address of the SMU window leg holds only while every alias remap region is invalid. The W data of the remap write legs on PR-OUT is not graded, because the console stores share PR-OUT W and are not told apart by address; the route is graded by address, AxUSER and AxPROT. The pool precondition reads the low 32-bit word of `STATUS` (`entropy_pool.adoc:40-43` refuses only the upper word); the leaf grades the level against the depth only as the precondition `CTRL-DMA-POOL-PRE`.

'''''

[[sep_outbound_contention_rand_test]]
===== 2.34 `sep_outbound_contention_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+sep_outbound_depth=64`, one seed per regression (testlist reseed 4).

*Source Module*: `sep_outbound_contention_rand_test` (stimulus and accounting in `cocotb/seq_lib/sep_outbound_contention_seq.py`)

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip as the CPU-LSU master (`s_axi`) and as the SMN inbound master (`m_axi`, through the inbound filter); the Secure DMA, programmed over the CPU-LSU master; the outbound responder `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv`, which stalls AW, W and AR READY and holds B and R VALID from a seeded stream and returns `{~a, a}` as read data; the PR-OUT, PR-OUT-RSP and PR-INFLT fabric taps (read-only).

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the two LSU AxUSER values (12 bits, `[11:4]` non-zero, `[3:0]` non-zero and distinct) and the System Interface AxUSER, the READY stall percent of AW, W and AR, the response hold percent and its maximum, the single, burst and concurrent transaction counts, the burst length, the number of DMA jobs, the start-offset range, the same-ID AxID and its outstanding count, and the read and write mix. Every phase runs on every seed.

*Objective*: The CPU LSU and the Secure DMA each reach one outbound window, singly and at the same time, under seeded target delays: one request per access, exactly one B per write and its beats per read, every response routed to its initiator, same-ID reads returned in port order, and AxUSER and AxPROT at the port as the specification states. The SMN inbound master has no route to the outbound port (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, Fabric Topology: the peripheral crossbar answers an inbound address at or above `0x4000_0000` with DECERR). Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` ("Fabric Topology", "Transaction Routing", "Filtering and Protection"); `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` (LSU master, AxPROT table); `hw/sys/sep/doc/dma.adoc` (master path).

*Test Coverage*: The LSU, the DMA and the SMN inbound master alone; three-way write, read and mixed phases and the three pairs, each opened with the responder hold up for a bounded lead so the requests of the phase are outstanding at the port together; same-ID reads and writes from the LSU alone and beside the SMN inbound master; attribute and idle accounting at the end.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Print `SEED seed=<S>` and one `DRAW` line per drawn value. Bring up with the LSU splice. Program inbound filter entry 0 to admit the SMN inbound accesses (window, AxUSER source ID, NS).
. Singles. The LSU, the DMA and the SMN inbound master each issue single accesses to their own slot of the outbound page (CHK-LSU-PATH, CHK-DMA-PATH, CHK-SYSIF-NO-OUTBOUND-PATH).
. Bursts. Each master issues bursts of 2 to the drawn maximum beats.
. Concurrent phases. Write, read and mixed phases with all three masters, then each pair, under the drawn READY stalls and response holds (CHK-CONC-ALL, CHK-RESP-ROUTE).
. Same-ID phases. The LSU issues the drawn number of same-ID reads and writes, alone and beside the SMN inbound master; at least two same-ID reads are outstanding at the port at once (CHK-ID-ORDER).
. Attributes and idle. Grade AxUSER and AxPROT of every LSU and DMA request at PR-OUT, the LSU/DMA overlap, and the B and R counts after the idle window.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-LSU-PATH, CHK-DMA-PATH
|The LSU and the DMA alone: one request per access at PR-OUT, AxLEN 0 for a single beat, one B or ARLEN+1 R beats with RLAST on the last, DMA `STATUS` DONE then cleared, and no PR-OUT request outside the slots of the page.
|CHK-SYSIF-NO-OUTBOUND-PATH
|Every single and burst access of the SMN inbound master answers DECERR with one B or its R beats, RLAST on the last, and none reaches PR-OUT. Each one leaves the inbound filter (one PR-INFLT AW per write and one AR per read in the SMN slot), so the filter admitted it and the DECERR is the decode's.
|CHK-CONC-ALL
|Every transaction of every initiator completes in the write, read, mixed and pair phases, and in each phase each READY channel with a drawn stall percent that carries a request at the port stalled at least one beat there.
|CHK-ID-ORDER
|Same-ID reads, two or more outstanding at the port at once, return to the LSU in the PR-OUT-RSP order, with no word of another initiator.
|CHK-B-COUNT, CHK-R-COUNT
|Exactly one B per accepted write, exactly its beats per accepted read, and none after the idle window.
|CHK-RESP-ROUTE
|Every response reaches the initiator that issued it.
|CHK-ATTR-USER
|AxUSER[3:0] of every LSU and DMA request at PR-OUT is known, is not OTHERS (0) and is the same on every request, although the LSU issues two distinct non-zero values and the DMA issues OTHERS.
|CHK-ATTR-PROT
|AxPROT of every LSU and DMA request at PR-OUT: bits 0 and 2 as issued, NS 1 (the inverse of address bit 55).
|CHK-LSU-DMA-OVERLAP
|At least one LSU request and one DMA request are outstanding at the port at once.
|CHK-NONVAC
|The ordered fence of the phases.
|CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS
|Each bounded wait logs its bound and raises on expiry; every one has a measured use within its bound.
|===

*Logged, not graded*:

* `OBS-ID-ORDER`: the completion order of the same-ID writes. A same-ID B carries no tag, and the master VIP pairs each B with its write in ID order before it records the completion, so the recorded order is the issue order by construction and a check on it could not fail.
* `OBS-ATTR-ID-SHARE`: the AxID of LSU and DMA requests outstanding at the port together. No specification sentence states whether they must differ after the outbound ID widening.
* `OBS-ATTR-SRC-ID`: the number of SEP's ID in AxUSER[3:0] at the port, with AxUSER[11:4].
* `INFO-WAIT` and `INFO-DELAYS-MEASURED`: bench bounds and the delays the responder applied.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The leaf does not grade the completion order of same-ID writes, the completion latency of a phase, the outbound ID encoding, or the acceptance point of a request that the filters answer themselves.

'''''

[[sep_outbound_stall_saturation_test]]
===== 2.35 `sep_outbound_stall_saturation_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+sep_outbound_depth=64`, one seed per regression (no reseed). Verilator and VCS. Testlist groups `all`, `cpu_stub`, file `testlists/system.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_outbound_stall_saturation_test` (transaction helpers shared with `cocotb/seq_lib/sep_outbound_contention_seq.py`; responder control in `cocotb/seq_lib/sep_outbound_stall_seq.py`)

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip as the CPU-LSU master (`s_axi`) and as the SMN inbound master (`m_axi`, through the inbound filter); the Secure DMA, programmed over the CPU-LSU master; the outbound responder `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv`, driven through its top ports `smn_outbound_ready_stall_i` and `smn_outbound_resp_hold_i` (READY stall per channel, B and R VALID delay and hold, release); the PR-OUT, PR-OUT-RSP and PR-INFLT fabric taps (read-only). The responder queues `+sep_outbound_depth` write bursts, pending B responses and reads; the leaf requires that depth to be at least the 36 requests of one hold (20 LSU requests and 16 DMA words), so every stop it reaches at the port is the DUT's and not the responder's.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed (DIRECTED with seed-drawn delays): the two LSU AxUSER values and the SMN inbound AxUSER, the maximum one-channel delay (8 to 32 clocks), the READY stall percent of AW, W and AR (20 to 50 each), the response hold percent (3 to 10) and its maximum (4 to 16 clocks), and the write data. Every phase runs on every seed. The leaf prints `SEED seed=<S>` and one `DRAW` line per drawn value.

*Objective*: The CPU LSU and the Secure DMA issue to one outbound window while the responder delays AW, W and AR READY and delays or holds B and R VALID, and is then released: the outbound path stops accepting at a finite count, and every request completes after the release with exactly one B per write and the beats of each read, without deadlock. The SMN inbound master has no route to the outbound port (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, Fabric Topology: the peripheral crossbar answers an inbound address at or above `0x4000_0000` with DECERR). No specification states the outstanding limit of the local crossbar or of the outbound chain, so no count is compared: reaching a stop is a control of the leaf, and the counts are logged. Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` ("Fabric Topology", "Transaction Routing", "Filtering and Protection"); `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` (LSU master, AxPROT table); `hw/sys/sep/doc/dma.adoc` (master path).
